# GoodSentry

Status: design authorized by the user on 2026-10-14. Implementation is in progress; tasks/todo.md records verified increments and remaining acceptance work.

## Objective and acceptance boundary

<a id="close-operacion-001"></a>

Build an independently authored Go error tracking and observability server. Existing applications use their Sentry SDK by changing the DSN to GoodSentry. Deliver one application image containing the HTTP server, UI, background jobs, scheduler and migrations. PostgreSQL is the only required external service. Valkey is optional. Development and verification run in WSL2.

The user approved the capability map and authorized elaborating and executing its specifications, plans and tasks. The requested product name is GoodSentry. The existing workspace directory remains named SentryGo.

<a id="close-operacion-002"></a>

GlitchTip's publicly documented functionality is the minimum target, not merely an ingestion endpoint. Shipping an intermediate increment does not satisfy the complete goal. A capability is complete only when its observable result has been verified. Accepting and dropping an unsupported item is not functional compatibility.

## Clean-room method

<a id="close-operacion-003"></a>

Use public protocol specifications, product documentation, release notes, published API contracts and black-box SDK/CLI traffic. Do not read, copy, translate or adapt Sentry, Relay or GlitchTip server implementation source, tests, assets or database schema. Test SDKs as external clients. Keep provenance links here. Third-party Go libraries may implement general protocols such as PostgreSQL, compression and source maps.

## Capability map and ownership

| Stable id | Responsibility | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| runtime | Environment, PostgreSQL, migrations, HTTP lifecycle, jobs, embedded UI | none |
| workspace | Accounts, sessions, organizations, teams, memberships, projects, DSNs and API tokens | runtime |
| ingestion | Sentry store/envelope wire formats, authentication, compression, CORS, limits | workspace |
| artifacts | Releases, deploys, source maps, debug IDs, native symbols | workspace |
| issues | Error grouping, event context, filtering, triage, assignment, comments, merge/unmerge | ingestion, artifacts |
| telemetry | Transactions, spans, logs, aggregates and correlation | ingestion |
| uptime | HTTP/TCP checks and incoming heartbeats | workspace |
| alerts | Frequency rules, uptime transitions, email and webhook delivery | issues, uptime |
| integrations | Supported management API, OpenAPI, MCP and cold archival | issues, telemetry, uptime, alerts |

Construct the UI alongside each capability. Provider contracts and all design decisions live in this specification. Implementation order and execution evidence live in tasks/plan.md and tasks/todo.md.

## Architecture

<a id="close-operacion-004"></a>

Use one Go module, a small executable in cmd/goodsentry, net/http routing and explicit dependency construction. Embed static HTML, CSS and JavaScript using go:embed. The UI has no external CDN or runtime build service. Use pgx for PostgreSQL, migrations embedded with the binary, and transactions for consistency. Keep the database schema relational for memberships, keys and grouping; retain original structured event data in JSONB.

<a id="close-operacion-005"></a>

Ingest events durably before returning success. A request may normalize synchronously and commit the event and its alert work atomically. Postgres owns jobs and retry state. Acknowledged events must survive restarts. A unique project/event-id constraint prevents SDK retries from incrementing counts twice. Concurrent grouping uses a unique project/fingerprint constraint and database atomic operations. Claim background work with row locking and recover expired leases. Never require Redis for correctness.

<a id="close-operacion-006"></a>

HTTP, workers and scheduler stop on termination signals and close database connections. Startup applies forward-only, versioned migrations under a PostgreSQL advisory lock. Readiness checks the database. Deployment rollback requires a compatible binary or database backup restoration; destructive rollback migrations are not automatic.

<a id="close-operacion-007"></a>

Serve an original, responsive application UI with a compact sidebar, neutral surfaces, a teal accent, readable tables and accessible forms. The user authorized Tailwind/Tailwind UI on 2026-10-14; use Tailwind CSS compiled into the embedded static stylesheet. Build tooling is excluded from the runtime image. Show loading, empty, error and permission states. Preserve filters in URLs. Escape telemetry as text, including stack traces, tags and log bodies. Never render event content as trusted HTML.

## Deployment and configuration

<a id="close-operacion-008"></a>

Default HTTP port is 8000. `docker compose up -d` pulls the verified public application image and starts GoodSentry and PostgreSQL with health checks, persistent database storage and an uploads volume. Explicit source builds use `GOODSENTRY_IMAGE=goodsentry:local docker compose up --build -d`. The application image runs as a non-root user and includes CA roots. No separate web/worker/cron containers are needed. An externally managed PostgreSQL URL also works.

<a id="close-operacion-009"></a>

Canonical variables are DATABASE_URL, SECRET_KEY, GOODSENTRY_DOMAIN, PORT, EMAIL_URL, DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL, VALKEY_URL and GOODSENTRY_ENABLE_* flags. Accept GLITCHTIP_DOMAIN and the supplied GLITCHTIP_ENABLE_LOGS/UPTIME/MCP/DUCKDB names as migration aliases, with explicit GoodSentry variables taking precedence. SERVER_ROLE=all_in_one is supported. Reject unsupported role values rather than silently losing background tasks. ENABLE_OPENAPI and ENABLE_ADMIN control GoodSentry's own API documentation and administration surface, not Django functionality. GOODSENTRY_ENABLE_ORGANIZATION_CREATION defaults false and accepts ENABLE_ORGANIZATION_CREATION as its GlitchTip-compatible alias, with the GoodSentry name taking precedence.

<a id="close-operacion-010"></a>

GOODSENTRY_AUTH defaults to disabled for frictionless local startup. If SECRET_KEY is unset in this mode, generate 32 cryptographically random bytes and persist their lowercase hexadecimal encoding as UPLOAD_DIR/.goodsentry-secret with owner-only permissions; reuse it across restarts. GOODSENTRY_AUTH=required requires an explicitly configured SECRET_KEY of at least 32 characters. Multi-instance deployments must configure the same SECRET_KEY for every process. In disabled mode, startup idempotently ensures one owner at GOODSENTRY_DEFAULT_EMAIL, one GOODSENTRY_DEFAULT_ORGANIZATION and its default team under an advisory lock; restart preserves the existing user, password and rows. Every session-auth request resolves that owner without a cookie, /api/v1/setup reports required:false and /api/v1/me returns the owner plus a stable CSRF token. POST /api/v1/setup and /api/v1/login reject attempts to enter authenticated mode while disabled. Mutations still require configured-origin/same-origin checks and the CSRF header. GOODSENTRY_DEFAULT_NAME supplies the new owner name. An optional GOODSENTRY_DEFAULT_PASSWORD (12..72 bytes) is bcrypt-hashed only on first creation; when absent, an unguessable discarded password is stored. Open mode is single-identity: OAuth/MCP use the implicit owner directly, and only invitations addressed to GOODSENTRY_DEFAULT_EMAIL can attach another organization to that owner. The owner must use the password configured at first creation to accept through /api/v1/invitations/accept; invitations for other addresses are rejected instead of creating accounts whose sessions open mode ignores. Use required mode for multi-user invitation acceptance. This single-user mode is for local/trusted networks and does not grant public multi-user security. Set GOODSENTRY_AUTH=required and configure a persistent SECRET_KEY before exposing the service; required mode restores the complete existing setup/login/session/OIDC/invitation behavior and runs no implicit owner provisioning.

<a id="close-operacion-011"></a>

An empty VALKEY_URL selects PostgreSQL-only operation. Optional features must report their actual availability. Never accept a true feature flag while leaving its behavior unimplemented. EMAIL_URL supports consolemail:// and SMTP, with credentials excluded from logs. Persist uploads under a configurable directory. Reject placeholder production secrets. Document local development defaults separately.

<a id="close-operacion-012"></a>

Memory target: measure application RSS under an explicit repeatable workload and publish results. The user's 256-512 MB example is an operational target, not a measured claim. Do not claim this target until a representative load test demonstrates it.

## Runtime and workspace contracts

<a id="close-identidad-001"></a>

Provide /healthz for process health and /readyz for database readiness. Setup creates the first account and organization without a shipped shared password. Accounts can log in/out and recover passwords when email is configured. Use a memory-hard or established adaptive password hash. Sessions are opaque random secrets, stored hashed, expire, and use HttpOnly/SameSite cookies with Secure under HTTPS. Browser mutations require same-origin/CSRF protection. API Bearer tokens are hashed, revocable and scoped to authorized organizations/projects. SDK ingestion keys identify a project and do not grant management access.

<a id="close-identidad-002"></a>

Organizations contain memberships and teams. Projects belong to an organization and may be assigned teams. The same identity can access multiple organizations only through membership. Enforce scope at every read and mutation. Owners/admins manage memberships, projects, credentials and alerts; members can perform the approved issue workflow. Do not expose a bootstrap endpoint after the first owner has been created. Record account, organization and project identifiers as opaque strings at the HTTP boundary.

<a id="close-identidad-003"></a>

The automation-credential prerequisite belongs to workspace, before compatible release mutations in artifacts. Owners/admins create, list and revoke organization-scoped API tokens through session-only native `GET/POST /api/v1/organizations/{org}/api-tokens` and `DELETE /api/v1/organizations/{org}/api-tokens/{id}`. Keep the existing cookie and CSRF controls for these mutations; possession of a bearer token never permits issuing another credential. A token is `gs_` followed by 32 cryptographically random bytes encoded as 64 lowercase hex digits. Return it once on successful creation, never through list/read, URLs or logs. Store only its HMAC-SHA256 under SECRET_KEY, as for sessions, plus an identifying prefix of the first11 characters. Credential metadata contains string `id`, `name`, `prefix`, `scopes`, `allProjects`, `projects:[{id,slug,name}]`, string `createdBy`, UTC `dateCreated`, `expiresAt` and nullable `revokedAt`. List active, expired and revoked metadata newest-first by date/id,25 rows per canonical page1..1000000, with `{data,page,pageSize:25,hasMore}`. Revoke returns204 and is idempotent for an already revoked token in the same organization; foreign/absent identifiers return404. Names contain1..100 Unicode characters and no control characters. Creation accepts `name`, optional `scopes` (default `org:read` and `project:read`), optional `projectIds` (canonical positive int64 strings), and optional `expiresInDays` (default90, range1..366). Explicit empty/duplicate/unknown scopes are invalid. Implement those two read scopes and project:releases with the release consumers specified below. Reject unknown fields and malformed inputs at the HTTP boundary. These defaults and bounds are the versioned native credential contract, not measured operational capacity.

<a id="close-identidad-004"></a>

An omitted or empty project list explicitly grants all current and future organization projects. A nonempty list grants only the selected existing projects; reject any absent/foreign project atomically with404. Explicit null scopes, projectIds or expiresInDays are invalid400; absent values use the defaults above. A supplied empty/malformed native page is invalid400. Credential names require a nonwhitespace character. Persist the all-project choice independently so a restricted credential with no remaining projects can never become unrestricted. Token lookup requires an unrevoked, unexpired record and the creator's current membership in that organization; deleting membership removes their credentials permanently, including after re-enrollment. A role downgrade does not remove ordinary member reads, but credential management still requires current owner/admin status. Recheck membership and project scope in the data provider. Revocation/expiry prevent subsequent requests; they do not abort a request already authorized. Creation/list responses and application logs must not disclose stored hashes or the original token. Duplicate credential names are allowed and IDs distinguish them.

<a id="close-identidad-005"></a>

Consume these credentials through Sentry-compatible `GET /api/0/organizations/{org}/projects/` (requires `org:read`) and `GET /api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/` (requires `project:read`), followed by the explicitly specified release consumers below. Browser sessions may read the same endpoints. Unregistered compatibility paths return404; match project paths exactly so descendants cannot be mistaken for a project response. Only explicitly registered compatibility routes accept bearer authentication; native session management and SDK ingestion keep their existing authentication. A supplied Authorization header is authoritative: require one Bearer credential, never fall back to a cookie after malformed/invalid bearer input. Return401 for absent/invalid/expired/revoked credentials,403 for a missing implemented scope, and404 for another organization or an out-of-scope project. Organization/project path values accept an exact slug or canonical positive numeric ID. A bearer grant is intersected with current membership and selected projects. Return actual project `id`, `name`, `slug`, `platform`, `dateCreated`; exclude SDK keys and DSNs. List supports case-insensitive literal name/slug substring `query` (up to200 Unicode characters), canonical `limit`/`per_page` aliases1..100 (default100; if both are present they must agree), and an opaque cursor. Each page-size alias may appear only once. Preserve the supplied alias and size in pagination links; when neither is supplied, emit `per_page`. Accept an absent/empty cursor as the first page. Define our cursor as canonical base64url without padding of `offset:<canonical nonnegative decimal>`, with offset at most100000000; reject malformed/noncanonical cursors. Order by numeric project ID ascending and fetch one extra row. Emit previous/next HTTP Link entries with `rel`, `results` and `cursor`, using the configured public domain/path prefix and preserving query/size. Offsets describe the current authorized result; separate requests may observe intervening changes. Detail of an accessible absent project returns404; an authorized empty list returns200[]. These project reads are the first management routes, not a claim of complete management or release API parity.

<a id="close-identidad-006"></a>

Add an owner/admin-only API tokens view to the existing original Tailwind/daisyUI navigation. Show label, prefix, creator ID, scope, selected/all-current-and-future projects, expiry and revoked/expired/active status; support25-row pages. The create form defaults to the two implemented read scopes and offers an unchecked project:releases write scope, project selection and lifetime, reports validation/server errors without losing inputs, and reveals the returned token with copy and dismiss controls. Never persist the secret in location/history/localStorage/sessionStorage; dismissing or leaving the view removes it. Successful revocation refreshes the same page and removes the token's usability. Members must have neither credential navigation nor controls; direct navigation displays permission failure. Organization/sidebar navigation resets token-specific state. Verify creation/revocation/one-time display, real permissions, pagination, input retention, safe text and320/768/1440 rendering in Chromium. Real PostgreSQL owns token hashing, organization/project boundaries, all-versus-restricted semantics, expiry/revocation, membership changes and reopen persistence. HTTP owns actual credential issuance and compatible consumption, scope/parameter/header validation, cookie/CSRF separation and prefixed Link navigation. The pinned official sentry-cli3.8.0 must list only authorized projects using an actually issued credential against the built image, then fail after revocation. The release/write scope ships only with its implemented consumers and acceptance; remaining workspace and artifacts requirements stay open.

<a id="close-identidad-007"></a>

Malformed percent encoding or other invalid URL query syntax returns400 rather than becoming an omitted/default parameter. Every emitted pagination cursor remains within the accepted offset range, including disabled links. At the maximum boundary, cap the disabled next cursor and report no next page when its normal offset would exceed that boundary.

### Account credential generations and authenticated password changes

<a id="close-identidad-008"></a>

This workspace increment supplies the shared revocation contract needed by password recovery. It also lets any signed-in owner, admin or member change their own password without an email service. Email recovery, invitations, team and membership management remain required capabilities with separate delivery and authorization contracts. This section defines GoodSentry's native behavior independently; no external account API equivalence is claimed.

<a id="closure-source-table-1"></a>

| Workspace child | Consumer and acceptance boundary | Dependency |
|---|---|---|
| credential-generation | Login/bootstrap session issuance and native API-token issuance retain their original authentication generation; password changes atomically invalidate all of that user's credentials | Existing accounts, sessions and scoped API tokens |
| account-password | A signed-in user changes their own password through API and original Account UI, then signs in again | credential-generation |
| account-mail | Purpose-specific account messages, secret handling and actual SMTP delivery/retry behavior | Existing mail transport; its alert payload and project-required outbox are insufficient |
| account-recovery | Email request and single-use redemption with enumeration, expiry, replay and generation rules | credential-generation, account-mail |
| membership-invitations | Owner/admin membership and team management, invitation issuance and acceptance for new/existing identities | account-mail; complete role/last-owner and invitation contracts still required |

<a id="close-identidad-014"></a>

Additive migration035 after034 adds `users.auth_generation bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK (auth_generation > 0)`. Preserve every existing password hash, user identifier, session, token, grant, membership and migration receipt. Do not rewrite credentials during the upgrade. Carry `AuthGeneration` in the internal `User` returned by bootstrap, email lookup and session admission; exclude it from JSON just like `PasswordHash`, including nested comment/activity users. A zero/missing generation never authorizes credential issuance. Existing session and token rows need no generation column under the locking contract below.

<a id="close-identidad-015"></a>

Both production issuers must require the generation observed by their caller. Login retains the generation from the same user lookup whose password hash passed bcrypt; bootstrap retains the inserted generation. Native API-token creation retains the generation from the session middleware's original admission. Never refresh that value after a mismatch or retry using the previous password/session proof. Require it in the store interfaces; leave no overload/default that issues credentials using user ID alone. All future credential issuers and password-reset authorizations must obey this rule too.

<a id="close-identidad-016"></a>

Session and API-token issuance use an explicit `pgx.ReadCommitted` transaction. Lock the user's row `FOR SHARE`, compare its generation with the observed value, and retain the lock through credential commit. API-token creation then keeps its current membership SHARE and sorted selected-project SHARE locks and authorization checks. Credential issuance orders its locks as user, membership, selected projects, credential. This is a requirement for these issuers, not a claim that every existing issue workflow takes user locks first. A missing or changed user fails with a dedicated internal credentials-changed error and no inserted credential. Preserve existing project-scope, role, expiry, one-time-secret and CSRF behavior.

<a id="close-identidad-017"></a>

Password change uses explicit `pgx.ReadCommitted` regardless of the deployment's default isolation. Lock the user's row `FOR UPDATE` before any password or credential mutation; compare the original authentication generation. In that same transaction replace the password hash, increment the generation once, delete all sessions belonging to the user, and revoke all their unrevoked personal API tokens across every organization. Use `api_tokens.user_id`, without an organization filter; expired unrevoked tokens are included. Preserve existing `revoked_at` values and all token/grant history. Capture one database timestamp after acquiring the user lock for the newly revoked rows. Other users' credentials, memberships, project keys/DSNs and retained telemetry remain unchanged. An exhausted bigint generation or a confirmed transaction abort changes none of the state; never wrap or reset the counter. If the connection fails during COMMIT, the whole operation may have committed even though the client receives an error. A deferred rollback cannot remove that uncertainty. Do not automatically retry or claim the password stayed unchanged; verify the result through a new sign-in.

<a id="close-identidad-018"></a>

When issuance acquires its user SHARE lock first, password change waits, then its subsequent credential statements see and invalidate that committed credential. When password change wins first, waiting issuance observes the new generation and fails. Two changes with the same original generation have one winner. Ordinary requests admitted before revocation keep the existing in-flight-request semantics; they cannot use that admission to issue a fresh credential after the generation changes. Later authentication rejects deleted sessions and revoked bearer tokens. A login with the new password reads the new generation and can issue a usable session.

<a id="close-identidad-019"></a>

The need for a fresh statement snapshot follows PostgreSQL's [Read Committed and Repeatable Read contracts](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/transaction-iso.html). Repeatable Read can retain a snapshot taken while an issuer holds a lock and subsequently miss the issuer's committed credential. User SHARE must conflict with the exclusive password-change lock, as specified by PostgreSQL's [row-lock modes](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/explicit-locking.html#LOCKING-ROWS). This reasoning requires real interleaving tests before acceptance; documentation review is not a concurrency proof.

<a id="close-identidad-020"></a>

Register prefix-aware `POST /api/v1/account/password` through the existing native session/CSRF/same-origin middleware. Bearer or DSN credentials alone never authorize this route. Preserve native cookie handling when an Authorization header is also supplied; only compatible routes apply bearer precedence. The body is one JSON object with required string `currentPassword` and `newPassword`; reject unknown fields, invalid types, trailing JSON and bodies beyond the existing1MiB native limit. The current password is1..72 bytes and the new password12..72 UTF-8 bytes. Do not trim either password or substitute character counts for byte bounds. Reusing the same password is permitted and still performs the full revocation operation.

<a id="close-identidad-021"></a>

Load the user's current password hash and generation by the admitted user ID. Reject a generation mismatch with401 before treating that newer hash as authorization for the old session. Compare the current password with bcrypt, then compute the replacement bcrypt hash outside the write transaction, using the existing adaptive cost. Pass the original admitted generation into the store mutation. Malformed input or an incorrect current password returns400 and changes no account or credential state. A stale/removed identity returns401 and issues no cookie. Invalid/missing session and CSRF/origin failures retain401/403. Storage failure returns503 with the existing generic response and no hash, password or generation disclosure. Login whose generation loses a race returns its existing generic401 credentials failure; native token creation returns401 for that stale session. Do not translate this condition into a misleading resource-conflict response.

<a id="close-identidad-022"></a>

Return204 only after the password transaction commits. Expire the current session cookie with the existing prefix, HttpOnly, SameSite and HTTPS Secure attributes, and require a new sign-in; do not automatically issue a replacement credential. Passwords, hashes and authentication generations never enter response bodies, URLs, telemetry or logs. If the client loses the response after commit, it can sign in with the new password. Do not promise an HTTP retry using the revoked session will succeed.

<a id="close-identidad-023"></a>

Expose an Account link beside the signed-in identity and sign-out action, using the existing `view=account` navigation. It is available to every authenticated role and to an identity with no current organization; render it before organization/project loading. Show the account's name/email as text and a labeled current/new/confirm password form with `autocomplete=current-password` and `new-password`. Explain before submission that a successful change signs out every session and revokes personal API tokens in every organization, while project SDK keys keep working. Confirmation is a browser check; do not add it to the API contract. Disable duplicate submission while pending. Show safe, accessible validation/server feedback without persisting any password beyond the mounted form. Clear fields when leaving the view or receiving an authentication failure; never retain them in shared state, history or browser storage. On success clear authenticated application state and show the sign-in form with a plain success message. A400 leaves the form available without claiming completion. A503 or failed network response leaves it available but explains that the result could not be confirmed and offers a new sign-in to verify it; do not resubmit automatically. The usual keyboard, focus, responsive and safe-text rules apply.

<a id="close-identidad-024"></a>

Real PostgreSQL owns035 upgrade preservation/reopen, explicit-isolation behavior, generation checks, all-user-session removal, cross-organization token revocation, unchanged already-revoked timestamps and unrelated-user/project-key preservation. Extend existing session/token fixtures and use real transactions to observe blocking before release, with bounded cancellation and existing database observation helpers. Cover both orderings for session and token issuance, two concurrent changes, stale login proof, stale admitted session, rollback after revocation and deferred commit failure. Run the critical issuer-first/change-waiting case with a pool whose default is Repeatable Read; the provider must explicitly override it. Avoid sleeps as synchronization and production-only test hooks. Verify a rejected stale issuer has no credential row, and that a newly authenticated generation can issue and consume both credential types.

<a id="close-identidad-025"></a>

Real HTTP owns actual current-password verification, byte boundaries including multibyte input, strict body handling, role-independent account access, CSRF/origin/bearer separation, prefixed cookie expiry, old/new login, both users' independent sessions, and issued bearer-token rejection after change. Browser acceptance covers all authenticated roles, no-organization access, validation/pending/error/success flows, secret lifetime, keyboard focus and320/768/1024/1440 rendering. A new disposable image/database copy must preserve earlier rows and hashes through035, perform the change through the public API/UI, prove unaffected SDK ingestion, verify revocation independently and preserve the resulting state through app-only restart. Full PostgreSQL integration/race/vet and matching-source publication remain required before closing this child. Keep accepted databases and the public5dd302f image untouched.

<a id="close-identidad-026"></a>

Deployment must stop every older app process that can issue unguarded credentials before enabling password mutation; recreate the single documented application service with the new image. Mixed old/new replicas cannot uphold this contract and are not a supported rolling upgrade for this increment. Keep the prior image and a separately restored backup for rollback; do not run an older issuer against a database after password changes.

### Account email and single-use password recovery

<a id="close-identidad-027"></a>

Account mail is purpose-specific and independent of project alert mail. Reserve additive migration036 after035 for `account_password_resets`: a text delivery ID, owning user, recipient, unique64-hex token hash, AES-GCM token ciphertext, issuing authentication generation, expiry, nullable consumption instant, delivery state (`pending`, `processing`, `sent`, `failed`, `consumed`, `canceled`), attempt count, next-attempt instant, creation/update/send instants, bounded reason, and fenced lease token/expiry under the same processing-state check used by alerts. Index due pending work and expired leases without rewriting existing rows.

<a id="close-identidad-028"></a>

`register` and `login` screens expose a password-reset request form. Request normalization lowercases and trims one email of at most254 bytes. Malformed bodies, unknown fields, invalid email syntax, unknown accounts and recently requested accounts return the same202 with no response data; known nonrate-limited accounts enqueue a reset in one Read Committed transaction. Require the generation from `UserByEmail`; lock the user FOR SHARE, reject a changed/missing generation, replace earlier unconsumed resets, and enforce a60-second per-user request floor. Do not disclose account existence through explicit status, response bodies, diagnostics or mail transport errors; this is not a constant-time timing promise. Every valid request returns202 only after its enqueue transaction commits; storage failure returns503. A recently requested account leaves existing pending mail/token unchanged. Creation accepts only the authenticated email; no caller-supplied destination.

<a id="close-identidad-029"></a>

A reset token is 32 random bytes as64 lowercase hex digits. Store only its HMAC-SHA256 under SECRET_KEY. Encrypt the raw token with AES-256-GCM derived from SECRET_KEY and bind the token hash as AAD; use a fresh random nonce and reject malformed, truncated, swapped or unauthenticated ciphertext. The encrypted token exists only in the dedicated outbox and is never returned, logged, shown in history or placed in application telemetry. A reset request is usable only after its delivery state is `sent`, its generation equals the current user generation, it is unconsumed and the database clock is before expiry. Links expire30 minutes after creation.

<a id="close-identidad-030"></a>

The one application worker sends a plain-text MIME message through the existing SMTP/consolemail transport with a stable delivery Message-ID and a link to the configured public domain/path prefix. The body contains the recipient's display name, exact expiry instant, single-use warning and instructions to ignore an unsolicited request. Use a fixed account-reset subject and no project payload/rules. Bound the generated URL and MIME body; reject unsafe recipient/header/URL input as permanent without SMTP. SMTP4xx, network/timeout/cancellation retry under the shared completion/fencing policy; SMTP5xx is permanent. Retry delay is the existing bounded alert backoff unless a future explicit provider minimum is implemented. Expired work becomes failed without sending. A new request can make an old emailed link unusable; delivery and reset are at-least-once, never claimed exactly-once.

<a id="close-identidad-031"></a>

`POST /api/v1/account/password/reset/request` is public but requires same-origin policy and strict JSON `{email}`. `POST /api/v1/account/password/reset` accepts strict JSON `{token,newPassword}` with a64-character lowercase-hex token and the existing12..72 valid UTF-8 byte password bound. Both reject a bearer credential alone and never use a different user's session. Reset uses one explicit Read Committed transaction: read token owner without lock, lock the current user first FOR UPDATE, then lock and revalidate the reset row so a concurrent password mutation cannot authorize stale proof. Require current generation, sent/unconsumed/unexpired state and then replace the password, increment generation once, delete every session, revoke every unrevoked personal API token across organizations, mark the reset consumed and invalidate every other unconsumed reset for that user. A stale/expired/consumed/foreign/malformed token returns400 without writes; success returns204 and expires the current session cookie. The normal authenticated password change also invalidates every unconsumed reset.

<a id="close-identidad-032"></a>

The reset form consumes a `resetToken` query parameter without persisting it into application state or storage. It asks for new/confirm passwords, sends only the new password and token, prevents duplicate submissions, clears the URL token on success, and shows a generic sign-in result. Request success shows the same enumeration-safe message for every syntactically valid email. Invalid/expired links explain how to request another message. Recoverable storage/mail errors do not claim delivery. Keep keyboard focus, accessible feedback, safe text,320/768/1024/1440 layout and no password retention after leaving.

<a id="close-identidad-033"></a>

Pure checks own token parsing, AES-GCM integrity/AAD, generation/size validation and MIME headers/body/link/expiry with consolemail. Real PostgreSQL owns036 preservation/reopen, request floor, generation/email replacement, due/expired/fenced sends, retry failure states, token single use, cross-organization revocation, unrelated users and rollback. Real HTTP owns same-origin/strict bodies, enumeration-safe request status, bearer rejection, actual reset and session/cookie behavior. A real local SMTP sink plus responsive Chromium now proves delivery, link redemption, old-password rejection, new-password login, replay rejection and the60-second request floor; copied-image/restart, exact source/publication and CI remain required. Keep every accepted runtime and earlier migration untouched.

### Two-factor authentication

<a id="close-identidad-034"></a>

Additive migration `053_user_totp.sql` adds nullable `users.totp_secret` (AES-256-GCM ciphertext), `users.totp_confirmed_at`, `users.totp_last_step` and a `user_recovery_codes` table keyed by user with one-way hashes, `created_at` and nullable `used_at`. The encryption key is derived from SECRET_KEY with a fixed label; the plaintext secret exists only in the enrollment response and in memory while a code is checked. TOTP is RFC6238 over HMAC-SHA1 with six digits and a30-second step. Verification accepts the current step and one step either side, compares in constant time and refuses any step at or below the recorded last accepted step, so an observed code cannot be replayed.

<a id="close-identidad-035"></a>

POST `/api/v1/login` keeps its strict body and identical `Invalid email or password` response. When the matched account has a confirmed secret it instead creates a five-minute single-use challenge and returns200 with `twoFactorRequired:true`, the challenge token, `methods:["totp","recovery"]` and no session cookie. POST `/api/v1/login/two-factor` accepts strict `{challengeToken,code}`, is public but same-origin, and completes exactly one challenge through a valid TOTP step or one unused recovery code. Recovery codes are ten single-use values shown once; their hashes are compared in constant time and the row is marked used inside the same transaction that creates the session. Each wrong code increments a per-challenge counter; the fifth failure, an expired or consumed challenge, a changed credential generation or an unknown token invalidates the challenge and returns401 without a session. Completion reuses the existing session, CSRF and credential-generation contract.

<a id="close-identidad-036"></a>

GET `/api/v1/me/two-factor` reports `enabled`, `pending` and `recoveryCodesRemaining` for the current account. POST `/api/v1/me/two-factor` starts enrollment and returns the base32 secret and its `otpauth://totp/` URI once. POST `/api/v1/me/two-factor/confirm` requires a valid current code, confirms the secret and returns the recovery codes exactly once. DELETE `/api/v1/me/two-factor` requires the current password and clears the secret, confirmation, step and unused codes. POST `/api/v1/me/two-factor/recovery-codes` requires the current password and replaces every outstanding code. Mutations use the existing same-origin and CSRF admission and return private no-store JSON.

<a id="close-identidad-037"></a>

The Account view exposes the two-factor state, the manual-entry secret and `otpauth` URI with copy actions, a confirmation field, one-time recovery-code display, password-confirmed disable and code regeneration. The sign-in view asks for the challenge code after a password when the account requires it, accepts a recovery code in the same field and returns to the password form on failure or expiry. Password, recovery, invitation, OIDC and open-mode flows are unchanged, and open mode still bypasses login.

<a id="close-identidad-038"></a>

Additive migration `073_organization_require_2fa.sql` adds `organizations.require_2fa` defaulting false without rewriting existing rows. The compatible organization detail/update accepts the public `require2FA` boolean; enabling it requires the acting owner/admin to have a confirmed TOTP enrollment. The native Organization view exposes the same setting and explains that the administrator must enroll first.

<a id="close-identidad-039"></a>

When any current organization requires two-factor authentication and the account has not confirmed TOTP, session and Bearer access is denied except for `GET /api/v1/me`, logout, and the two-factor enrollment/confirmation endpoints. `/api/v1/me` reports `twoFactorRequired` and `twoFactorEnabled`; the browser shows a focused enrollment gate and returns to the dashboard after confirmation. Existing login, recovery, OIDC, social, invitation and open-mode identity flows still establish their normal identity first, then obey this gate. Disabling the account second factor is rejected while an organization requires it. This strict global gate protects project, issue, log, release, MCP and compatible API data without exposing which organization triggered it.

### Organization people and teams

<a id="close-identidad-040"></a>

Organization members and project teams are managed through the native authenticated API and a People view available to current owners, managers and admins. Members may read organization membership and teams through the API; owners, managers and admins may mutate them. All mutations retain session/CSRF/same-origin controls. IDs are canonical positive numeric strings. Users from another organization and absent teams/members return404; malformed IDs, roles and names return400.

<a id="close-identidad-041"></a>

`GET /api/v1/organizations/{org}/members` returns `{data:[{id,name,email,role}]}` ordered case-insensitively by name then numeric ID. Owners and managers may set `manager`, `admin` or `member`; owners may also set `owner`. Admins may set `admin` or `member`, cannot assign or mutate `manager`, and cannot promote to or mutate an existing owner. Only an owner may assign `owner`, promote/demote an existing owner, or transfer ownership. Only an owner or manager may assign `manager`; an admin cannot change a manager's role. The organization row is locked before membership changes so concurrent operations serialize. The last current owner cannot be demoted or removed and returns409. Removing a member deletes that organization membership, team memberships in its teams and membership-owned tokens/assignments through existing foreign keys; it does not delete the user, sessions for other organizations or historical comments/activity.

<a id="close-identidad-042"></a>

`GET/POST /api/v1/organizations/{org}/teams` lists or creates teams; `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/v1/organizations/{org}/teams/{team}` reads, renames or deletes one. Team names trim to1..120 Unicode characters without controls; slugs are generated once and immutable. Team records expose numeric string ID, slug, name, current member count and project count. Creation conflict is409. The bootstrap `default` team has slug immutable and cannot be deleted; its display name may change. Deleting another team removes its memberships and project-team association. For affected projects, the legacy `projects.team_id` mirror selects the smallest remaining associated team ID, or stays null when none remain; project data and other associations are preserved. Team details expose ordered current organization memberships only.

<a id="close-identidad-043"></a>

`POST/DELETE /api/v1/organizations/{org}/teams/{team}/members/{member}` adds/removes an existing member of the same organization. Add is idempotent; remove is idempotent for an absent assignment. Both reject foreign users/teams. Owners and managers may change any assignment in their organization. Admins may change assignments only on teams where they are already members. Team membership is display/grouping/alert metadata and does not widen project or event access. Every People mutation updates the view in place with recoverable validation errors; removal/deletion requires an explicit confirmation communicating token, assignment, team and project-association effects.

<a id="close-identidad-044"></a>

Pure/API checks own canonical IDs and strict bodies. Real PostgreSQL owns owner/manager/admin permissions, last-owner races, serialized role/removal updates, membership cascades, team uniqueness, default-team protection, project unassignment and reopen persistence. Real HTTP owns session/CSRF separation and member/owner status codes. Browser acceptance covers role/team creation, add/remove, last-owner/conflict errors, confirmations, keyboard focus and responsive layouts. Copy-image/restart, full integration/race/vet and public CI remain required. Invitations and acceptance into a new account remain a separate required child; this management surface cannot create credentials or add an arbitrary email.

### Organization invitations

<a id="close-identidad-045"></a>

Additive migration037 after036 stores organization email invitations: delivery ID, organization, normalized email, role, inviter, unique token hash, encrypted token, creation/expiry/acceptance instants, delivery state/attempts/next attempt, bounded reason and the same fenced lease fields as account mail. Permit at most one active `pending`, `processing` or `sent` invitation for the same organization and case-insensitive email through a partial unique index. Seven-day expiry follows the database clock. Persist no raw token. The delivery worker uses a generic greeting when the invitee has no display name; a valid email address up to 254 bytes must not be constrained by the 120-character display-name limit.

<a id="close-identidad-046"></a>

Owners may invite `owner`, `manager`, `admin` or `member`; managers may invite `manager`, `admin` or `member`; admins may invite `admin` or `member`. Only owners may invite owners, and only owners or managers may invite managers. The recipient must not already have an organization membership. Creation reuses session/CSRF, organization-row serialization, the accountmail AES-GCM/token-hash contract and SMTP/consolemail retry worker. Owners, managers and admins list pending invitations and may cancel one; cancellation clears any lease and makes its emailed link unusable. Delivery retry success marks the invitation `sent`; permanent/expired delivery failure leaves it unusable and visible. Invitation acceptance validates state, expiry and current organization existence in one transaction, locks the organization and invitation, creates the user only when absent, verifies an existing user's email and credential generation, creates exactly one membership, and consumes the invitation. An existing account accepts with its password; a new account supplies name and a valid12..72-byte password. Success issues a normal session. Replay, cancellation, foreign token, expiry, mismatched email and membership conflict fail without writes. Invitations never use project-alert recipients.

<a id="close-identidad-047"></a>

Public request/accept routes are same-origin, contain no bearer authentication and require the secret only in the JSON body. `POST /api/v1/invitations/inspect` returns the invited email, organization display identity, role and whether an account already exists after validating a sent, unexpired token. `POST /api/v1/invitations/accept` accepts `{token,password}` for an existing account or `{token,name,password}` for a new one, rechecks the credential/generation and returns a normal authenticated session. The UI consumes `inviteToken` from the query, never stores it in application state or storage, reveals only the invited email/workspace/role, differentiates existing/new account fields, prevents duplicate submission and clears the token from the URL after success. Existing users retain all prior organizations and sessions; new users are attached only to the invited organization.

### Compatible invitation acceptance

<a id="close-identidad-048"></a>

`GET/POST /api/0/accept/{invitation}/{token}/` maps GlitchTip's invitation-acceptance route onto the same single-use invitation rows. The path identity is GoodSentry's stable 32-hex invitation ID; it is not a fabricated numeric OrganizationUser row. GET is public and validates a current `sent`, unexpired invitation by both identity and token hash. It returns `acceptInvite:false` and `orgUser` with the invitation ID, invited email, role/roleName, creation instant, `pending:true`, `user:null`, empty teams and the nested organization. POST requires a session or compatible Bearer identity and a same-origin request, accepts only `{acceptInvite:boolean}`, and ignores unknown JSON fields. A false value returns the same pending view without writes. A true value requires the authenticated account's primary or verified secondary email to match the invitation, creates exactly one membership, consumes the invitation and returns `acceptInvite:true` with the accepted organization user, its real member ID and nested organization. Mismatched identity/token pairs, wrong accounts, replay, cancellation or expiry answer 403; an existing membership answers 409. The route never creates an account or a session; new-account acceptance remains `POST /api/v1/invitations/accept`.

<a id="close-identidad-049"></a>

Acceptance owners are pure token/MIME tests, real PostgreSQL migration/partial uniqueness/outbox/retry/replay/membership/password generation tests, actual HTTP admission, and a controlled SMTP sink plus copied image/restart and browser320/768/1024/1440 checks. Invitations are not accepted until these run; no email is treated as a user until redeemed.

### OpenID Connect sign-in

<a id="close-identidad-050"></a>

OIDC is disabled unless `GOODSENTRY_OIDC_ISSUER`, `GOODSENTRY_OIDC_CLIENT_ID` and `GOODSENTRY_OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET` are configured together. Issuers require HTTPS except for loopback development, discovery is bounded to ten seconds, and configured scopes must include `openid`. The callback is the configured public domain plus `/auth/oidc/callback`. The secret remains server-side.

<a id="close-identidad-051"></a>

`GET /auth/oidc/start` creates random state, nonce and PKCE verifier values, stores an HMAC-authenticated HttpOnly SameSite Lax cookie for at most ten minutes, and redirects to the discovered authorization endpoint with S256 PKCE and nonce. `GET /auth/oidc/callback` receives the authorization code in its one-time callback query, requires the exact state cookie, exchanges the code with the verifier, verifies the ID token signature, issuer, audience, expiry and nonce, and accepts only a nonempty subject plus `email_verified:true` with a valid normalized email. It immediately redirects to a clean application URL with `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. Provider secrets, access tokens, raw ID tokens and the authorization code after exchange must not be persisted, copied into later URLs, logs or telemetry.

<a id="close-identidad-052"></a>

Additive045 stores provider, subject, normalized email, user and login instants with unique `(provider,subject)` and `(user_id,provider)` constraints. A known subject logs in directly. Otherwise a verified email links to the existing normalized account; a second subject for the same provider returns conflict. With `GOODSENTRY_OIDC_AUTO_CREATE=true`, an unknown email creates an OIDC-only user whose stored password value is intentionally unusable until account recovery sets a real password. Failed or stale flows delete the flow cookie and redirect to the sign-in page with a generic error; no partially linked user is committed.

<a id="close-identidad-053"></a>

`GET /api/v1/auth/config` exposes only whether OIDC is enabled and its display name. The sign-in screen renders a provider button using that value. Tests use a local signed-JWT OIDC provider and real PostgreSQL to cover discovery, PKCE, nonce/state, verified-email linking, repeat login, configuration while disabled, and tampered callbacks. A remote provider receipt remains required before claiming a particular vendor is deployment-verified.

### Organization single sign-on applications

<a id="close-identidad-054"></a>

Additive migration `055_social_apps.sql` stores organization-managed OpenID Connect and OAuth2 applications: organization, provider `openid_connect`, `google`, `microsoft`, `github`, `gitlab`, `gitea` or `digitalocean`, brand `openid_connect`, `google`, `microsoft`, `github`, `gitlab`, `gitea` or `digitalocean`, unique `provider_id`, name, client ID, sealed client secret and issuer URL. The client secret is sealed with AES-256-GCM under a key and associated data derived from SECRET_KEY and the fixed purpose `GoodSentry social apps v1`; it is returned only to the sign-in flow and never to the API. Deleting an organization removes its applications through the foreign key.

<a id="close-identidad-055"></a>

GET, POST `/api/0/organizations/{org}/social-apps/` and GET, PUT, DELETE `.../social-apps/{id}/` serve the published shape: integer `id`, `provider`, `brand`, `providerID`, `name`, `clientID`, `serverUrl`, `callbackUrl` and `loginUrl`. Reads use session or Bearer `org:read`; mutations use owner/admin session or `org:write` Bearer authority. Names are1..40 characters, client IDs and secrets1..191, the issuer must be an https URL of at most500 characters, OpenID Connect requires an issuer and the Google preset defaults to `https://accounts.google.com`. `provider` cannot change after creation; an omitted secret or issuer keeps the stored value. Provider identities are `orgslug-` plus six hexadecimal characters and are retried on conflict.

<a id="close-identidad-056"></a>

Each application's callback is `{domain}/auth/social/{providerID}/callback` and its shareable login page is `{domain}/login/{org}`, which redirects to the workspace sign-in view focused on that organization. GET `/api/v1/organizations/{org}/sso` is public and returns the organization's name/slug plus each application's name, brand and start URL, so the sign-in view can offer the buttons without a session. GET `/auth/social/{providerID}/start` runs standard PKCE/nonce discovery against the stored issuer and callback, and the callback completes the flow only when the signed flow cookie names the same provider identity.

<a id="close-identidad-057"></a>

Sign-in through an organization application links the verified subject as `social:{providerID}`, reuses an account with the same verified email or creates an OIDC-only account, and ensures active member membership in the application's organization because the identity provider vouches for that workspace. Existing password, instance OIDC, recovery, invitation and open-mode behavior are unchanged. The Account view does not expose application secrets; the Organization view lists applications with their callback URLs for admins and owners and offers add, edit and delete.

<a id="close-identidad-058"></a>

Organization applications also accept the `github` and `gitlab` presets. Additive migrations `070_social_oauth2_providers.sql`, `071_gitea_social_provider.sql` and `072_digitalocean_social_provider.sql` extend the provider constraint without rewriting rows. GitHub, GitLab, Gitea and DigitalOcean use the OAuth2 Authorization Code flow with PKCE, not an ID token. GitHub reads the verified primary address from `/user/emails`; GitLab requires an active account with a confirmed address. The server token exchange and profile fetch are bounded by the existing ten/fifteen-second flow deadlines, a 1 MiB response limit and the fixed `https://` rule. Hosted GitHub defaults to `https://github.com` and `https://api.github.com`; hosted GitLab defaults to `https://gitlab.com`. A self-hosted `https://` server URL is supported for GitHub, GitLab and Gitea, using `/api/v3` for GitHub Enterprise and `/api/v4` for GitLab. Gitea requires an explicit HTTPS server URL and reads an active `/api/v1/user` profile with its login, full name and email. DigitalOcean uses its published authorize/token endpoints, requests `read` and requires an active `email_verified` `/v2/account`; the account UUID is the stable subject.

<a id="close-identidad-059"></a>

The `microsoft` preset keeps the stored `openid_connect` provider and defaults its issuer to `https://login.microsoftonline.com/common/v2.0`. Microsoft ID tokens do not uniformly provide `email_verified`; this preset therefore requires a nonempty tenant (`tid`) and accepts the verified issuer-backed `email` claim or `preferred_username` as the address. Generic OIDC and Google continue to require `email_verified:true`. A Microsoft token without a tenant or usable address is rejected.

<a id="close-identidad-060"></a>

Successful OAuth2 sign-in writes the same `social:{providerID}` identity with the stable provider subject (numeric account ID or DigitalOcean account UUID), then uses the existing account-link, organization-membership and session path. Clients cannot select a provider during update. The public social-app shape reports the selected provider; OAuth2 providers never expose their client secret. A missing usable email, inactive GitHub/Gitea/DigitalOcean or unconfirmed GitLab account, malformed profile, failed exchange, oversized response or cross-application flow callback fails without creating an identity or session.

### Compatible user API tokens and recovery codes

<a id="close-identidad-061"></a>

Additive migration `056_account_tokens.sql` stores user-scoped API tokens and staged recovery codes. `user_api_tokens` holds the owning user, a label of at most255 characters, a 16-bit scope mask, a lookup hash and an AES-256-GCM sealed 32-byte secret. The secret is sealed under a key and associated data derived from SECRET_KEY and the fixed purpose `GoodSentry user tokens v1`; it is shown at creation and on every list read, never logged. `compat_recovery_codes` holds one staged set of ten code hashes per user. Deleting a user removes both rows through foreign keys.

<a id="close-identidad-062"></a>

GET, POST `/api/0/api-tokens/` and DELETE `/api/0/api-tokens/{id}/` serve the published shape: integer `id`, string `label`, scope names in ascending bit order, the 64-character hexadecimal `token` and a UTC millisecond `created` instant. These routes are session-only: any `Authorization` header or missing session answers 401 `Unauthorized`, and mutations without a valid same-origin CSRF token answer 403 `CSRF check Failed`. Validation answers 422 with the published detail shape and locations rooted at `body.payload`, `query` or `path`: missing `scopes` is required, null is an invalid integer, fractional numbers are fractional integers, unparseable strings are integer-parsing errors and objects are invalid integers. An empty scope array grants no scope; a non-empty array containing an unknown or non-string name reports `Invalid scope`. Booleans, whole floats and numeric strings coerce as integers. `label` defaults to an empty string when absent or null and must be a string of at most255 characters. Unknown body fields are ignored. Malformed JSON answers 400 `Cannot parse request body`; an empty body answers 422. Missing tokens answer 404 `Not Found` and non-integer path identities answer 422. The scope mask keeps the low16 bits, so -1 grants every scope and unknown bits are dropped; the mask maps bit0..15 to project read/write/admin/releases, team read/write/admin, event read/write/admin, organization read/write/admin and member read/write/admin. The list is newest-first with bidirectional cursor `Link` pages. Limit0, an omitted limit or a value above100 uses100; a negative limit returns an empty page whose next cursor points at the newest entry; an empty cursor means the newest page; an unparseable cursor answers 422.

<a id="close-identidad-063"></a>

A user token also authenticates other compatible reads and writes as a Bearer credential. Only the native scope names it carries apply, unknown scope bits grant nothing, and organization routes resolve membership through the token owner, answering 404 outside the owner's workspaces. The token management routes themselves never accept Bearer credentials.

<a id="close-identidad-064"></a>

GET, POST `/api/0/generate-recovery-codes/` stage single-use account recovery codes. GET always generates ten distinct eight-digit codes, replaces any staged set and returns them as `codes`; it accepts session or Bearer credentials and answers 401 `Unauthorized` without them. POST consumes the staged set: a matching code answers 204 and clears the set, a wrong code answers 400 `Invalid code`, and posting with no staged set answers 400 `No recovery codes set, use GET first`. Missing or non-string codes answer 422. The staged set is independent of native two-factor backup codes, which keep their own storage and lifecycle.

## Ingestion contract

<a id="close-errores-001"></a>

Support POST /api/{project}/store/ and /api/{project}/envelope/, including deployments under a URL path prefix. Parse Sentry protocol v7 authentication from X-Sentry-Auth, query parameters and the envelope dsn header. Multiple supplied identities must agree and match the route's project. An absent or invalid SDK key returns 403. Never use management tokens as SDK credentials.

<a id="close-errores-002"></a>

Envelope headers and item headers are one-line UTF-8 JSON. Item length counts bytes and permits arbitrary binary data and internal newlines. Missing length consumes one line. An optional terminal newline, zero-length attachments, unknown attributes and unknown item types must not corrupt subsequent items. Reject malformed/truncated framing and negative/overflowing lengths. Preserve unsupported item metadata and account for unsupported types without pretending to provide their feature.

<a id="close-errores-003"></a>

Accept identity, gzip, zlib-wrapped deflate, Brotli and Zstandard HTTP bodies. Support legacy store zlib+base64 encoding without Content-Encoding. Apply compressed and decompressed byte limits and bounded item counts. Return 413 on limits, 400 on malformed data, 415 on unsupported encodings and 503 on unavailable durable storage. Successful event submission returns 200 JSON with its event id. Return rate limits using 429, Retry-After and X-Sentry-Rate-Limits. Browser SDK requests support the documented simple content types and OPTIONS/CORS, with rate-limit headers exposed.

<a id="close-errores-004"></a>

Persist errors/messages with timestamps, exception chains, stack frames, breadcrumbs, tags, users, request, contexts, SDK, environment, release and arbitrary extra fields. Store binary attachments as PostgreSQL bytes associated with event IDs and use a generated SHA-256 storage ID; treat client filenames only as metadata, never as paths. Protect attachment reads with project authorization.

## Issues and artifacts

<a id="close-errores-005"></a>

Default grouping is an independently designed stable hash of exception type and normalized stack location, with message fallback. Respect SDK custom fingerprint arrays and the {{ default }} marker. Grouping is project-scoped and deterministic. Repeated events update first/last seen and occurrence counts once. A new event for a resolved issue creates a visible regression according to recorded resolution state. Support unresolved/resolved/ignored, assignment, comments, search by text/status/environment/release/tags and sorting with pagination.

<a id="close-errores-006"></a>

Merge groups without losing events or their original grouping keys; subsequent events follow the merged association. Unmerge restores the original partition. Retain activity history. Release operations support create/finalize/list/delete, commit metadata and deploys. Traditional source maps and debug-ID artifacts must turn a known minified JavaScript frame into its original file, line, column and source context. Upload acceptance alone is insufficient. Native minidumps and debug symbol formats require explicit upload and symbolication proof. Modern sentry-cli artifact bundles need their own verified compatibility contract.

### Issue search, historical filters and pages

<a id="close-errores-007"></a>

Complete the existing native session-authenticated `GET /api/v1/organizations/{org}/issues` and original UI before claiming this workflow complete. This increment owns search, tag/environment/release selectors, ordering and pages; assignment/activity/regression completion, merge/unmerge and compatible API0 listing remain separate required increments. Keep bootstrap/session/CSRF controls and stored telemetry, grouping, issue summaries and source maps unchanged.

<a id="close-errores-008"></a>

The native query accepts single `project`, `query`, `status`, `environment`, `release`, `sort`, `page` parameters; unknown or repeated parameters and malformed URL encoding are400. Project is an optional canonical positive int64 project ID; an absent/foreign selected project returns404 under the same organization membership snapshot. Missing status means all states, preserving the existing API; `all` is an explicit equivalent for the UI. Otherwise accept unresolved/resolved/ignored. Missing/empty sort means `date` (last seen descending); `freq` sorts lifetime count descending and `new` sorts first seen descending, all with ID descending as deterministic tie breaker. Missing page means1; supplied page must be canonical decimal1..1000000. Pages contain25 and return `data`, `total`, `pageSize:25` and canonical `page`. Empty or past-end pages return200 with empty data and the real total. Validate UTF-8/no NUL, query at most4096bytes, environment at most128 Unicode characters and release at most255 Unicode characters (exact identity, no trimming/case folding). Empty environment/release omit that selector.

<a id="close-errores-009"></a>

Search accepts free text plus intersected `key:value` terms. Bare keys address exact tag keys except reserved `is`/`status`, `environment`, `release`, `level`; explicit `tags[key]:value` or `tags["key"]:value` accesses a colliding/arbitrary tag key. Prefix `!` negates that term. Values can be bare strings, JSON-style double-quoted strings or nonempty comma-separated lists in brackets; lists mean OR within the term and terms mean AND. Bare `*` is a wildcard; quoted values match literally, including a quoted star. SQL percent/underscore/backslash are always literal input characters. Structured values are case-sensitive. Status terms accept only unresolved/resolved/ignored and no wildcard. Bound queries to20terms,20values per term and tag keys to128 Unicode characters. Missing values, malformed quotes/lists/tag brackets and invalid status values return400 with a useful error. Any unnegated bare token ending in a colon without an attached value (for example `Error:` or `customer:`) is free text, preserving ordinary message searches; it does not consume the next space-separated word as its value. Explicit `tags[key]:` and negated missing values remain invalid. Quoted empty values are valid except for status. `env` is an ordinary tag key; only `environment` is reserved. `release:latest` is a literal version in this native contract. Free tokens/quoted phrases, wherever placed between terms, join with one space and perform a case-insensitive literal substring search against stored event title or raw event JSON. There is no implicit boolean-expression language or latest-release alias in this native contract; compatible API0 semantics remain separate work.

<a id="close-errores-010"></a>

Keep domain values explicit with `IssueSearch{Text string; Terms []IssueSearchTerm}`, `IssueSearchTerm{Field string; Key string; Negated bool; Values []IssueSearchValue}` and `IssueSearchValue{Value string; Wildcard bool}`. Field is tag/status/environment/release/level. `ParseIssueSearch(string) (IssueSearch,error)` returns errors wrapping `ErrInvalidIssueFilter`. The existing `IssueFilter` and `Issues` entry point retain their callers; the provider parses/validates before acquiring a database snapshot. No schema or dependency change is needed. Parameterize every user value/key and escape SQL pattern metacharacters; query text must never become SQL identifiers/operators.

<a id="close-errores-011"></a>

Evaluate explicit environment/release, event-level structured terms and free text within **one existing event attached to the issue**. Support string-valued SDK tags in object form and array-of-string-pairs form without rewriting raw data. Missing/non-string tag values do not match; negation means that event does not match the positive predicate, including absent tags. Issue-level status terms and the explicit status selector intersect separately. Historical matching must work when the newest sample has a different environment/release/tag; combinations split across different events do not match. Matching selects issues only: title/status/count/firstSeen/lastSeen remain their existing lifetime summaries and ordering uses those summaries. The UI explains that distinction. Merged-hidden issues remain hidden and queries follow existing events.issue_id ownership.

<a id="close-errores-012"></a>

Organization membership/selected-project authorization, count and row page use one read-only repeatable-read PostgreSQL transaction. A concurrent capture or triage may produce either complete snapshot, never a total from one state and rows from another. Reuse the existing typed issue DTO and canonical organization slug; do not add test-only synchronization flags or SQL mocks. Confirm real25+1 pages, deterministic orders/ties, historical same-event selection, raw object/pair tags, quoting/lists/negation/wildcards, missing/scalar tag controls, other-organization/project denial, concurrent real triage and reopen persistence against PostgreSQL. Parser tests own malformed grammar and pattern intent; transport tests own URL/query rejection and authenticated response wiring rather than replaying every storage combination.

<a id="close-errores-013"></a>

A mixed request with independently billable logs or spans still receives its one normal project throttle sample; if admitted, only those independent items consume project-key quota. ### Compatible issue comments

<a id="close-errores-061"></a>

`GET/POST /api/0/issues/{issue}/comments/` and the corresponding organization-prefixed routes require `event:read` or `event:write`; mutations require `event:write`. The handler verifies the issue through CompatibleIssue for the current Bearer project grant before invoking the native comment transaction. Lists accept strict `limit`/`per_page` from1..100 and an offset cursor, order newest first, and emit prefixed Link navigation.

<a id="close-errores-062"></a>

POST and PUT accept only `{"data":{"text":"..."}}` and return CommentSchema with numeric comment ID, type`note`, data.text, UTC creation instant and user id/email. Text follows the native1..10000-byte UTF-8/no-NUL rule. Authors or organization owners/admins may edit/delete according to the native comment transaction; missing or foreign issues/comments return404, non-authors403. Native and compatible comments share rows, activity and deletion semantics.

### Compatible issue tags

<a id="close-errores-063"></a>

`GET /api/0/issues/{issue}/tags/` and the organization-prefixed route require `event:read` or `event:write`. After the issue is validated for the current membership and Bearer project grant, tags are aggregated across the current issue and its merged aliases. Both normalized object tags and two-element array tags are supported; only string values are counted, and duplicate key/value pairs within one event count once.

<a id="close-errores-064"></a>

Each IssueTagSchema includes the exact key, a title-cased display name, distinct-value count, total pair occurrences and up to five top values ordered by count descending then value. Tags are ordered by key. Optional `key` filters the returned list by exact, case-sensitive key equality without changing aggregation. An absent key returns all tags; an empty or unmatched key returns `[]`. Unknown or repeated query parameters and malformed query encoding return400. Both route aliases use the same behavior. The route returns an empty array for an issue without tags.

### Compatible user preferences

<a id="close-errores-065"></a>

Additive047 stores a JSONB user-options object and subscribeByDefault preference with defaults that preserve existing accounts. PUT /api/0/users/{id}/ accepts UserIn name and timezone/language/theme/clock/stack-order options for the current user or me; name remains1..255 UTF-8 characters without NUL. GET user already returns the stored options and existing primary identity.

<a id="close-errores-066"></a>

Additive050_user_emails.sql adds globally unique secondary addresses without rewriting users. `GET /api/0/users/{id}/emails/` returns the primary address first followed by verified secondary addresses and outstanding unverified additions. POST accepts EmailAddressIn, stores a 24-hour one-use verification token and sends a purpose-specific plain-text message. Repeating POST for the same unverified address refreshes its token and delivery; a primary or verified duplicate returns400. PUT promotes only a verified secondary address and atomically moves the former primary into the verified-secondary set. DELETE removes unverified or verified secondary addresses but never the primary. POST `/emails/confirm/` resends an outstanding verification message. All routes are current-user/`me` only, impose strict JSON/email bounds and return private no-store responses through the shared writer.

<a id="close-errores-067"></a>

The emailed link is `GET /account/emails/confirm?token=...` on the configured domain/prefix. It validates one 64-hex single-use token, marks exactly that address verified and redirects to the application. Verified secondary addresses can authenticate with the existing password, recover the account, receive organization invitations and be resolved as assignees/actors without changing the primary address returned in the general UserSchema. OIDC and invitation membership checks include verified secondary addresses, while unverified rows never authenticate or satisfy membership. The Account UI lists every address with primary/verified/pending state, adds addresses, resends verification, promotes a verified secondary and confirms secondary removal. It updates the visible identity after a primary swap and exposes recoverable inline errors without dead controls.

<a id="close-errores-068"></a>

GET/PUT /users/{id}/notifications/ and /notifications/alerts/ expose and update subscribeByDefault for the current user. Email management is never simulated by changing users.email directly; primary changes use the verified swap transaction above. Browser acceptance remains the final UI gate.

Project event GET still requires project:read; release mutation still requires project:releases. event:admin/deletion remains required later and is not advertised yet. Token creation/list/revocation remains owner/admin; a valid writer must still be a current member when its Store transaction authorizes.

PUT paths require canonical positive int64 issue IDs, exact trailing slash and no query parameters. A missing slash receives the pinned Go1.27.2 ServeMux's ordinary307 redirect; that noncanonical request never invokes the mutator. Both return200 with the same compatible detail shape, actual post-change status/actor/policy and real analytics; {} is an authorized read of the current issue with no expiry/activity/policy write. Invalid JSON/UTF-8, duplicate/unknown fields, nonobject/null body, extra JSON values or body over1MiB produce400. <a id="close-errores-072"></a>

Status accepts unresolved/resolved/ignored plus muted (explicit alias of ignored) and resolvedInNextRelease (resolved with native next_release policy); output uses the native normalized status. status omitted or null means unchanged, while empty/unknown status is invalid. A policy-only request applies to the current locked issue status; it must fit that status. An incompatible policy/status combination returns400. Omitted policy keeps native omission behavior, including preserving an equal active mute or resolution. Explicit policy null/empty object clears the active native condition: ignored becomes indefinite ignored; resolved becomes simple resolved; unresolved remains unresolved. Empty/null policy does not by itself change status. resolvedInNextRelease always selects next_release; an explicitly conflicting clear/release/ignore policy is invalid.

<a id="close-errores-073"></a>

assignedTo omitted means no assignment update; null or empty string explicitly unassigns. Accept canonical bare user ID, user:ID, team:ID, or a member's primary email. Bare numeric references must be canonical positive IDs; prefixed targets require that exact type and ID. Other bare strings are looked up as primary email, never display name or a fabricated username; missing targets return404. Validate UTF-8, no NUL/control characters and at most254 bytes. Primary email lookup is case-insensitive, restricted to current organization membership and rejects ambiguous case-folded matches. Users and teams must belong to the issue's organization; the same native target locks/actor fields apply. Username handles require a real future identity contract and remain open, not inferred from display names.

<a id="close-errores-074"></a>

statusDetails accepts only inRelease, inNextRelease, inCommit, ignoreDuration, ignoreCount, ignoreUserCount, ignoreWindow and ignoreUserWindow, with null/object as above. Nullable inRelease/inNextRelease follow GT input admission; null conveys no concrete selector. A nonempty actual inRelease selects its exact native version; latest selects the current project's most recently created real associated release, with numeric release ID descending tie-break, including archived candidates consistently with native candidate reads. No eligible latest yields404. Empty inRelease or inNextRelease:false without another concrete selector explicitly clears only the resolution condition and requires effective resolved status. Null selector values alone convey no concrete selector and retain the generic clear intent of a present statusDetails container. inNextRelease:true selects native next_release; combining it with a nonempty inRelease is400. Any resolution selector requires effective resolved status. Any mute-condition field requires effective ignored status. Mixing resolution-selector fields and mute fields is400 even when a selector is null/false or a count is zero.

<a id="close-errores-075"></a>

A nonnull `inCommit` is accepted only inside `statusDetails` as an object with exactly `repository` and `commit` string fields. Both are nonempty valid UTF-8 text of at most200 characters without controls. It is mutually exclusive with `inRelease` and `inNextRelease` and requires effective resolved status. Match both values exactly against `release_commits` linked to the issue's project through `release_projects`; a commit known only to another repository or project is invalid and returns400. If the same identity appears in multiple linked releases, select the earliest `(created_at,id)` release as its immutable resolution boundary. Save the exact repository/commit identity plus the release snapshot; expose `statusDetails.inCommit` and the selected `inRelease`. Future fresh events use the existing release comparison/regression behavior against that snapshot. An identical commit policy is a no-op; clearing or replacing it follows existing resolution-policy rules. `inCommit:null` supplies no selector and follows existing null-selector clear semantics. This API does not auto-detect a local Git HEAD, parse commit messages, or contact a repository provider. These boundaries are GoodSentry choices: the public [Sentry bulk issue API](https://docs.sentry.io/api/events/bulk-mutate-an-organizations-issues/) supplies the `{commit,repository}` shape, while the [Sentry CLI](https://cli.sentry.dev/commands/issue/) describes explicit commit selection against a registered repository.

<a id="close-errores-076"></a>

The shared resolution input adds mode `commit` with the exact `Commit` and `Repository` values; the persisted policy also retains the selected release's existing immutable snapshot. Compatible policy normalization adds a typed commit kind and passes these values to the Store, which owns lookup and project scope. The native issue response may expose that stored resolution, but this increment does not add a native PATCH/UI commit picker.

<a id="close-errores-077"></a>

ignoreDuration/ignoreCount/ignoreUserCount/ignoreWindow/ignoreUserWindow may be top-level or nested; if both copies appear their integers must agree.
