Any actual resolution-key presence, even null/false, conflicts with mute-number fields. Values must be JSON integers0..2147483647, not null. Zero disables that individual condition; all disabled means indefinite ignore. Positive duration is elapsed minutes; ignoreCount is additional fresh unique issue receipts using the existing private receipt counter; ignoreUserCount is additional distinct identified users admitted while the policy is active. Positive ignoreWindow and ignoreUserWindow are elapsed rolling intervals of1..10080 whole minutes and require positive ignoreCount and ignoreUserCount respectively; zero or omission preserves the corresponding unbounded count. Both top-level and nested user/count-window fields are accepted by individual and bulk issue updates. An identified user follows the existing affected-user identity rule (first nonblank ID, email, username or non-auto IP; numeric and equivalent decimal-string IDs match; attribute namespaces remain separate and matching stays case-sensitive). Anonymous captures, transactions, feedback and duplicate event receipts do not advance the user count. Without a user window, the user threshold expires on the first fresh error/message from a previously unseen identity. With a user window, each nonbaseline identity contributes once while its latest eligible receipt is in the inclusive interval [receipt_now-ignoreUserWindow, receipt_now]; another receipt refreshes that identity, and it can contribute again after its prior receipt ages out. Baseline identities stay excluded, and userRemaining can increase as receipts age out. Simultaneous duration/count/user-count expiry records duration before count before user_count. Conditions retain OR semantics.

<a id="close-errores-078"></a>

Open mode's persisted generated key supports restart. Replicas that share a database must share `SECRET_KEY`, consistent with the existing credential contract. Seed the digest set from the issue group's retained events when a user-count condition begins. A newly admitted identified user is recorded in the same issue-lock transaction as its receipt; retries do not add progress. A rolling user window stores the latest database receipt instant alongside each nonbaseline digest; baseline digests have no counting timestamp. Digest rows are owned by the issue and are deleted when the condition is cleared or expires. Event retention during an active mute does not erase its baseline or progress. Reapplying an identical policy preserves its digest set and timestamps. When groups merge into a root with an active user-count policy, identities from absorbed event partitions are added as baseline while the root's already-counted additional users remain additional. Unmerge keeps the active mute and its accumulated progress on the surviving root; detached partitions receive no mute. A bounded issue-locked maintenance batch removes stale nonbaseline digests after their user window; event retention does not erase active baseline or progress. Reads expose configured ignoreUserCount and nonnegative, dynamically windowed userRemaining without mutating or expiring state.

<a id="close-errores-079"></a>

Compatible individual/bulk updates support `ignoreWindow`, `ignoreUserWindow` and commit-bound resolution; the native session-authenticated PATCH/UI exposes the rolling mute windows. Its issue detail form edits and shows both rolling windows, additional-user input and remaining counts without losing other conditions. Existing OR semantics, identical-policy idempotence, expiry-on-write behavior, count/late/retry/grouping handling and alert suppression remain unchanged. Native release selection, comparison, immutable snapshots, pending binding, no-op behavior and regression continue to govern API-created policies. Reads do not bind or expire them.

### Native merge, original partitions and unmerge

<a id="close-errores-113"></a>

Complete native merging and separation of error issues through the existing authenticated UI. Preserve each original fingerprint and raw event, route future captures through the merged association and permit separating any original partition, including the current root. Sources: [Sentry merge/unmerge](https://docs.sentry.io/concepts/data-management/event-grouping/merging-issues/), [bulk mutation](https://docs.sentry.io/api/events/bulk-mutate-a-list-of-issues/), [GlitchTip5 merge/unmerge](https://glitchtip.com/blog/2025-05-19-glitchtip-5-0-released/) and its [public OpenAPI](https://app.glitchtip.com/api/openapi.json). These establish the product capability but do not fully define retained triage state, comment ownership or splitting APIs. The following rules are GoodSentry's native contract. Compatible API0 merging, hashes and event scopes remain mandatory separate acceptance.

<a id="close-errores-114"></a>

An issue row owns its original project/fingerprint forever. A visible root has merged_into NULL; every other member points directly to that root in the same project. The lowest numeric member ID is the root. Merging existing groups flattens all members into one group. events.original_issue_id retains the original partition while events.issue_id identifies the current root. Do not copy events, raw JSON, attachments or source maps, or change SDK IDs, occurrence/receipt timestamps or release observations. When a legacy error has a current owner and NULL original_issue_id, save that owner as its origin before the first native reparenting. Historical records with both issue IDs NULL remain unowned; never infer ownership from their raw payload. Transactions are never issue members. Repeated SDK IDs return the event's current root without capture side effects.

<a id="close-errores-115"></a>

Each fresh error increments only its effective root's receipt_count once under the existing issue lock. Retries, transactions and all merge/unmerge operations leave it unchanged. Conditional mute baseline, remaining and expiry use this counter. Preserve existing issue_ignores rows, baselines, deadlines, remaining progress, raw telemetry, history and001..014 migration receipts. Keep the counter private. A group may have fewer current historical events than its capture progress after a split; never clamp or reset progress to the new count. The first fresh capture of a zero-occurrence partition initializes its current first/last seen and latest text from that occurrence, while first_seen activity still depends only on capture progress. This foundation can ship before the grouping API and must retain all current behavior on its own.

<a id="close-errores-116"></a>

Add a subsequent migration016 for topology constraints and indexes. Enforce same-project parent references, strictly lower parent IDs and no self reference. Index direct members and original event ownership.
