### Native debug-file storage prerequisite

#### CFI extended dereference

<a id="close-nativo-001"></a>

Support DWARF `DW_OP_xderef` (`0x18`) and `DW_OP_xderef_size` (`0x95`) in CFI expressions. The top stack value is the address and the value immediately below it is the address-space identifier. GoodSentry maps identifier `0` to the process address space represented by captured Minidump memory ranges; reject every other identifier because the current unwinder has no other address-space source. `DW_OP_xderef` reads the target pointer width. `DW_OP_xderef_size` consumes one unsigned byte and reads 1 through the target pointer width, zero-extending little-endian bytes. Read only captured memory. Invalid stack shape, size, address-space identifier, unavailable bytes, truncated operands or branch targets into the size operand stop unwinding without partial frames. This mapping is GoodSentry's implementation-defined address calculation under [DWARF 5](https://dwarfstd.org/doc/DWARF5.pdf).

<a id="close-nativo-002"></a>

Additive042 stores project-scoped debug files with a UUID identity, SHA-1 content identity, original bytes and bounded metadata. Compatible `GET/POST /api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/files/dsyms/` supports pagination and one multipart file per request under MAX_BODY_BYTES. Current organization/project grants and project:releases protect upload; sessions require owner/admin and mutation CSRF. Duplicate SHA-1 content returns the original identity without replacing stored data. Existing project/telemetry rows and migration checksums survive upgrade and reopen. Upload is durable storage, not proof of native symbolication.

<a id="close-nativo-003"></a>

The compatible `POST /api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/reprocessing/` keeps Sentry CLI's empty `200` response and durably queues work before responding. Store at most one queue row per project. Each accepted request snapshots the greatest row ID for that project's native error events and restarts its cursor at the beginning; repeated requests coalesce and include the newest snapshot. A startup and idle worker locks one project queue row at a time with `SKIP LOCKED`, then processes at most 10 stored error events per transaction whose platform is `native` and whose row ID is within that snapshot. It loads only that project's debug files under the existing 25-file/64MiB symbol budget. Use each event's existing `debug_meta.images` and stack-frame `package`/`instruction_addr` to match uploaded objects. Fill an existing frame's missing function and available source filename/line only when a matching object resolves it; never add frames or unwind because raw Minidumps are not retained. Preserve all other event JSON, event IDs, timestamps, issue ownership/grouping, counts and notifications. Commit changed event JSON and cursor together; a failed transaction leaves both available for retry. Delete completed queue rows, and cascade pending work when its project is deleted.

<a id="close-nativo-004"></a>

Chunk negotiation advertises `debug_files` before `artifact_bundles`, a 64MiB maximum file, bounded SHA-1 chunks, the configured request limit, gzip and concurrency2. `POST /api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/files/difs/assemble/` follows the official CLI state contract: missing chunks return200 with `state:not_found` and the exact missing SHA-1 list; the CLI uploads missing gzip parts; a subsequent all-present request verifies the complete SHA-1, saves the original bytes and returns `state:ok` with the stored compatible debug-file DTO. Invalid metadata, cross-project grants, expired/missing chunks and mismatches fail without a partial file. The actual SHA-256-pinned `sentry-cli 3.8.0` completes negotiation, chunk upload and assembly for a textual Breakpad `.sym`; the compatible dSYM multipart route remains available. Stored textual Breakpad, ELF, Mach-O, dSYM companion and bounded ZIP archive formats are consumed by Minidump symbolication. Add bounded TAR and gzip-compressed TAR containers to this consumer: process at most256 logical entries and128MiB of expanded archive bytes in memory, read only regular files as independent candidate objects, and never extract paths or follow links. Require the standard two zero end blocks and zero-only trailing padding; reject malformed/truncated archives and invalid gzip checksums without returning partial symbols. Permit at most eight archive layers, including ZIP, TAR and gzip-compressed TAR. This GoodSentry bound expands compatibility while keeping recursive parsing bounded; share entry and expanded-byte budgets across layers and charge both each nested container member and its expanded descendants. Reject an archive deeper than eight layers or a malformed nested archive as a whole without returning partial symbols. Classic PDB/MSF 7 procedure, C13 source-line, stripped-public and inline records are consumed; legacy/Portable PDB and complete native unwinding remain required. The bounded readers use the Go 1.27 standard-library contracts for [archive/zip](https://pkg.go.dev/archive/zip), [archive/tar](https://pkg.go.dev/archive/tar) and [compress/gzip](https://pkg.go.dev/compress/gzip).

For standalone Portable PDBs, consume the ECMA-335 metadata root, `#Pdb`,
`#~` and referenced heaps using checked stream and table extents. Resolve
Document names and MethodDebugInformation sequence points, including document
switches, signed and unsigned compressed integers and hidden points. Bound
each input to the existing64MiB debug-file limit, metadata rows and decoded
sequence points to1,000,000 each, and names to4,096 bytes. Reject malformed
or unsupported metadata without partial mappings. Derive the debug identity
from the `#Pdb` GUID and stamp using the Sentry Portable PDB identity convention;
do not substitute the PE CodeView age. Select matching stored files only from
the current project's existing25-file/64MiB set, including regular members of
the already supported bounded archives. A supplied upload Debug ID must match
the embedded identity; different contents claiming one identity are ambiguous.
Bound retained decoded sequence points for matching identities in that set to
1,000,000 and retained decoded source-name bytes to64MiB; if either limit is
exceeded, capture the event without any Portable PDB enrichment rather than
retaining a partial set.

For captured managed frames with `addr_mode:rel:N`, use N to select a
`pe_dotnet` image in `debug_meta.images`, `function_id` as its one-based
MethodDef row and `instruction_addr` as function-relative IL offset. Like
Sentry's Portable PDB cache, omit hidden points from lookup and use the last
visible point at or before the offset within that same method. An offset before
the first visible point has no match; an offset after the last retains the last
point. Map that pair to a source document and line, never to a native RVA or a
method name invented from the Portable PDB. Enrich applicable top-level, exception and
thread stacktraces before normalization; preserve existing source fields and
all unrelated event data, and do not attach a line to a conflicting existing
source path. Missing files, identities, metadata or source points leave frames
unchanged rather than failing capture. Keep native Minidump symbolication and
its existing native-only durable reprocessing contract unchanged. Do not
claim embedded Portable PDB, symbol-server lookup or full native closure from
this standalone slice.

Verify the decoder against an independent `System.Reflection.Metadata` oracle
for a real locally compiled .NET Portable PDB, including identity, method rows,
IL offsets, document changes and hidden points; add owned malformed and budget
controls, archive and cross-project controls, and HTTP/PostgreSQL event tests.
Prove a real Sentry .NET SDK event separately before final acceptance. The
formats and frame fields follow [.NET Portable PDB metadata](https://github.com/dotnet/runtime/blob/main/docs/design/specs/PortablePdb-Metadata.md),
[.NET PE debug entries](https://github.com/dotnet/runtime/blob/main/docs/design/specs/PE-COFF.md)
the [Sentry Portable PDB protocol](https://github.com/getsentry/rfcs/blob/main/text/0013-portable-pdb.md),
and the Symbolic 13.9.0 [cache writer](https://github.com/getsentry/symbolic/blob/13.9.0/symbolic-ppdb/src/cache/writer.rs)
and [lookup](https://github.com/getsentry/symbolic/blob/13.9.0/symbolic-ppdb/src/cache/lookup.rs).

<a id="close-nativo-005"></a>

Textual Breakpad symbol files accept the optional `m` marker on `FUNC` and `PUBLIC` records. Skip that marker when locating the same address, size, parameter-size and name fields used for unmarked records, preserving the current handling of parameter sizes. Keep symbol names with spaces. `PUBLIC` entries provide a source-free fallback range from their address up to the next distinct address in a `FUNC` or `PUBLIC` record; multiple starts at the same address share a range and the first public record wins ties. The final public range is limited to the captured module extent during symbolication. If a `PUBLIC` and `FUNC` both cover an instruction, prefer `FUNC`. Line rows attach only to the most recently accepted `FUNC`; encountering any `PUBLIC` or a malformed `FUNC` clears that association so following rows cannot leak onto a previous function. The marker's identical-code-folding meaning and public-range rules follow the [Breakpad symbol-file format](https://chromium.googlesource.com/breakpad/breakpad/+/HEAD/docs/symbol_files.md) and the [Breakpad address resolver](https://chromium.googlesource.com/external/google-breakpad/src/+/refs/heads/master/processor/basic_source_line_resolver.cc).

<a id="close-nativo-006"></a>

For ELF and Mach-O DWARF, derive source mappings from each compilation unit's line table. A non-end-sequence row applies on its half-open address span up to the next row; an end-sequence row closes the prior span and is never itself a source mapping. Do not bridge end-sequence boundaries, unmapped gaps, or discontiguous procedure ranges. Intersect source spans with each subprogram range and retain the complete function-name range as a fallback without source fields, so a frame outside line coverage still resolves its function without inheriting a misleading line. Bound processing to262,144 line rows per compilation unit and262,144 emitted source spans per object. If a line table is malformed or exceeds its row bound, omit that compilation unit's source spans while preserving function ranges; when the object span bound is reached, retain function ranges and stop emitting additional source spans. Keep line addresses in object virtual-address space until the existing image-base normalization. These rules follow the Go `debug/dwarf` line-reader contract for increasing rows, `EndSequence` boundaries and line lookup ([LineEntry and LineReader](https://go.dev/src/debug/dwarf/line.go)).

<a id="close-nativo-007"></a>

The first native-processing slice accepts `POST /api/{project}/minidump/` with Sentry DSN version/key authentication, the multipart `upload_file_minidump` field and bounded extra form fields. It parses the public Minidump directory, SystemInfo, ThreadList, ModuleList, Exception and Memory64 streams, selects the exception thread context, requires its supported context layout to match the SystemInfo processor architecture, and distinguishes AMD64, x86, ARM32, ARM64 and RISC-V64 from their public context layouts; for x86, the processor architecture value is 0 according to [Breakpad’s public Minidump format](https://chromium.googlesource.com/breakpad/breakpad/+/HEAD/src/google_breakpad/common/minidump_format.h). For AMD64, accept the 1,232-byte base context or an appended XSTATE area of at most 16,384 bytes when context flags include bit `0x40`; ignore the extension for register recovery. Crashpad defines the XSTATE flag and AMD64 base context in its [context format](https://chromium.googlesource.com/crashpad/crashpad/+/refs/heads/main/minidump/minidump_context.h) and [context writer](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/third_party/crashpad/crashpad/minidump/minidump_context_writer.cc); Breakpad bounds optional XSTATE bytes at 16 KiB in its [context reader](https://chromium.googlesource.com/breakpad/breakpad/+/HEAD/src/processor/minidump.cc). For x86, accept the 716-byte base context or an appended XSTATE area of at most 16,384 bytes when context flags include bit `0x40`; ignore the extension for register recovery. Crashpad defines the x86 context size and XSTATE flag in its [context format](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/cc032bd4e8986d7c14c09d9a04c856395b90fe0b/third_party/crashpad/crashpad/minidump/minidump_context.h) and [context writer](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/third_party/crashpad/crashpad/minidump/minidump_context_writer.cc); use the same 16 KiB extension bound defined by Breakpad’s [context reader](https://chromium.googlesource.com/breakpad/breakpad/+/HEAD/src/processor/minidump.cc). The parser constructs fatal exception/debug-meta JSON and ingests it through the normal durable event path and key quota. The request body is bounded by MAX_BODY_BYTES; unsupported context/architectures, malformed multipart and malformed dumps fail without an event. Raw minidumps are not retained.
Module CodeView records carry identity in `RSDS` PDB data or the ELF `BpEL` build-id record. Read `BpEL` from its little-endian signature and bounded build-id bytes, without the 24-byte minimum used by `RSDS`, so Crashpad Linux modules match uploaded ELF symbols. The public format is [MDCVInfoELF](https://chromium.googlesource.com/breakpad/breakpad/+/HEAD/src/google_breakpad/common/minidump_format.h).

Also recognize legacy `NB10` CodeView records through the public
[`MDCVInfoPDB20` layout](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/breakpad/main/src/google_breakpad/common/minidump_format.h).
The fixed header is16 bytes: signature `0x3031424e`, debug-data offset,
little-endian timestamp and little-endian age. Require at least one filename
byte after the header and a terminating NUL at the record's end. Identity uses
the timestamp as eight hexadecimal digits followed by the hexadecimal age,
including a zero age, consistently with
[Breakpad's debug identifier](https://chromium.googlesource.com/breakpad/breakpad/+/refs/heads/main/src/processor/minidump.cc).
The offset and filename do not select or open a local file. A truncated or
unterminated NB10 record contributes no Debug ID; normal unsymbolicated capture
continues. The same timestamp/age identity in an uploaded PDB2 SymCache must
resolve a matching module despite a different basename, including age0, and
must not resolve another timestamp/age. This connects existing SymCache symbols;
it does not implement the legacy MSF/PDB or Portable PDB readers required by004.
Acceptance extends the existing HTTP/PostgreSQL SymCache identity owner with
matching, mismatched, age0 and malformed NB10 records, retaining RSDS cases.


<a id="close-nativo-008"></a>

For each `Memory64ListStream` range, validate its 64-bit file offset against the dump length, then compare its size with the remaining file bytes before slicing. The list-wide base RVA advances by each descriptor's size, as defined by Microsoft's public [`MINIDUMP_MEMORY64_LIST`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/minidumpapiset/ns-minidumpapiset-minidump_memory64_list) contract. Reject out-of-file descriptors with a parse error, without overflow-prone offset addition or partially returning memory ranges. Malformed Minidump bounds must not panic.

<a id="close-nativo-009"></a>

When a full 168-byte-or-larger Exception stream contains its `ThreadContext` location descriptor at offset 160, use the described context as the crashing thread's register source, even when ThreadList also contains a context for that thread. Require the descriptor size and RVA to be both zero (meaning absent) or both nonzero and fully within the dump; a present but malformed or unsupported context rejects the dump without fallback. Preserve compatibility with the existing short 32-byte Exception streams by selecting the matching ThreadList context, then the first available ThreadList context when the exception thread has none. A full stream with an empty descriptor uses the same fallback; partial Exception streams between 32 and 168 bytes fail. This follows Microsoft's public [MINIDUMP_EXCEPTION_STREAM](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/minidumpapiset/ns-minidumpapiset-minidump_exception_stream) and [MINIDUMP_LOCATION_DESCRIPTOR](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/minidumpapiset/ns-minidumpapiset-minidump_location_descriptor) definitions.

<a id="close-nativo-010"></a>

Legacy Breakpad ARM64 Minidumps use processor architecture `0x8003` and a distinct 796-byte context: 64-bit flags at offset 0, x0–x32 beginning at offset 8, then CPSR and the legacy floating-point area. Accept only that exact size, matching SystemInfo architecture, and integer-present flags `0x80000002` or `0x80000006`; ignore floating-point fields and decode x29, SP and PC at offsets 240, 256 and 264. Reject other legacy flags, sizes or architecture pairs. The layout and flags follow [Breakpad's ARM64 context definition](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/breakpad/main/src/google_breakpad/common/minidump_cpu_arm64.h), the old architecture value is in its [Minidump format](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/breakpad/main/src/google_breakpad/common/minidump_format.h), and Breakpad's [context reader](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google/breakpad/main/src/processor/minidump.cc) reads the old context only at its exact structure size before converting it.

<a id="close-nativo-011"></a>

The standard Sentry envelope route also accepts native SDK crash events containing exactly one `event` item whose event JSON has `platform` equal to `native`, and at most one `attachment` item whose `attachment_type` is `event.minidump`. A typed Minidump requires that event and must parse successfully; duplicate typed Minidumps, a missing/non-native event, or malformed bytes reject the envelope before any event or child item is committed. Parse, unwind and symbolicate the attachment through the same project-scoped debug-file set as the multipart route. Preserve the SDK event identity, timestamp, level, message, tags, contexts and other event data. Replace the first exception's stacktrace with the Minidump result while retaining its existing type, value and mechanism; if there is no exception, use the generated native exception. Merge the generated module images into `debug_meta.images`, preferring Minidump module metadata and deduplicating by `(debug_id,image_addr)`. Do not persist typed Minidump bytes in the attachment or opaque-item stores; ordinary attachments and the other independent envelope items retain their existing behavior and limits. The outer envelope and item byte bounds remain the configured MAX_BODY_BYTES limits. This covers the official Sentry Native SDK convention: its [Crashpad backend](https://github.com/getsentry/sentry-native/blob/master/src/backends/sentry_backend_crashpad.cpp) adds the Minidump as a typed envelope attachment, and the [Sentry SDK envelope contract](https://develop.sentry.dev/sdk/envelopes/) defines the shared transport framing.

<a id="close-nativo-012"></a>

Basic stack reconstruction starts at the crashing AMD64/x86/ARM32/ARM64/RISC-V64 context and follows a validated pointer-size-aware frame-pointer chain. For ARM32, accept the 368-byte Crashpad and Breakpad context layouts, read r11/r13/r14/r15 as FP/SP/LR/PC, and use LR as the first caller when no frame-pointer caller is available; do not guess a return address from the stack pointer. ARM64 uses the public Breakpad/Windows ordering for `x29`, `x30`, SP and PC at the start of the context; floating-point/debug tails are ignored. After the authenticated debug files load, bounded ELF `.eh_frame`/`.debug_frame` and Mach-O `__eh_frame`/`__debug_frame` CFI can continue from the crashing registers when the frame-pointer chain is shorter. CIE version 4+ headers read `address_size` and `segment_selector_size` immediately after the augmentation string and before the alignment factors; accept four- and eight-byte addresses and reject nonzero segment selectors until segmented FDE addresses are supported, following [DWARF 4 §6.4.1](https://dwarfstd.org/doc/DWARF4.pdf) and [DWARF 5 §6.4.1.1](https://dwarfstd.org/doc/DWARF5.pdf). ELF CFI covers AMD64, x86 and ARM64, plus little-endian ARM32 through DWARF CFI using registers r0-r15 from the Minidump context and recovered register rules across frames; Mach-O CFI covers AMD64 and ARM64. Missing ARM CIE initial rules default to same-value for callee-saved r4-r8, r10-r11 and r13, and undefined for other core registers; r9 remains undefined unless described because its preservation is platform-specific, following [Arm DWARF32](https://github.com/ARM-software/abi-aa/blob/main/aadwarf32/aadwarf32.rst) and [AAPCS32](https://github.com/ARM-software/abi-aa/blob/main/aapcs32/aapcs32.rst). Little-endian ARM32 ELF also reads `SHT_ARM_EXIDX`/`.ARM.exidx` and linked `.ARM.extab` entries. Validate sorted function entries and bounded `prel31` references; honor `EXIDX_CANTUNWIND`; support personality 0 inline entries and compact personalities 0, 1 and 2 in `.ARM.extab` with integer-register unwind operations for VSP adjustment, VSP-from-register, core-register pops and finish. Bound each file to 262,144 index entries and 16MiB of decoded operations, and each frame to 256 operations. Cleanup/catch descriptors are ignored because symbolication does not perform language exception handling. Generic personalities, reserved operations, VFP/WMMX restoration and pointer authentication remain unsupported and stop unwinding. The format follows the official [EHABI32](https://github.com/ARM-software/abi-aa/blob/main/ehabi32/ehabi32.rst) and [AAELF32](https://github.com/ARM-software/abi-aa/blob/main/aaelf32/aaelf32.rst) definitions. `DW_OP_skip` and `DW_OP_bra` use signed 16-bit offsets from the byte after the operand and branch only to operation boundaries; at most 4,096 expression operations may execute, and malformed or unsupported expressions stop unwinding instead of inventing a frame. Common register-offset and value-offset rules are supported; unknown CFI rules also stop unwinding. `DW_OP_addr` consumes the target pointer width. `DW_OP_shl`, `DW_OP_shr` and `DW_OP_shra` use the top stack entry as a shift count and the preceding entry as the value; counts of 64 or more stop evaluation. `DW_OP_abs`, `DW_OP_neg`, `DW_OP_div` and `DW_OP_mod` use signed 64-bit stack values; `DW_OP_mod` uses the remainder from truncated division, following the [accepted DWARF clarification](https://dwarfstd.org/issues/250924.2.html). `DW_OP_not` complements one value; `DW_OP_nop` leaves the stack unchanged; `DW_OP_rot` rotates the top three entries; relational operators compare signed values and push zero or one. Division or modulo by zero and signed overflow stop evaluation. The evaluator supports address-sized `DW_OP_deref` and `DW_OP_deref_size` with a one-byte size from 1 through the target pointer width. It reads only captured memory, zero-extends the result, and stops on unavailable bytes or invalid sizes. In a CFI `DW_CFA_expression`, a final `DW_OP_stack_value` makes the expression result the register value; without it, the result is an address in captured memory. `DW_CFA_val_expression` always uses its expression result as the register value. For the signed-factored offset instructions `DW_CFA_offset_extended_sf`, `DW_CFA_def_cfa_sf`, `DW_CFA_def_cfa_offset_sf` and `DW_CFA_val_offset_sf`, multiply the signed LEB128 operand by the CIE `data_alignment_factor`; reject signed 64-bit multiplication overflow before storing the rule, following [DWARF 5 §§6.4.2.2–6.4.2.3](https://dwarfstd.org/doc/DWARF5.pdf). A CIE `S` signal-frame marker makes the unwinder use its restored return address unchanged when it looks up the next FDE; ordinary frames continue to use return-address-minus-one. This handles the signal-frame PC convention without claiming platform-specific signal-context register recovery. The address, dereference and expression-result rules follow [DWARF 5](https://dwarfstd.org/doc/DWARF5.pdf). Stored textual Breakpad `.sym` files are parsed from MODULE/FILE/FUNC/PUBLIC/line records. Stored ELF and Mach-O objects are parsed with the standard Go DWARF readers and symbol-table fallbacks; preferred image addresses are normalized to module-relative addresses, and the module Debug ID is used when the uploaded object name differs. A debug upload may also be a ZIP with at most 256 regular entries and 128MiB of declared uncompressed content; each entry is read within its declared size and parsed as an independent native or Breakpad object. TAR and gzip-compressed TAR uploads follow the bounded in-memory contract above and likewise expose only regular files to the object parsers; nested ZIP, TAR and gzip-TAR members recurse under the same eight-layer, shared-budget contract. Unsymbolicated frames retain instruction address and module. Symbol and unwind loading is bounded to 25 stored files/64MiB and occurs only after key authorization. Duplicate stored symbols retain their native SHA-1 behavior. The 32-bit ARM layouts follow [Crashpad MinidumpContextARM](https://chromium.googlesource.com/crashpad/crashpad/+/refs/heads/main/minidump/minidump_context.h) and the legacy [Breakpad ARM context](https://chromium.googlesource.com/external/omaha/+/e2c3f15816f1a394e56433de4fb58db30548fdb0/third_party/breakpad/src/google_breakpad/common/minidump_cpu_arm.h); architecture value5 is defined in the public [Minidump format](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromiumos/platform/google-breakpad/+/f1f5090c82314f281c6934d10494f1d0a66bc8a7/src/google_breakpad/common/minidump_format.h).
Uploaded ELF and Mach-O objects also expose their embedded Debug ID: the GNU build-id note for ELF and `LC_UUID` for Mach-O. This identity is used ahead of the uploaded basename, so a Minidump module resolves symbols when the names differ and a mismatched uploaded identity cannot contribute symbols.


<a id="close-nativo-013"></a>

Scalar CFI expressions support `DW_OP_implicit_value` with an unsigned-LEB128 byte length from1 through the target pointer width, followed by that many bytes in the supported little-endian target representation. Decode the block as a zero-extended scalar; reject empty, truncated or wider-than-pointer blocks without returning partial frames. `DW_CFA_val_expression` uses the resulting scalar as the register value. `DW_CFA_expression` keeps its existing rule: the result is an address unless a terminal `DW_OP_stack_value` marks it as the register value. Composite and wider-than-register implicit blocks remain unsupported. This follows the [DWARF 5 implicit-value operation](https://dwarfstd.org/doc/DWARF5.pdf).

<a id="close-nativo-014"></a>

Decode CFI ULEB128 and SLEB128 operands without truncation: accept only values representable as `uint64` or `int64`, respectively, and reject an overflowing or unterminated encoding. Decode `DW_EH_PE_uleb128` and `DW_EH_PE_sleb128` pointer formats with those same bounded readers, consume the complete encoded value for both FDE start and range, and apply PC-relative bases after sign extension. Before narrowing any encoded CFI register number to the implementation's `uint32` register key, reject values above `math.MaxUint32`; in particular, `DW_OP_regx` and `DW_OP_bregx` must never alias a different register after conversion. Malformed operands stop CFI parsing or expression evaluation without producing partial unwind state. These operand encodings follow [DWARF 5 §2.5.1.1–2 and §7.6](https://dwarfstd.org/doc/DWARF5.pdf), and the EH pointer format assignments follow the [LSB DWARF Exception Header Encoding](https://refspecs.linuxfoundation.org/LSB_2.1.0/LSB-Core-generic/LSB-Core-generic/dwarfehencoding.html).

<a id="close-nativo-015"></a>

For CIE `P` augmentation, consume the personality field according to its supported base and application encoding even when `DW_EH_PE_indirect` is set; ignore only that bit while consuming this field. The value is metadata outside stack reconstruction: do not dereference, resolve or invoke it. Keep indirect FDE pointer encodings unsupported, and reject truncated fields or fields that extend beyond the declared CIE augmentation. This follows GNU assembler’s documented indirect `.cfi_personality` encoding, which names the location containing the personality pointer ([GNU `CFI directives`](https://sourceware.org/binutils/docs/as/CFI-directives.html#cfi_005fpersonality)).

<a id="close-nativo-016"></a>

Add RISC-V64 minidump support for Breakpad architecture `0x8006` and Crashpad `MinidumpContextRISCV64` version 1. Accept only a 528-byte little-endian context, version 1, and integer-valid flags `0x08000001` or integer-plus-floating-point flags `0x08000003`; reject a mismatched architecture, size, version or other flag combination. The context fields are `context_flags`, `version`, `pc`, `regs[31]` for x1-x31, `fpregs[32]` and `fcsr`; x0 is the immutable zero register. Ignore floating-point state for unwinding. Set the crash PC, SP=x2, FP=x8, LR=x1, pointer size 8 and DWARF integer registers x0-x31. For a frame-pointer chain, x8 points to the CFA; read the previous FP at fp-16 and return address at fp-8. Require both reads to be in captured memory, previous FP to be zero or strictly greater than the current FP, and return addresses to be nonzero and unseen; cap the whole stack at 64 frames. If that chain yields no caller, x1 may supply the first caller. Never guess a return address from SP.

<a id="close-nativo-017"></a>

RISC-V64 ELF CFI accepts little-endian ELF64 `EM_RISCV` `.eh_frame` or `.debug_frame` and matches only `riscv64` tables. Map DWARF registers 0-31 to x0-x31 and register 64 to the alternate frame-return column. Keep the active PC separate from DWARF registers, honor each CIE's declared return-address column, and carry recovered integer-register rules into caller frames. Floating-point, vector and CSR registers are not propagated; a CFA or return-address rule that requires unavailable non-integer state stops unwinding. Platform-specific signal-context recovery remains unsupported. The layout and architecture values follow [Crashpad's RISC-V context](https://chromium.googlesource.com/crashpad/crashpad/+/refs/heads/main/minidump/minidump_context.h), [Crashpad's minidump architecture extension](https://chromium.googlesource.com/crashpad/crashpad/+/refs/heads/main/minidump/minidump_extensions.h), [Crashpad's context writer](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/third_party/crashpad/crashpad/minidump/minidump_context_writer.cc), [Breakpad's context reader](https://chromium.googlesource.com/breakpad/breakpad/+/HEAD/src/processor/minidump.cc) and the [RISC-V ELF psABI](https://github.com/riscv-non-isa/riscv-elf-psabi-doc/blob/master/riscv-cc.adoc) and [DWARF register mapping](https://github.com/riscv-non-isa/riscv-elf-psabi-doc/blob/master/riscv-dwarf.adoc).

<a id="close-nativo-018"></a>

The PDB procedure-symbol slice consumes classic Microsoft PDB/MSF 7 files up to the existing 64MiB per-file processing limit. Validate MSF block geometry, directory and every referenced stream before reading; accept only block sizes 512, 1024, 2048 and 4096, at most 4096 streams, 4096 modules, 512 PE sections, 200,000 procedure records and 1,000,000 CodeView records, and return no symbols from a malformed PDB. Read the PDB GUID and age from stream 1, the module records from DBI stream 3, and the section-header stream referenced by DBI optional-debug-header slot 5. Locate that optional header after the declared module, section-contribution, section-map, source-info, type-server-map and EC substreams. Consume the EC byte extent without interpreting its contents or adding alignment, including a nonzero odd-sized extent; the existing total-length validation must reject a truncated or overflowing declaration before returning symbols. This order follows the [LLVM DBI EC and optional-header layout](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/DbiStream.html#ec-substream). Acceptance extends the existing public PDB parser owner with an owned fixture containing25 opaque EC bytes, retaining exact identities and procedure ranges. For the modern procedure records, require the C13 signature (4) and extract only `S_GPROC32`, `S_LPROC32`, their `_ID` forms and the corresponding DPC procedure records from module symbol streams. Convert each valid one-based executable PE section plus procedure offset to an image-relative address using the section virtual address, retain the procedure's declared code size as its frame range, and key the result by the embedded GUID+age. A nonempty multipart Debug ID that disagrees with the PDB identity makes that PDB ineligible for the request. Keep the original procedure spelling. A PDB may be a regular member of the already bounded ZIP/TAR readers.

The first legacy procedure increment also consumes Small MSF 2.00 files with
the exact 44-byte signature, a 60-byte header, 1024/2048/4096-byte pages and
16-bit page indices. Require the file length to equal the declared page count
times page size. Read the current directory's direct page list from page 0;
it must fit in that page. Reserve page 0, both complete FPM copies (8 pages
each at 1/9 for 1024-byte pages, 4 each at 1/5 for 2048, 1 each at 1/2 for
4096), and current directory pages. The directory has a 16-bit stream count
and padding, eight-byte stream entries (size plus an ignored obsolete pointer),
then 16-bit stream-page lists. Preserve the existing 4096-stream, 64MiB active
stream-byte and exact-directory-consumption limits. Active streams 1 onward
must reference distinct in-range pages outside reserved pages; malformed
geometry, lengths, lists or references reject the whole PDB. In both MSF2 and
MSF7, consume stream 0's declared page-list bytes for framing but expose it as
absent: its obsolete directory snapshot may name reused pages, so it contributes
neither page ownership nor active-byte budget and is never read as an object
stream. Never follow obsolete pointer values. These layouts and stream-0
semantics follow Microsoft's pinned [Small MSF reader](https://github.com/microsoft/pdb-rs/blob/2081a0dd6f1150cf965dec10855e75cfecfde176/msf/src/open.rs#L293),
[MSF allocation reader](https://github.com/microsoft/pdb-rs/blob/2081a0dd6f1150cf965dec10855e75cfecfde176/msf/src/open.rs#L593)
and [original MSF implementation](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/PDB/msf/msf.cpp#L1618).

Recognized PDB Info versions before VC70 use their 12-byte version/signature/age
header and derive the NB10 Debug ID from the eight-hex-digit signature followed
by hexadecimal age, including age zero. VC70 and later retain GUID+age and the
28-byte header. Named-stream maps, when required, begin after the applicable
header; VC2 has no such map. This increment requires the existing 64-byte
NewDBI header and equal Info/DBI age, with the V50 age-zero rule below. Accept module CodeView signatures 2 and 4;
signature2 permits the ST procedure records and opaque C11 extents, while modern
procedure kinds and C13/inline data continue to require signature4. Add
`S_LPROC32_ST`/`S_GPROC32_ST` (0x100a/0x100b), whose procedure fields have
the existing layout and whose names use a bounded one-byte length prefix.
Retain exact names, section/range and scope validation, and identity matching.
Bound declared C11 extents in the procedure reader; native019 defines the
following source-reader increment. Owned fixtures must prove procedure output and matching
NB10 identity, malformed lengths/pages/names/ranges rejecting all symbols,
stream-0 reuse in MSF2 and MSF7, and continued MSF7/C13 behavior. A privately
inspected external PDB can supplement those fixtures without redistribution
or executing its companion binary. This is a prerequisite of native004;
old DBI, unresolved legacy source profiles, other legacy layouts, Portable PDB and the broader
native requirements remain open. References: Microsoft's pinned [PDB Info
versions](https://github.com/microsoft/pdb-rs/blob/2081a0dd6f1150cf965dec10855e75cfecfde176/docs/pdb/pdbi_stream.md#L24),
[DBI structures](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/PDB/dbi/dbi.h#L94)
and [CodeView procedure layout](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/include/cvinfo.h#L3722).

The next legacy increment supports NewDBI V50 (19960307) with the 48-byte
`MODI50` prefix. Read its module stream index at26, symbol/C11/FPO sizes at
28/32/36, and two NUL-terminated names after byte48; align each complete record
to four bytes. Validate the FPO extent but do not interpret it. An absent module
stream must not claim symbol, C11 or FPO bytes. Preserve the existing signature2
ST procedure reader and native019 C11 mapping. For V50 only, DBI age zero is
permitted even when Info age is nonzero; a nonzero DBI age must equal Info age.
V60 and later retain the existing age equality check. Do not infer the age or
section RVAs from module names, serialized pointers or the section map.

This increment admits the direct V50 section-map profile: every segment used
by a procedure or C11 line must have a valid logical descriptor whose physical
frame equals its one-based segment number, whose offset, overlay and group are
zero, and whose length contains the referenced range. Resolve its RVA from the
corresponding PE section, checking both descriptor and section bounds. A missing, malformed
or non-direct mapping must not produce partial symbols. Mapping other logical
segments, including nonzero offsets and different frames, remains required
legacy work and must not be claimed as supported by this increment.

When a V50 PDB has no optional PE section-header stream, obtain section headers
from an uploaded PE image with the same embedded NB10 timestamp and age as the
PDB Info identity. Read the image as data only. Validate PE headers, section
extents and the debug-directory records with checked offsets; read each
CodeView record through its `PointerToRawData`, including when that record's
`AddressOfRawData` is zero. When the latter is nonzero, require it to map
unambiguously to the same file extent and size as the pointer. Reject
truncated or conflicting NB10 records, missing NUL-terminated NB10 paths and
ambiguous debug-directory address mappings. Bound each input to64MiB, section
headers to512 and debug-directory records to4096. A supplied upload Debug ID
must match the embedded identity; never use a filename as identity. Do not emit
symbols or an object merely because a PE image was uploaded.

Associate PE and PDB candidates only within the current project's selected
debug-file set, whether they are separate files or regular members of supported
ZIP/TAR/gzip-TAR archives. Preserve archive-wide failure and the existing depth,
entry and expanded-byte budgets. If separate passes are used to collect PE
metadata and decode symbols, each pass must enforce the same archive limits and
must not retain candidates from a malformed archive. Identical section tables
for one NB10 identity may be reused; distinct tables for that identity are
ambiguous and cannot supply external sections. A PDB with no matching usable
sections emits no partial symbols. Existing PDBs with their own valid section
stream continue to use it; a declared but malformed section stream is not
rescued by a companion. Preserve project isolation, upload metadata validation,
current symbolication and reprocessing behavior.

Extend the existing public parser and HTTP/PostgreSQL owners with independently
authored V50 PDB/PE fixtures: matching and mismatched identity, absent companion,
age-zero and different nonzero DBI age, malformed and ambiguous PE records,
multiple modules and C11 source lines, direct files and archive members. Prove
whole-archive rejection and the unchanged modern PDB/ELF/Mach-O owners. Compare
the complete private tuples from a real V50 PDB and its matching PE without
redistributing or executing either file. The older DBIHdr, other record kinds,
unknown historical code pages, Portable PDB and all broader native requirements
remain open. References: Microsoft [V50 modules](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/PDB/dbi/dbi.h#L1138),
[age validation](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/PDB/dbi/pdb.cpp#L826),
[image headers for RVA translation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/debugger/debug-interface-access/idiaaddressmap-set-imageheaders),
and [PE debug directory](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/debug/pe-format#debug-directory-image-only).

The next pre-V50 increment supports the old 24-byte DBI header and C7
signature1 module symbols. The old header contains three stream indices and
padding followed by four bounded substream lengths for modules, section
contributions, section map and file information; validate that the four
substreams exactly fill the stream. It has no DBI age or optional section
header: require a pre-VC50 Info identity and the same project-scoped NB10 PE
association and direct section-map checks above. Decode MODI50 records and
validate their declared extents. Decode C7 `S_LPROC32_16t` and
`S_GPROC32_16t` with their 16-bit type index, distinct field offsets and bounded
length-prefixed names. Preserve procedures and exact identities. For C7 module
line data using the documented ST-name `sstSrcModule` layout, apply the native019
reader and its budgets after validating the declared extent. Other historical
line layouts remain required work.
Reject malformed old headers, mixed incompatible procedure/signature layouts,
bad stream extents and non-direct section mappings without partial symbols.
Keep other old record kinds and the remaining legacy profiles
open. Owned public and HTTP/PostgreSQL tests must cover matching and mismatched
PE identity, sections, malformed records and current format regressions.
References: Microsoft's [old DBI conversion](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/PDB/dbi/dbi.cpp#L260),
[C7 signatures and 16-bit-type procedures](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/include/cvinfo.h#L3363),
and Wine's [old DBI structures](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/master/include/wine/mscvpdb.h#L2449).

<a id="close-nativo-019"></a>

For C13 source lines, read each module's declared `C13ByteSize` after its symbol and C11 byte ranges. Keep the C13 subsection reader separate from the legacy C11 reader below. Parse bounded four-byte-aligned subsection records, consuming `DEBUG_S_LINES`, `DEBUG_S_FILECHKSMS`, and `DEBUG_S_STRINGTABLE`; safely skip other well-framed subsection kinds. Resolve checksum filenames through a module string-table subsection when present or through the PDB `/names` global string table named by stream 1's named-stream map. Each line block's name index must point to a checksum record boundary. Resolve its one-based PE section, relocation offset, code size, and line offsets against an executable section, then associate each source location with a procedure from that module and expose it in the existing frame `filename` and `lineno` fields. A location applies from its line offset up to the next line offset in that block or contribution end, clipped to the procedure range; columns are parsed for structural validation but are not returned. Preserve filename spelling without path normalization. Bound C13 subsections to 65,536 per module, source line records to 1,000,000 per PDB, checksum file records to 65,536 per PDB, generated line-to-procedure ranges to 1,000,000 per PDB, and each filename to 4,096 bytes. Invalid subsection framing, missing referenced strings/checksums, invalid ranges or decreasing line offsets invalidate the entire PDB without partial symbols. Keep legacy source-line decoding, Portable PDB and demangling outside this C13 source-line slice. Format references: [LLVM MSF format](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/MsfFile.html), [PDB Info stream and named-stream map](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/PdbStream.html), [DBI and module stream contracts](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/DbiStream.html), [CodeView procedure records](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/CodeViewSymbols.html), [CodeView line subsection layout](https://www.llvm.org/doxygen/DebugLinesSubsection_8h_source.html), [CodeView checksum records](https://www.llvm.org/doxygen/DebugChecksumsSubsection_8cpp_source.html), [PDB string-table layout](https://www.llvm.org/doxygen/RawTypes_8h_source.html); Microsoft CodeView signatures are documented in [cvinfo.h](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/master/include/cvinfo.h).

The following legacy source increment consumes raw C11 in the native018
pre-VC70 Info/NewDBI64 profile with module CodeView signature2. Read exactly
the declared C11 bytes immediately after `SymBytes`, whose count already
includes the four-byte module signature. Require a four-byte-aligned extent.
All fields are little-endian. The root contains u16 file/segment counts,
u32 file offsets, segment start/end u32 pairs and u16 segment IDs. Each file
record contains u16 contribution count/padding, u32 line-table offsets,
start/end pairs and a one-byte-length-prefixed filename, aligned to four bytes.
Each line table contains u16 segment/pair counts, u32 section offsets, u16 line
numbers and alignment to four bytes. File and line-table offsets are relative
to the beginning of the C11 extent. Check all counts, aligned referenced
extents and names before reading; do not assume physical ordering or require
unused padding to be zero. Empty or embedded-NUL ST names, invalid references,
unknown sections, reversed/out-of-section ranges and decreasing line offsets
invalidate the entire PDB, including its previously decoded procedures.
Once the MSF2 or MSF7 signature identifies a PDB, a parse failure must not
fall through to other object readers or salvage embedded Breakpad text.

Interpret each known SE end as the last included byte and convert to a half-open
extent with checked uint64 arithmetic. A root start/end pair of (0,0) denotes
unknown bounds, not the one-byte range at offset zero. Validate its segment
and use known file contributions for that segment without asserting root
coverage. A file contribution with unknown (0,0) bounds is resolved from its
line offsets and same-module procedures: validate each offset in its section
and any known root coverage, associate it only with procedures containing that
offset, and end it at the next distinct line offset or that procedure's end,
whichever comes first. Clip also to the containing known root range when the
root is known. Do not carry a line into a different procedure without its own
line record. Preserve duplicates, zero-line boundaries and existing budgets.
This bounded attribution policy does not establish other historical layouts.
Validate known root ranges against their PE
sections. Once per module, sort and merge known root ranges by segment; each
known file contribution must be covered by that segment's union using binary
search unless that segment has an unknown root, never a scan of all root
ranges per contribution. This bounds coverage work
to O(R log R + T log R). Root envelopes only validate coverage: never use
them to bridge gaps between file contributions. Parse non-executable-section
metadata for validity but emit mappings only to executable sections and
procedures from the same module. A line applies from its section offset to
the next distinct offset in that table or the contribution end plus one,
clipped to the procedure range. Preserve every duplicate in input order;
the existing equal-span resolver selects the first positive source record.
This is GoodSentry's deterministic policy, not a claim about a Microsoft
debugger's unresolved tie rule. Line0 emits no source fields but remains a
boundary; retain every positive u16 line, including65535. The modern inverse
C13 converter's special-marker convention does not reserve65535 universally
in historical C11.

Share the existing per-PDB limits of1,000,000 source pairs,65,536 source files
and1,000,000 emitted line/procedure ranges across C11 and C13. Additionally
charge all processed C11 extents, including repeated references, against64MiB,
and all root-segment plus file-table references, including zero-pair tables,
against1,000,000 per PDB. Preserve function-only fallback outside source spans.
Historical MBCS ST filenames containing only ASCII are emitted byte-exact,
without path normalization. For non-ASCII historical bytes, validate the
complete structure and budgets but omit that file's source mappings while
retaining its procedures: do not guess a code page or replace bytes with a
misleading path. Code-page-aware decoding remains required for full legacy
closure; this fallback is not an accepted scope exception. Other C11 filename
profiles remain bounds-only until their representation is established. Do not
infer the missing `IS_SZ_FORMAT_PDB` macro from an unrelated version test.

Acceptance extends the public source-line parser owner with owned legacy
fixtures for exact identity/function/path/line, the final included byte and
first excluded byte, duplicate offsets,65535, zero-line boundaries, contribution
gaps and module isolation. Add late malformed references/ranges/names, unknown
code-page fallback and cumulative-budget controls at their strongest owners.
Preserve existing C13/inline tests and use an independently decoded private
C11 sample only as supplemental evidence, without redistribution or executing
its companion binary. Portable PDB, other legacy profiles and broader native
requirements remain open. References: Microsoft's pinned [C11 structures](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/PDB/include/cvlines.h#L10),
[historical ST filename reader](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/PDB/dbi/mli.cpp#L451),
[C11 consumer and inclusive ranges](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/PDB/dbi/mod.cpp#L3961),
[MBCS conversion](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/805655a28bd8198004be2ac27e6e0290121a5e89/PDB/include/szst.h#L222)
and [system ANSI code-page meaning](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/stringapiset/nf-stringapiset-multibytetowidechar).

<a id="close-nativo-020"></a>

For classic MSF 7 PDBs whose DBI ArePrivateSymbolsStripped flag is set, scan the DBI SymRecordStream CodeView record sequence (the GSS) for S_PUB32 records; GSI and PSI are lookup indexes and are not required for a bounded scan. Accept records with cvpsfFunction, preserve the emitted name, and map the one-based segment and offset through the DBI Section Map to an executable PE section and image-relative address; reject selector, absolute-address and group descriptors because their Frame values do not identify PE sections. S_PUB32 has no code size, so emit a best-effort nearest-public-function range ending at the next distinct public function in that mapped executable section or at the descriptor boundary. Exact module procedure and source-line ranges remain more precise when both are present. Bound the stream to the existing 64MiB file limit, 1,000,000 records, 200,000 public function records, 512 Section Map descriptors, and 4,096-byte names. Validate record framing, CodeView `F1`/`F2`/`F3` alignment bytes, section references and range arithmetic; malformed PDBs contribute no partial symbols. Do not read PSI/GSI hash tables or infer public symbols from PDBs without the private-stripped flag. Follow LLVM [CodeView S_PUB32](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/CodeViewSymbols.html#s-pub32-0x110e), the [DBI Section Map and stream indices](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/DbiStream.html), Microsoft [GSS/PSI stream layout](https://github.com/microsoft/pdb-rs/blob/main/docs/pdb/globals.md), [CodeView symbol framing](https://github.com/microsoft/pdb-rs/blob/main/docs/codeview/symbols/symbols.md#symbol-record-framing), and [cvinfo.h](https://github.com/microsoft/microsoft-pdb/blob/master/include/cvinfo.h).

<a id="close-nativo-021"></a>

SymCache debug files are accepted in little-endian `SYMC` versions 7, 8 and 9, using the DebugId stored in the 72-byte header. Parse the version-specific file record sizes, the fixed function/source-location/range records and the bounded string table; v7 strings use little-endian u32 lengths while v8/v9 use unsigned LEB128 lengths. For v9, accept no optional variable section or the current variable-header version1 after validating every aligned table extent; do not interpret variable/type payloads. Cap each file at the existing64MiB processing limit and each record table and the number of strings at1,000,000 entries, use checked extents and reject unsupported versions, malformed strings, invalid references, inline cycles and unsorted or empty ranges without returning partial symbols. The range table maps to the tail of source locations; emit the mapped innermost function, optional source path and positive line number as module-relative symbol ranges, and treat the final no-source-location sentinel as an end boundary. Resolve symbols by the embedded DebugId, never by an inferred binary name, and do not expand inline parents into additional physical frames. This follows the public [SymCache format and lookup contract](https://docs.rs/symbolic-symcache/13.9.0/symbolic_symcache/), [v7/v8 records](https://github.com/getsentry/symbolic/blob/13.9.0/symbolic-symcache/src/raw/v7.rs), [v7/v8 sections](https://github.com/getsentry/symbolic/blob/13.9.0/symbolic-symcache/src/v7/mod.rs), [v9 records](https://github.com/getsentry/symbolic/blob/b4571fb2ba0fb3244edd0ed4e27fb7eff7475d33/symbolic-symcache/src/v9/raw.rs), [v9 section parsing](https://github.com/getsentry/symbolic/blob/b4571fb2ba0fb3244edd0ed4e27fb7eff7475d33/symbolic-symcache/src/v9/mod.rs), and [DebugId byte layout](https://github.com/getsentry/rust-debugid/blob/6185e37d328fe9e3336444118b1e25780458ddb9/src/lib.rs).

For UUID-form SymCache DebugIds with appendix0, preserve the parser's existing
32-hex UUID `U` and index the equivalent Breakpad key `U0` even when upload
metadata omits its Debug ID. The raw type0/appendix0 representation does not
distinguish Mach-O from RSDS/PDB70 age0: both constructors produce the same
DebugId. A successfully parsed Mach-O LC_UUID likewise owns the `U0` alias of
its existing `U` identity. Carry that one optional alias from the format reader
to symbol-table admission and indexing, including unwind tables for Mach-O.
Index each destination once per object, including a name equal to the alias.
Only the embedded identity and its reader-established alias may match declared
metadata. Reject `U1` or any other age and a different UUID; metadata must not
relabel the object. Never infer this equivalence from a generic32-character
string: an ELF GNU build ID of16 bytes retains its exact identity. Preserve
PDB20 timestamp/age behavior and all nonzero UUID appendices. Do not add a
filename fallback for identified objects. The identity owner must exercise
metadata omitted, `U`, `U0`, different age and different UUID through actual
symbolication; the HTTP/PostgreSQL owner additionally proves that an omitted
metadata ID reaches a matching RSDS-age0 event with the exact function/path/line.
References: [UUID and GUID/age constructors](https://github.com/getsentry/rust-debugid/blob/6185e37d328fe9e3336444118b1e25780458ddb9/src/lib.rs#L142),
[Breakpad formatting including zero appendix](https://github.com/getsentry/rust-debugid/blob/6185e37d328fe9e3336444118b1e25780458ddb9/src/lib.rs#L378),
and [SymCache converter header identity](https://github.com/getsentry/symbolic/blob/b4571fb2ba0fb3244edd0ed4e27fb7eff7475d33/symbolic-symcache/src/writer.rs#L729).

<a id="close-nativo-022"></a>

For classic MSF 7 PDB inline call sites, read module S_INLINESITE (0x114d) and S_INLINESITE_END (0x114e) scopes, the module C13 DEBUG_S_INLINEELINES (0xf6) subsection, and the fixed IPI stream's LF_FUNC_ID (0x1601) and LF_MFUNC_ID (0x1602) names. Decode bounded binary annotations for code offsets/lengths, source-line deltas and file switches; resolve file IDs through that module's C13 checksum table and preserve path spelling. For each captured instruction, emit every matching inline scope as a virtual Sentry stack frame with the same instruction address, innermost inline first, immediately before the existing physical procedure frame. Keep the current Minidump frame ordering and physical frame source mapping. Do not demangle names or infer an inline frame when its required IPI/C13 record is missing. Bound inline sites to 100,000 per PDB, nesting to 128, IPI records and decoded annotations to 1,000,000 each, and expanded frames to the existing 64-frame input limit plus at most 128 inline frames per physical frame. Malformed scope nesting, annotation encoding, missing IDs/files, invalid ranges or budget excess reject the entire PDB without partial symbols. This PDB behavior does not change the SymCache contract below, which continues to emit its innermost symbol without expanding inline parents. Follow [LLVM CodeView symbols](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/CodeViewSymbols.html), [IPI/TPI streams](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/TpiStream.html), [CodeView type records](https://llvm.org/docs/PDB/CodeViewTypes.html), [C13 inlinee lines](https://www.llvm.org/doxygen/DebugInlineeLinesSubsection_8h_source.html), [annotation decoding](https://www.llvm.org/doxygen/SymbolRecord_8h_source.html), and [LLVM's inline line lookup](https://www.llvm.org/doxygen/NativeInlineSiteSymbol_8cpp_source.html).

<a id="close-nativo-023"></a>

This native slice remains incomplete: Minidump CPU contexts beyond AMD64, x86, ARM32, ARM64 and RISC-V64; uncommon CFI expressions and platform-specific signal-context register recovery; nested archive discovery beyond eight layers; and symbol-server resolution are not implemented. ZIP, TAR and gzip-compressed TAR dSYM/DIF uploads, Mach-O dSYM companion files whose zero-byte __TEXT segment still defines the executable address base, and their DWARF are consumed directly. It does not claim general crashpad/breakpad coverage. Keep the parent native-processing task open until those formats and real multi-architecture crash receipts pass. The unadjusted signal-frame return-address rule follows the `.cfi_signal_frame` behavior documented in the [Linux arm64 signal trampoline](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/arch/arm64/kernel/vdso/sigreturn.S).

<a id="close-nativo-024"></a>

Classic PDB symbol-scope Parent/End pointers are offsets from the beginning of the module symbol substream, including its four-byte CodeView signature; normalize parsed record offsets to that origin before validating nesting.

<a id="close-nativo-025"></a>

The official Sentry Native 0.17.4 Crashpad backend sends Minidump requests with `sentry_client` and `sentry_key` but may omit `sentry_version`; permit that omission only on `/api/{project}/minidump/`, and require version `7` whenever it is supplied. Crashpad gzip-compresses the full multipart body by default. Decode supported `Content-Encoding` values before multipart parsing, enforcing `MAX_BODY_BYTES` independently for the compressed and expanded bodies; reject unsupported encodings, malformed streams and size overflow before event persistence. Other SDK ingestion keeps its existing version-7 authentication. This follows the upstream [Minidump URL](https://github.com/getsentry/sentry-native/blob/0.17.4/src/sentry_utils.c), [Crashpad upload construction](https://github.com/getsentry/sentry-native/blob/0.17.4/external/crashpad/handler/crash_report_upload_thread.cc) and [gzip behavior](https://github.com/getsentry/sentry-native/blob/0.17.4/external/crashpad/handler/crashpad_handler.md).

<a id="close-nativo-026"></a>

The repository's repeatable native SDK demonstration pins official `sentry-native` 0.17.4 and Crashpad on Linux/amd64. It compiles a named, non-inlined crash function with ELF/DWARF symbols, uploads that exact executable through the project's authenticated `files/dsyms/` route, and triggers one intentional fault using the same binary and project's DSN. The resulting GoodSentry event must resolve the named function to the demo C source and a positive line number by the ELF Debug ID; GoodSentry must not retain the uploaded minidump. This proves one local Sentry SDK workflow and does not close the broader native architecture/format task.

## Telemetry, uptime and alerts

<a id="close-rendimiento-001"></a>

Transactions persist trace/span relationships, operation, endpoint, duration, status, release and environment. Provide counts, error counts, average, p50 and p95 for a selected time window, plus transaction detail and span breakdown. Logs support Sentry log envelopes and native OTLP /v1/logs, preserving typed attributes, severity and trace links. Filter by time, level, service, environment, text and trace. Retention is configurable by data class.

### Uptime status pages

<a id="close-uptime-001"></a>

Additive046 stores organization-owned status pages and a many-to-many selection of same-organization uptime monitors. Slugs are globally unique, stable and generated once from the trimmed display name with a bounded collision suffix. Owners/admins create, update and delete pages; current members can list them. Unknown, foreign or cross-organization monitor IDs never attach. Compatible `GET/POST /api/0/organizations/{org}/status-pages/` uses `org:read`/`org:write` or the equivalent current session role; the POST body is the public `StatusPageIn` name/isPublic shape and starts with no monitors, while native update accepts up to100 bounded monitor IDs.

<a id="close-uptime-002"></a>

`GET /status/{slug}` and `GET /api/v1/public/status-pages/{slug}` expose only public pages. The public view includes the page name, selected monitor names, current status and last-check time. It never exposes monitor URLs, heartbeat keys, project IDs, environments, expected bodies or check errors. A private/nonexistent page returns404. The native Uptime UI lists pages, creates/edits name, publication and selected monitors, links to the public page and requires explicit deletion confirmation.

### Compatible uptime monitors

<a id="close-uptime-003"></a>

When uptime is enabled, `GET/POST /api/0/organizations/{org}/monitors/`, `GET/PUT/DELETE .../monitors/{id}/` and `GET .../checks/` map the public MonitorIn/MonitorSchema onto the same native scheduler and monitor rows. Reads require `org:read` or current membership; mutations require `org:write` plus the native owner/admin rule. Monitor type aliases map Ping/HEAD, GET, POST, TCP Port, SSL and Heartbeat to native kinds; IDs, status, last change, interval, timeout, expected status/body, confirmation threshold, project identity and heartbeat endpoint are returned from live state.

<a id="close-uptime-004"></a>

Lists and checks use strict cursor pagination. Checks expose start time, isUp and optional response time; native string reasons are omitted rather than coerced into the public integer union. Numeric environment IDs are rejected because GoodSentry's uptime environments are stored as names, not the public environment entity. PUT preserves omitted mutable fields and cannot change monitor kind.

<a id="close-uptime-005"></a>

Additive057 gives every monitor a stable UUID endpoint identity and a unique index without rewriting prior monitor or check rows. Compatible monitor JSON exposes `endpointId`; heartbeat monitors expose `heartbeatEndpoint` as the absolute compatible URL `POST /api/0/organizations/{org}/heartbeat_check/{endpoint}/`. That route is public and capability-based, accepts no query parameters, drains at most1024 request bytes, records one up check through the native enabled-heartbeat transaction and returns `{isUp:true, reason:null, startCheck}` in the public check shape. Unknown, foreign or disabled endpoints return404; an oversized body returns413. The native `/api/v1/heartbeat/{key}` capability remains unchanged.

### Compatible project alerts

<a id="close-alertas-001"></a>

GET/POST /api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/alerts/ and PUT/DELETE .../alerts/{id}/ expose the native owner/admin alert rules. The compatible project slug resolves inside the current token grant before the native project ID is used. Reads require project:read; writes require project:write plus native owner/admin authority. Lists use strict cursor pagination.

<a id="close-alertas-002"></a>

MonitorIn-style fields map as follows: timespanMinutes to the rolling window, quantity, environments, uptime to the native uptime/frequency kind, and alertRecipients to native recipients. Email maps to project-team email; webhook/slack/discord/teams/googlechat/feishu/ntfy/zulip map to their native URL providers. Nonempty tagsToAdd or Zulip botEmail/apiKey/channel configuration, and nonempty email URLs, return400 because those modes are not implemented. PUT preserves omitted kind, enabled and cooldown values and cannot change the native kind.

<a id="close-alertas-003"></a>

POST .../alerts/{id}/test/ queues the same native test delivery and returns one recipientType/status:queued/message:null entry per configured recipient. It never claims an external delivery succeeded synchronously.

### Native log retention

<a id="close-logs-001"></a>

This is the first retention increment, covering Sentry and OTLP logs. Error events, transactions/spans, uptime checks, completed files and releases remain separate required retention increments; temporary artifact-chunk expiry is already independent. [Public GlitchTip installation documentation](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/install/#data-retention) lists a master retention default and per-class overrides, including logs. Those configuration names are public facts; the receipt clock, bounded cleanup and shared-batch lifecycle below are GoodSentry decisions. Cold archival remains independently required and is not implemented by deleting PostgreSQL rows.

<a id="close-logs-002"></a>

Resolve the master lifetime from GOODSENTRY_RETENTION_DAYS, then GLITCHTIP_RETENTION_DAYS, then the existing RETENTION_DAYS, default90. Resolve logs from GOODSENTRY_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS, then GLITCHTIP_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS, then that master. Empty values fall through as in current configuration; validate only the winning value as a whole integer in1..3650 days, and reject invalid selected values before opening a port. Keep Config.RetentionDays as the master and add LogRetentionDays. Add GOODSENTRY_RETENTION_INTERVAL_SECONDS, default60 and range1..3600. The configuration prerequisite alone does not activate cleanup or close any retention task. Do not advertise per-class overrides for classes whose cleanup consumer has not shipped.

<a id="close-logs-003"></a>

Measure log age from the existing logs.created_at receipt timestamp, not the application-supplied log timestamp. A day is exactly86400 seconds on PostgreSQL's clock; one cutoff is captured inside each cleanup transaction. Eligibility is strict created_at<cutoff, so the exact boundary is retained. Late or future-dated telemetry receives the same receipt lifetime as other new arrivals. Native list/detail and trace correlation use retained rows and preserve their existing query/authorization contracts. A physically removed log returns404 from selected detail. Lists may show eligible logs until their next maintenance pass; no physical deletion is claimed merely because a filter hides them.

<a id="close-logs-004"></a>

Add forward-only migration022 with an index on logs(created_at,id) and a partial index on the log batch reference if the existing unique ordinal index cannot serve the orphan lookup. Preserve every preexisting value and001..021 receipt; do not rewrite telemetry timestamps or canonical payloads. The Store cleanup provider accepts validated log days and performs at most250 log deletions plus250 orphan-batch deletions in one transaction. Use a dedicated transaction advisory lock to make the log cleanup sequence single-writer across application replicas; an unavailable try-lock is an idle result, not an error. Ordinary SDK/OTLP ingestion remains independent of that maintenance gate. Select eligible log IDs in(created_at,id) order with row locks/SKIP LOCKED, delete only that fixed cohort and commit before reporting removal. A failing statement, cancellation or commit rolls back both log and batch removal.

<a id="close-logs-005"></a>

OTLP canonical batches are authoritative shared bytes. Delete a batch only when no log references it; never cascade a cleanup batch deletion across a surviving sibling. Only IngestOTLP creates its batch and complete record set atomically; no later API appends children to an existing batch. Cleanup must not fetch or decode canonical telemetry. Bounded passes may leave expired sibling bytes until the last referencing row is removed; drain the backlog and then remove the orphan batch. Also reclaim committed orphan batches left by a prior interruption or project-level cleanup, regardless of their receiver timestamp, because no retained log can read them. Each pass remains idempotent and current membership/selected-detail lookup cannot reveal a removed sibling. Existing Sentry content hashes on surviving rows and OTLP at-least-once multiplicity stay unchanged. A retry of an actually expired SDK log may create a fresh receipt, because its old identity no longer exists. The provider signature is Store.CleanupLogs(ctx, days) (removedLogs int64, removedBatches int64, err error); days is the validated log lifetime, and both counts are zero on any failure or an unavailable maintenance lock. Namespace that transaction lock by the current database/schema plus log-retention so independent application schemas do not block one another.

<a id="close-logs-006"></a>

Run the log-retention loop inside the one application process, on startup and after each idle configured interval. Immediately continue bounded successful work while checking cancellation; retry errors after an idle interval. Stop it with the existing shutdown lifecycle. Keep it active when log ingestion or uptime is disabled so configuration cannot strand old data. Emit factual structured counts for committed removals and bounded diagnostics for failures without telemetry, SQL or credentials. There is no separate cron service or destructive cleanup endpoint. Document that expiration removes primary-database bytes and that recovery requires restoring a prior operator backup; automatic undo is not claimed.

<a id="close-logs-007"></a>

Acceptance owners: configuration tests protect master/log precedence, inheritance, exact integer limits and invalid selected values. Real PostgreSQL provider tests protect the strict cutoff, receipt-versus-event time, bounded progress, exact surviving SDK rows/hashes, OTLP sibling bytes, orphan cleanup, real two-pool locking, cancellation/failure rollback and022/reopen preservation. A lifecycle test owns startup/backlog/idle/shutdown and replica behavior through actual stored rows, without testing private call order. An isolated final-image copy receives actual SDK and OTLP traffic in new projects, expires only explicitly owned fixture receipts, proves list/detail/trace absence plus exact byte removal, restores a prior backup into a different database and verifies the original data there. Preserve all previously accepted databases, runtime states and uploads. Run full integration/race/vet, inspect actual source/image assets and matching public CI before closing log retention; the parent per-class retention task stays open until every required class is verified.

### Compatible retained-log API

<a id="close-logs-008"></a>

Additive migration `054_log_host.sql` adds `logs.host` and a `(project_id,timestamp DESC,id DESC)` index. The Sentry log reader projects a typed `host.name` attribute into the column, and the OTLP reader projects the resource `host.name`. Historical rows keep an empty host, so the projection never invents a value that was not received.

<a id="close-logs-009"></a>

GET `/api/0/organizations/{org}/logs/` serves the published GlitchTip log shape from retained rows: `id`, UTC `timestamp`, `level`, `body`, `service`, `environment`, `host`, `traceID`, `spanID`, nullable `severityNumber`, `data` and `projectId`. Retained identities are numeric, so `id` is the decimal row identity rather than a fabricated UUID. Levels use the published spelling: stored OpenTelemetry `warn` is returned as `warning`, and a `warning` filter matches both stored spellings. `severityNumber` inverts the ingest mapping (`trace1`, `debug5`, `info9`, `warning13`, `error17`, `fatal21`) and stays null for an unmapped level. `data` unwraps the typed Sentry attribute envelope into a plain object and leaves unknown shapes untouched.

<a id="close-logs-010"></a>

Admission is session or Bearer `event:read`/`event:write`. Repeated `project` filters must all be inside the membership and token grant, otherwise the request returns404; repeated `level` values are distinct published levels and other keys must not repeat. Unknown keys, an unpublished level, a limit outside1..200, an unpaired or over-366-day window and a malformed cursor return400. Log ingestion disabled returns404 for every compatible log route.

<a id="close-logs-011"></a>

Pages are keyset ordered by `timestamp DESC,id DESC` with a default limit100 and a `timestamp|id` `cursor`. A full page also emits a `Link: rel="next"` URL carrying the next cursor. GET `/logs/{id}/` accepts no query, validates a canonical positive decimal identity and returns404 for an absent or foreign row. GET `/logs/resources/` returns at most200 distinct `name`/`type`/`lastSeen` entries ordered by last use, with an optional `resource_type` of service, environment or host. GET `/logs/stats/` returns hourly UTC `intervals` floored from the window start through the last partially covered hour and one series per level with zero-filled counts; its window defaults to seven days, is capped at90 and accepts repeated level, service and environment filters.

<a id="close-rendimiento-002"></a>

Transaction/span retention is the next required class after accepted log retention. [Public installation documentation](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/install/#data-retention) defines GLITCHTIP_TRANSACTION_RETENTION_DAYS inheriting the master lifetime. GoodSentry will use that class for both legacy transaction events and standalone spans; that grouping is a local decision, not a claim about undocumented upstream span cleanup.

Eligibility is strictly created_at before that cutoff, independently of producer timestamps, trace completeness or last SDK retry. Limit each pass to250 legacy receipts and250 standalone spans. Select legacy candidates by created_at,id without row locks; freeze that bounded set, collect its project IDs and acquire exclusive existing issue-project gates in the signed advisory-key order already owned by lockBulkIssueProjects. Only then recheck the same event IDs, kind and receipt cutoff under FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED. Do not expand the set after waiting. Select eligible standalone spans by created_at,id with FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED. A database/schema-scoped transaction-retention advisory try-lock prevents duplicate maintainers from doing work; a busy replica returns zero progress. Row locks held by the legacy projector or another writer are skipped until a later pass. Zero counts on any error, cancellation or failed commit describe no successful removal.

<a id="close-rendimiento-005"></a>

Under those project gates and pinned event rows, delete envelope_items matching each selected legacy receipt's exact project_id,event_id before deleting its event. Existing attachment and transaction_span_operations foreign keys cascade in that same transaction. Do not acquire receipt advisory locks after project gates: ingestion already acquires receipt then shared project gate, so reversing that order would deadlock. Existing duplicate-with-children ingestion rechecks its initially observed numeric receipt after obtaining the gate and must never attach to a later generation. Standalone envelope items whose external event identity has no selected transaction remain untouched; a later standalone envelope can create such an opaque item under the existing contract. General file retention must handle that independent lifetime; this class does not claim an orphan-envelope sweep. Retrying a physically expired SDK identity may create a new receipt and lifetime, while retries of retained identities remain idempotent.

<a id="close-rendimiento-006"></a>

Delete standalone spans independently, with no trace-wide cascade. A failed later span deletion rolls back earlier transaction/dependent deletions. Transaction aggregates, selected details/span-operation groups and trace forest/metadata/raw reads derive their normal results from the surviving receipts. Existing occurrence-time query windows stay unchanged. A missing selected receipt or raw node returns the existing not-found response; surviving nodes may become forest roots according to existing rules. The existing Trace owner returns200 with an empty forest while an independent error or enabled log remains. It returns404 only when no nodes, errors or enabled logs remain; pending legacy projection retains its existing pending response. Do not fabricate an expired placeholder or remove surviving error/log correlation.

Migration023 adds only receipt-order indexes on events(created_at,id) WHERE kind='transaction' and spans(created_at,id). Preserve all rows and historical migration receipts on upgrade and reopen. Run an in-process transaction-retention lifecycle unconditionally, including when log ingestion or uptime is disabled, using the validated maintenance interval. Drain committed backlog immediately on startup and after progress, sleep after idle/error, retry failures without exposing SQL/telemetry in logs, and cancel SQL/timer before closing the store. ### Native OTLP log reception

<a id="close-logs-012"></a>

Neither OTLP/gRPC nor native OTLP traces/metrics are claimed by this log increment. <a id="close-logs-013"></a>
