The following header, limits, persistence and presentation are explicit GoodSentry decisions. Do not claim that copying a Sentry DSN into an OTLP endpoint provides portable authentication: standard exporters may ignore endpoint userinfo/query. Use `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_ENDPOINT=<configured-domain-and-prefix>/v1/logs`, `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_PROTOCOL=http/protobuf` and `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_HEADERS=x-goodsentry-key=<project-public-key>`. The JSON exporter uses `http/json` with the same endpoint/header; its actual client support must be verified separately.

<a id="close-logs-014"></a>

Authenticate using exactly one nonempty `X-GoodSentry-Key` header, with the existing SDK public key selecting its globally unique, enabled project. Reuse the existing key syntax and parameterized lookup; never create a management credential, derive project scope from telemetry attributes or trust a client-supplied organization. Reject duplicate/comma-combined/malformed keys and any supplied Authorization header with403, including an explicitly empty Authorization field. Reuse the SDK syntax without changing existing SDK validation; additionally reject invalid UTF-8 at this HTTP-to-PostgreSQL text boundary. Cookies, `X-Sentry-Auth`, URL userinfo and query credentials do not authorize this endpoint. Reject a nonempty or malformed URL query with400. An absent/disabled/unknown key returns403 with the same static diagnostic; a failed database lookup returns503. Authorization is checked before reading/decompressing the body. A key disabled after admission does not cancel an already authorized request. Do not retain authentication headers in stored telemetry or application logs. When logs are disabled, the authenticated OTLP endpoint returns404 without writing, including for an empty request; never acknowledge dropped OTLP logs as success.

<a id="close-logs-015"></a>

`OPTIONS /v1/logs` returns204 without database access or ingestion, allows POST/OPTIONS and `Content-Type`, `Content-Encoding`, `X-GoodSentry-Key`, and exposes Retry-After. Allow cross-origin SDK/exporter requests with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin:*` and no credentialed-cookie authorization. Other methods on the exact endpoint return405 with `Allow: POST, OPTIONS`; slash/descendant variants return404 and never redirect a POST into ingestion. The existing deployment-prefix wrapper remains authoritative. Use no-store on endpoint responses. Dispatch this endpoint before ServeMux path cleaning: its decoded path and canonical escaped path must match the configured endpoint exactly. A supplied RawPath must itself equal that canonical escaped path; URL.EscapedPath fallback must not silently admit a noncanonical raw target. Canonically escaped Unicode prefixes work; literal unescaped Unicode request-target bytes are rejected. Cleaned aliases (dot segments, repeated slashes or percent-escaped endpoint characters) and slash/descendant variants return404 without Location; the existing prefix boundary still applies. Include repeated-prefix aliases whose prefix-stripped normalization would otherwise redirect to the full external endpoint (for example /good//good/v1/logs); do not let StripPrefix remove that evidence before routing. Other product routes retain their current normalization. On the exact endpoint, handle OPTIONS and wrong methods first; for POST, reject nonempty/malformed queries, then credentials and key lookup, then disabled logs, then media/encoding/body validation. Parse the media type early only to choose the error representation. Reject repeated or comma-combined Content-Type/Content-Encoding fields rather than accepting only the first. An empty query delimiter alone carries no parameters and is allowed.

<a id="close-logs-016"></a>

Decode the HTTP entity as one ExportLogs request. Binary zero bytes and JSON `{}` are valid empty requests; JSON empty bytes, top-level null, arrays, NDJSON and trailing JSON values are malformed. Protobuf's legal concatenation/merge and last-scalar semantics are retained, rather than pretending a concatenation is detectable independent framing. Trace identifiers have exactly 32 hexadecimal digits, accept either case at this native boundary and normalize to lowercase without rewriting stored payloads. Reuse canonical positive int64 project and page1..1000000 validation; absent/empty project means all organization projects. Trace lookup is independent of originating aggregate/log time windows. The main response uses one repeatable-read snapshot for graph, metadata and related counts; an absent trace returns404, including foreign-only data. A trace with only related errors or enabled logs remains inspectable with an empty waterfall. Errors and node reads authorize the organization and selected project independently. Disabled logs expose neither their count nor a logs-only trace.

<a id="close-rendimiento-030"></a>

Migration008 extends events with normalized legacy root span ID, parent ID, operation, status and nullable start time, and extends the derived child projection with span/parent IDs, status and nullable start/end. Preserve every original payload. Projection version2 writes those fields plus the existing statistics atomically; the in-process worker processes versions0/1 with the existing one-event/SKIP LOCKED/cancellation contract. Replacing an older derived projection and setting version2 occur in the same transaction. Existing version1 statistics remain readable while trace metadata is pending. Trace and legacy-node reads return descriptive503/Retry-After2 if their matching transaction history is below version2, rather than inventing missing ancestors from an incomplete index. Add expression indexes for lowercase event/log trace lookup and a trace-leading standalone index; do not change ingestion deduplication or coalesce records from different transports.

<a id="close-rendimiento-031"></a>

A missing resolved parent uses parentId empty and parentPage0; roots/standalone records use ordinal0. An error/log-only trace has null span timing bounds. Each graph node has a storage identity independent of its SDK span ID: `event-<row>` for a legacy root, `event-<row>-<ordinal>` for a nested legacy record, and `span-<row>` for a standalone row. Ordinals are one-based and positive int32; row IDs are canonical positive int64. Preserve duplicate/missing/malformed legacy identifiers as distinct nodes. Resolve relationships only from explicit parent IDs. For lookup, accept 16 hex digits in either case and canonicalize; keep original strings for display/raw inspection. First consider matching nodes in the child's own project. Exactly one candidate links; multiple candidates are ambiguous. If no same-project candidate exists, search the other selected authorized projects and link only a unique candidate. Missing and malformed parent references stay unlinked. Empty parent strings mean no recorded parent. Self-parent edges and every edge belonging to a directed cycle become unlinked with cycle status; retain the nodes and their non-cycle descendants. Do not infer legacy-container, segment-attribute or time-containment ancestry, deduplicate by span ID, shift clocks or guess which duplicate is the parent.

<a id="close-rendimiento-032"></a>

Build the graph from a streamed narrow index of storage identities, validated identifiers and nullable times, without loading all names or raw JSON. Use iterative traversal/cycle detection to support deep chains. Order each root/sibling set by start time, end time as fallback, nulls last, then numeric project/source/row/ordinal identity; render a depth-first forest in that order. Chronological child times may precede a parent's time and remain unchanged. Paginate the resulting forest50 nodes at a time, including nodes beyond1000 across envelopes. Compute global timing bounds from every known endpoint, including incomplete/reversed intervals; a drawable interval and its millisecond duration require both endpoints and end>=start. Derive duration from normalized microsecond endpoints for this timeline. A zero-duration interval is a point, not unavailable timing. Retain invalid endpoints for inspection. Return parent-page coordinates so a parent on another page is reachable. No depth limit removes nodes; cap visual indentation while displaying the actual depth.

<a id="close-rendimiento-033"></a>

The main trace JSON contains `traceId`, `projectId` filter, `nodeCount`, `projectCount`, `errorCount`, `logCount`, `logsEnabled`, nullable `startTimestamp`/`endTimestamp`/millisecond `duration`, `relationships` counts keyed `none`, `resolved`, `missing`, `ambiguous`, `invalid`, `cycle`, `invalidSpanIdCount`, `untimedCount`, `data`, `page`, `pageSize:50`, `hasMore`. Nodes contain `id`, `source` (`event`/`span`), `recordId`, `ordinal`, `kind` (`transaction`/`segment`/`span`), `projectId`, `projectName`, `name`, `op`, `status`, `environment`, `release`, original `spanId`/`parentSpanId`, `spanIdStatus` (`valid`/`missing`/`invalid`), `parentStatus`, `parentId`, `parentPage`, `depth`, `childCount`, nullable UTC `startTimestamp`/`endTimestamp`/`duration`/millisecond `offset`, and `summaryTruncated`. Metadata strings are previews of at most2000 Unicode characters, a native-v1 presentation contract; their flag discloses truncation and the raw-node endpoint retains full values. It returns `{id,source,recordId,ordinal,projectId,data}` and retrieves only the selected original transaction/span JSON. Never include every original payload in the list. The errors endpoint returns `data`, `total`, `page`, `pageSize:25`, `hasMore`; each row has event row `id`, `eventId`, `issueId`, `projectId`, `projectName`, `title`, `level`, UTC `timestamp`, `spanId`, `summaryTruncated`. Include all nontransaction event kinds in the exact trace/project scope, ordered timestamp then numeric rowID. Raw and error endpoints return404 for an absent/foreign node or unauthorized organization; malformed inputs return400.

Related logs from the trace must include identifiers differing only in letter case. Preserve the existing Logs endpoint's exact trace matching by default. Reject any other mode or canonical mode with an absent/malformed trace with400. Carry this as a typed LogFilter option. The trace's related-logs link selects canonical mode and all stored time. This handles historical uppercase logs without broadening existing filter semantics.

Add View trace actions beside existing trace-to-logs/copy controls in Transactions, occurrence detail and Logs, and on an issue's selected event when it has a valid trace. Keep existing log controls working. Enter the trace with all authorized projects by default and a separate project selector. Use `traceView` under the Transactions view, plus `traceProject`, `tracePage`, `traceNode`, `traceErrorsPage` and `returnView` URL metadata, retaining originating project/time/filter/occurrence/page keys for Back. Refresh, paging, parent navigation, node selection and reload preserve that state; sidebar/organization navigation clears trace state. Show counts, actual UTC bounds, an ancestry-indented paginated waterfall with accessible per-node timing and relationship labels, selected original JSON with exact integer digits, related errors linking to issue detail and related logs when enabled. Related trace data uses all stored time, with that scope explicit. Loading/indexing/empty/error states support retry/back. Use original Tailwind/daisyUI components and compiled local CSS; mobile tables may scroll internally without document overflow. Acceptance owners: real PostgreSQL protects mixed-source/cross-project membership, independent identities, explicit-parent preference and ambiguity, cycles/deep chains, timing/null/zero/offset bounds, forest order/page/parent navigation beyond1000, related error/log counts and disabled-log isolation, raw fidelity and upgrade/reopen/cancellation. HTTP protects route/auth/canonical parameters/pending/404 and capture-to-trace access. Browser protects real entry points, waveform positions/labels, parent-page navigation, safe previews/raw large integers, project scope, preserved origin navigation, error/log links, disabled logs, pagination/indexing and responsive320/768/1440 rendering. The final image must resume version1 historical work and preserve the same authenticated trace/raw responses across restart. The complete product parity audit remains separate.

<a id="close-uptime-013"></a>

Uptime monitors support HEAD, GET, POST with expected status, TCP and heartbeat intervals. Store checks, response times and up/down transitions. Scheduler coordination prevents duplicate checks across application replicas; missed heartbeats become down and a subsequent heartbeat recovers. Configured monitor targets are the approved network boundary; bound timeouts, response reads and redirect behavior.

<a id="close-uptime-014"></a>

The first uptime slice uses native organization-scoped `/api/v1/organizations/{org}/monitors` GET/POST and `/{monitor}` GET/PUT/DELETE, with `/{monitor}/checks` GET. Members read; owners/admins create, replace configuration, pause/resume and delete. An optional project slug must belong to that organization. JSON configuration contains `name`, `type` (`HEAD`, `GET`, `POST`, `TCP`, `heartbeat`), `url`, `project`, `intervalSeconds`, `timeoutSeconds`, `expectedStatus`, `expectedBody`, `confirmationThreshold`, `enabled`. Defaults are enabled, 60 seconds, 20 seconds, status 200 and threshold 1. Validate intervals 1..86400, timeouts 1..60, thresholds 1..100, HTTP status 100..599, name 1..200 Unicode characters, URL at most 2000 and expected body at most 2000. Type cannot change after creation. TCP targets use host:port with bracketed IPv6. Heartbeat targets have an empty URL. HTTP permits absolute HTTP/HTTPS URLs without credentials or fragments; HEAD/POST send no payload. A nonempty expected body on GET/POST means a literal substring within the first 1 MiB, an independent GoodSentry decision because the public contract does not define matching. HEAD/TCP/heartbeat reject expected-body configuration. No custom headers or target authentication are claimed by this slice.

<a id="close-uptime-015"></a>

Use UTC PostgreSQL `timestamptz` instants with microsecond precision for creation, deadlines, claims and recorded checks. JSON emits RFC3339 instants. Intervals are elapsed seconds, not civil-time schedules; no daylight-saving recurrence applies. The database clock owns eligibility, leases, heartbeat receipt and completion. A network result records elapsed milliseconds using Go's monotonic clock. The next network deadline is completion plus interval, so a slow check does not create a catch-up burst. A new or resumed heartbeat deadline is database now plus interval. A heartbeat arriving strictly before or after its deadline records an up check and resets the deadline; expiry is eligible at equality. No additional grace interval is assumed. Missed-heartbeat checks recur at the configured interval until recovery and participate in the same consecutive-failure threshold.

<a id="close-uptime-016"></a>

Persist monitor configuration/current state and append-only check history, including raw up/down result, confirmed status (`unknown`, `up`, `down`), previous status, transition flag, start/completion instants, optional HTTP status, response time and a bounded reason code. Consecutive failures change confirmed status to down only at the threshold; a successful check resets failures and recovers immediately. Initial status is unknown. Configuration changes clear failures, keep the last confirmed status, invalidate any in-flight lease and rearm the next deadline. Pausing preserves history/status and suppresses probes and heartbeat writes. Check history defaults to transitions and supports all checks with 25-row pages. Deletion removes its checks. Durable alert delivery remains a separate acceptance item.

<a id="close-uptime-017"></a>

Claim one due enabled monitor with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, assign a random fencing token and database lease of timeout plus 30 seconds, then release the transaction before network I/O. Two workers per process by default, configurable with `MONITOR_WORKERS` 1..16, share this PostgreSQL queue across replicas. Completion locks the monitor and accepts only its current unexpired token; pause/update/delete or a fresh heartbeat invalidates old work. Expired leases are reclaimable after restart. This prevents concurrent valid check completions, not exactly-once external network requests after a crash. Cancel workers on shutdown before closing PostgreSQL, and do not record cancellation as a service outage. Log scheduler claim/storage failures with stable operation and monitor identifiers, excluding target URLs, heartbeat tokens and credentials.

<a id="close-uptime-018"></a>

An opaque random 128-bit heartbeat capability is exposed as `heartbeatURL` only through authenticated monitor reads. `POST /api/v1/heartbeat/{key}` records a check without a management session and returns the check; GET is not supported. Ignore a body up to 1024 bytes and reject larger bodies with 413 before writing a check. It grants no management access. Serialize heartbeat receipt and completion on the same monitor row so a stale expiry cannot overwrite a fresh heartbeat.

<a id="close-uptime-019"></a>

Block private, loopback, link-local, unspecified and multicast destinations by default. `GOODSENTRY_UPTIME_ALLOW_PRIVATE_IPS=true`, with `GLITCHTIP_UPTIME_ALLOW_PRIVATE_IPS` as its migration alias, explicitly permits internal targets. Resolve and validate every address at connection time, connect to a validated address, apply the same rule to every redirect, disable environment proxies, verify HTTPS certificates, limit redirects to five and bound the whole probe by its timeout. Read at most 1 MiB plus one byte for body matching and bound response headers. Public configuration validation does not perform network requests. Feature flags remove uptime routes/UI and suppress workers when false. Add uptime availability to the authenticated session feature object.

<a id="close-uptime-020"></a>

The Tailwind UI lists monitor status, target, interval and last check, provides accessible owner/admin creation and edit/pause/delete controls, and shows monitor detail with transition/all-check pagination and a copyable heartbeat URL. Member views omit write controls. Preserve useful fields after validation/storage errors. Verify HTTP/TCP down/recovery, heartbeat expiry/recovery, pause, threshold, cross-organization isolation, concurrent claims, stale completions and expired-lease recovery against real PostgreSQL, plus the actual responsive browser workflow. SSL certificate monitors appear in the current public schema and remain a separately tracked parity requirement until their behavior is researched and verified.

<a id="close-uptime-021"></a>

The SSL monitor increment uses the public `SSL` enum, independently defined standard TLS behavior and the existing native monitor workflow. Primary retrieved the official [GlitchTip OpenAPI](https://app.glitchtip.com/api/openapi.json) and [uptime documentation](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/uptime-monitoring/) on 2026-10-19; the OpenAPI SHA256 is 7b60511c4823a0dfe2b82fe1cdb16e5c5d092c250bb5b9ac87a8e842df948360. It publishes the SSL enum and common configuration, but no SSL target grammar, expiry-day setting or SSL reason semantics. Evidence lives in test-results/goal-ssl-contract. The following choices define GoodSentry behavior; the enum alone does not establish external SSL equivalence or an advance-expiry notification contract.

<a id="close-uptime-022"></a>

Accept native `type: SSL` with an absolute `https://host[:port]` origin, optionally ending in `/`. Reject credentials, fragments (including an empty fragment), query delimiters/parameters, non-root paths and encoded paths, empty hosts and invalid numeric ports. Reuse common Unicode/name/interval/timeout/threshold/project rules. Preserve supplied URL spelling after existing outer whitespace trimming. Default the connection port to 443. Continue requiring the shared valid expectedStatus value for configuration round trips, although SSL uses no HTTP status; reject nonempty expectedBody. The type remains immutable after creation.

<a id="close-uptime-023"></a>

Each SSL check creates a new TCP connection through the existing nettarget resolver/pinning policy, then calls the standard Go TLS client with the original hostname as ServerName, system roots, TLS1.2 minimum and no session cache. Keep standard hostname/IP, chain trust and current certificate validity verification enabled, including for explicitly allowed private targets. Trust honors the existing Go system-root environment settings; add no test-only public trust switch. Do not send an HTTP request or follow redirects. The probe covers certificate validity when it runs, using the process clock; scheduling and failure thresholds determine when a failure becomes a recorded outage/alert. No pre-expiry day threshold or certificate revocation service is claimed by this increment.

<a id="close-uptime-024"></a>

Share one configured deadline across resolution, connect and handshake. Close the raw TCP connection after every outcome so a TLS close_notify cannot extend shutdown. Preserve caller cancellation as an error, without recording an outage. Reuse bounded destination_blocked, dns_error, connection_error and timeout reasons; certificate/protocol negotiation failures use tls_error, and successful negotiation uses ok. SSL results have no statusCode and retain the existing total monotonic responseTimeMs covering DNS, TCP and TLS. There is no certificate payload or new timestamp field in monitor history.

<a id="close-uptime-025"></a>

Add SSL to the database kind constraint in additive033 without rewriting existing monitor/check rows. Existing claims, fencing, pause/resume, state thresholds, alerts and completed-history retention own SSL as another network probe. The native API must create/list/read/update/delete SSL configurations with existing organization/project access rules and CSRF, preserving status/history on configuration changes. In the existing Tailwind/daisyUI dialog add an SSL certificate option, label its target HTTPS origin, hide HTTP-specific fields and explain trust/hostname/current-validity checks and the whole-probe timeout. Reuse existing detail, history, status, latency and retry displays. For an SSL tls_error, show the human-readable reason “Certificate or secure connection failed” while keeping its stored reason code unchanged.

<a id="close-uptime-026"></a>

Extend owning configuration tests for accepted origins and distinct rejection reasons. A real local TLS fixture and child process with test-owned SSL_CERT_FILE/SSL_CERT_DIR must verify trusted success, hostname/untrusted/expired/future failures, new-check certificate rotation, no HTTP bytes, private-target policy, bounded handshake timeout and cancellation through NewProber/Check. This tests the actual TLS boundary without modifying internal transports or disabling verification. Store/API owners separately prove033 preservation/reopen, SSL admission/CRUD/lease/status/null-result behavior and unchanged access. The existing scheduler owner must record a real SSL outcome through the normal worker; existing shared state/alert/retention owners need only distinct SSL wiring or migration risks, not copied algorithms. Full integration/race/vet and responsive Chromium list/detail/edit at320/768/1440 pass. A new isolated image/database/restart, external CA target and publication remain required before advertising SSL as available. Preserve every accepted original runtime/database.


<a id="close-alertas-004"></a>

Alert rules use error counts over a rolling window and notify on threshold crossing with cooldown. Uptime transitions trigger down/recovery messages. Persist deliveries, retry failures with bounded backoff and expose delivery status. Email and webhook payloads contain a link to the actual issue/monitor. Do not claim exactly-once external delivery; persisted intents provide at-least-once delivery with stable delivery identifiers.

<a id="close-alertas-005"></a>

The first alert workflow uses owner/admin native project-scoped `/api/v1/organizations/{org}/projects/{project}/alerts` GET/POST, `/{rule}` GET/PUT/DELETE and `/{rule}/test` POST. Members do not receive alert destinations or delivery metadata. Configuration contains `name`, `kind` (`error_frequency` or `uptime`), `enabled`, `quantity`, `windowMinutes`, `cooldownSeconds`, `environments` and `recipients`. Default to enabled error frequency, quantity 1, five minutes, 900-second cooldown, no environment restriction and project-team email. Name is 1..200 Unicode characters, quantity 1..1000000, window 1..10080 minutes, cooldown 0..604800 seconds, environments at most 100 distinct nonempty strings of at most 128 characters, recipients 1..20 distinct entries. Kind is immutable. Recipients have `type` (`team_email`, `email`, `webhook`) and `destination`; team email requires an empty destination, email requires a bare valid address, webhook requires an absolute HTTP/HTTPS URL of at most 2000 characters without credentials or fragment. Resolve team email to distinct current organization members of the project's assigned team, falling back to its organization's default team when unassigned. Snapshot each resolved destination into its delivery.

<a id="close-alertas-006"></a>

Frequency means all non-transaction issue events in the project, including message events, filtered by configured environments. Use PostgreSQL `events.created_at` receipt instants, change its default to `clock_timestamp()` for new inserts and index project/receipt time. Do not reinterpret historical receipt timestamps. In the ingestion transaction, after duplicate suppression/event insertion and before commit, lock matching rules in increasing ID order. A new event triggers only if it lies in `(database now - window, database now]`, the count excluding that event is quantity minus one, and cooldown since the last trigger has elapsed. Count queries may stop at quantity. No repeat fires while the count remains above threshold, and a crossing suppressed by cooldown does not become a delayed alert. Evaluate future events only, without retrospective sends when a rule is created or edited. Preserve the existing advisory-identity/issue lock order before rule locks. Transactions, spans, logs and duplicate retries do not contribute.

<a id="close-alertas-007"></a>

Add an optional `environment` string of at most 128 characters to monitor configuration/API/UI and persist it with an empty default. Uptime rules match the monitor's associated project and environment; unassociated monitors have no project alert recipients. In the same transaction as the check, queue down on a confirmed transition to down and recovery only on down-to-up. Initial up, unchanged state and unconfirmed failures produce no alert. Frequency cooldown does not suppress recovery or distinct uptime transitions. Check and event identity deduplicate trigger/destination pairs.

<a id="close-alertas-008"></a>

Replacing configuration or deleting a rule cancels its pending/in-flight delivery records and invalidates tokens; completed history remains. Retain the last-trigger instant when replacing configuration so a rename cannot bypass cooldown. Tests require an enabled rule and do not consume frequency cooldown. An external send that already started cannot be undone. Persist rule/name, recipient and a compact version-1 payload containing delivery ID, kind, occurrence instant, project/organization, optional issue/event or monitor state, count/window and a resource URL. Store a generated application-relative resource URL and resolve it against the current configured public domain at HTTP/send boundaries, including path prefixes. Bound outbound titles/names to 200 Unicode characters and environment to 128; preserve the original stored event. Include trigger context, never raw events or credentials. A webhook sends JSON by POST with `Content-Type: application/json`, `X-GoodSentry-Delivery-ID` and `X-GoodSentry-Event`; its stable delivery ID also appears in payload and email Message-ID. Generic webhook format is independent GoodSentry behavior. Provider-specific Slack/Discord/Teams/etc. payloads remain explicit parity work, not claimed through generic delivery.

<a id="close-alertas-009"></a>

Delivery states are pending, processing, sent, failed and canceled. Store attempt number, start/completion times, result reason, optional three-digit remote status (100..999) and elapsed milliseconds in a separate attempt history. Unregistered HTTP replies such as 600 remain recordable permanent failures. Claim due delivery rows with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and a random fenced lease of delivery timeout plus 30 seconds; increment attempts durably before I/O. Expired claims recover after restart and abandoned attempts become lease_expired; stop after configured maximum attempts. Bound one cleanup transaction to 128 exhausted claims before yielding to the next poll. Completion accepts only the current unexpired token and atomically stores attempt/delivery result. HTTP 2xx succeeds; 408/425/429/5xx and network timeout/connection failures retry. Other HTTP statuses, invalid destinations, policy and certificate failures are permanent. SMTP 4xx/network failures retry; SMTP 5xx fails permanently. Retry delay is min(15 seconds times 2^(attempt-1), 900 seconds). Shutdown cancellation leaves the lease for recovery rather than manufacturing a failed send. A receiver may see a duplicate after acceptance followed by a crash, with the same delivery identifier.

<a id="close-alertas-010"></a>

Run alert workers in the same process with `ALERT_WORKERS` default 2, range 1..16, `ALERT_DELIVERY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` default 10, range 1..60, and `ALERT_MAX_ATTEMPTS` default 5, range 1..20. Stop them before PostgreSQL closes. Webhook networking uses the same validated/pinned destination guard as uptime, blocks internal destinations by default and allows explicit `GOODSENTRY_WEBHOOK_ALLOW_PRIVATE_IPS=true`. Do not use environment proxies or follow webhook redirects. SMTP is an operator-configured trusted destination. Validate `EMAIL_URL` and `DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL` at startup without exposing credentials. Support consolemail://, smtp:// with STARTTLS when offered, smtp+starttls:// requiring STARTTLS and smtps:// with verified implicit TLS. Defaults are ports 25, 587 and 465 respectively. Credentials require TLS; unauthenticated explicitly configured SMTP relays may use plain transport. No insecure certificate option. Bound connect/TLS/SMTP operations by the delivery timeout and cancel their socket on shutdown. Consolemail writes a complete MIME message to the configured console writer and records successful delivery; it does not send real email. Success is SMTP DATA acceptance, not a guarantee of recipient inbox placement.

<a id="close-alertas-011"></a>

List project delivery history at `/api/v1/organizations/{org}/projects/{project}/alert-deliveries`, optionally filtered by rule, with 25-row pages. `/{delivery}` GET returns delivery plus its attempt history. Test queues a normal durable test delivery and returns 202 with queued count; it does not bypass the worker. The Tailwind UI supplies alert CRUD/test and delivery/attempt views, including loading/error/empty states, retained configuration on validation failures, role restrictions and exact current delivery state. Use a separate embedded JavaScript module for this feature to keep app.js focused. Prove capture-to-delivery and monitor-transition-to-delivery using actual local HTTP/SMTP recipients, real PostgreSQL concurrent retries/cooldown, crash/lease recovery, tenant isolation and a real browser. Durable generic email/webhook completion alone does not complete provider-specific integrations.

<a id="close-alertas-012"></a>

On 2026-10-14 the user explicitly authorized automatic delivery to rule-configured email addresses, project team members and webhooks after reviewing the compact payload and the startup proposal. This resolves the earlier automatic approval rejection for missing outbound authorization. Compose sender/workers at startup; set the internal `AlertDelivery` capability only after successful composition and expose it as `features.alertDelivery`. Stop workers before closing their sender and database. HTTP-only test fixtures without workers retain false. Verify the actual application image using test-owned local receivers; no real external recipients are needed for acceptance. Preserve the previous disabled-runtime evidence as history.

### Slack incoming webhooks

<a id="close-alertas-013"></a>

Provider-specific alerts are independent of SSL certificate monitors and account recovery. Implement Slack first as one child of the still-required Slack/Discord/Teams/Feishu/Google Chat/ntfy/Zulip capability. Advance its locally verifiable message/configuration/retry contracts while retaining real-network, database, UI and final-image gates. No provider becomes available in public documentation merely because its serializer compiles or its pure tests pass.

<a id="close-alertas-014"></a>

Add explicit recipient type `slack` using the same destination admission as `webhook`: an absolute HTTP/HTTPS URL, at most2000 Unicode characters, without credentials or a fragment. Preserve the URL after existing outer trimming, including secret path/query components. Explicit selection determines the format; never infer a provider from a hostname or reinterpret existing generic webhook rules. Custom endpoints permit an operator's Slack-compatible relay. Keep current owner/admin-only destination visibility, session/CSRF permissions and private-address/DNS-pinning/TLS/redirect policy. A webhook URL can contain a secret; never place it in logs, telemetry, public fixtures or stable error reasons. This increment does not widen access to rule/delivery APIs.

<a id="close-alertas-015"></a>

Additive034_slack_alerts.sql expands only `alert_deliveries_recipient_type_check` to allow email/webhook/slack. Existing rules, destinations/hashes, payloads, attempts, state, clocks and001..033 checksums must remain exact. Team-email resolution remains unchanged; explicit Slack recipients pass intact into the existing durable intent and its type/destination deduplication identity. Claim, lease fencing, cancellation and maximum attempts stay shared. The previously reserved, unimplemented account-generation migration becomes035 after034; its account contract and preservation guarantees otherwise stay unchanged.

<a id="close-alertas-016"></a>

Use Slack's [incoming webhook JSON contract](https://docs.slack.dev/messaging/sending-messages-using-incoming-webhooks/) with an accessible top-level `text`, `mrkdwn:false`, `parse:"none"`, `unfurl_links:false`, `unfurl_media:false` and two section blocks. The first section has `plain_text`, `emoji:false` and the human-readable summary. The second has `mrkdwn`, `verbatim:true` and one manually encoded link labeled `View in GoodSentry` to the current public issue/monitor URL, including configured prefix. Do not add interactive buttons or channel/username overrides. The fallback text contains that summary followed by a newline and the full generated public resource URL, with ampersand/angle-bracket entities escaped in both; omit `link_names`. Screen readers receive the resource location too, following Slack's [message accessibility contract](https://docs.slack.dev/reference/methods/chat.postMessage/#accessibility_considerations). Keep all telemetry names and environment in plain text. Only the generated resource URL enters the manually formatted link. Canonicalize its HTTP URL escaping first so admitted spaces and Unicode path prefixes remain usable, then percent-encode any remaining literal angle brackets and pipes and entity-escape ampersands before formatting it. Follow Slack's [parsing and escaping rules](https://docs.slack.dev/messaging/formatting-message-text/).

<a id="close-alertas-017"></a>

The summary is `GoodSentry: <rule name>`, then a Project line. Frequency adds the optional Issue line and `<count> events in <windowMinutes> minutes`. The existing `uptime_down` and `uptime_recovered` kinds add their Monitor line and `Status: <previousStatus> -> <status>`. The existing `test` kind adds `Test notification` without requiring a real issue or monitor. Add an Environment line when nonempty, an Occurred line with UTC RFC3339, and a Delivery line with the stable payload ID. The summary is at most3000 Unicode characters, truncating with a final ellipsis when necessary; its separate resource link remains available. Both section text objects must satisfy Slack's [1..3000 character limit](https://docs.slack.dev/reference/block-kit/composition-objects/text-object/). Reject an invalid/overlong public link or an unsupported/missing frequency/uptime context with permanent `invalid_payload`, rather than constructing a misleading message. These formatting bounds are protocol invariants. Persist no transformed summary over the existing authoritative payload. Generic webhook JSON and MIME email remain unchanged.

<a id="close-alertas-018"></a>

Keep POST, JSON content type and the two existing GoodSentry identity headers for Slack. Slack succeeds only for HTTP200 and a complete response body that trims to `ok`. A different2xx reply is permanent `slack_response`, with no body disclosure. Read a200 response with a4096-byte limit plus one detection byte, under the existing whole-delivery deadline; an oversized or unexpected completed body is permanent `slack_response`, while a read/network/deadline failure uses the existing bounded retry/cancellation classification. The response bound protects the tiny acknowledgment protocol and does not authorize storing or logging body text. Other statuses retain the existing generic HTTP classification. Generic webhooks keep their original2xx/body-independent semantics.

<a id="close-alertas-019"></a>

Slack [rate-limit responses](https://docs.slack.dev/apis/web-api/rate-limits/) include a seconds `Retry-After` on429. Parse an optional surrounding-whitespace, digits-only nonnegative integer whose seconds fit a Go duration; invalid, signed, fractional, repeated or unrepresentable fields supply no hint. Carry the relative duration in the internal outcome only. On retry, the store calculates `next_attempt` from its database completion clock plus the greater of that hint and the existing15-second exponential delay capped at900 seconds. Do not cap a valid provider minimum at the default backoff ceiling. The duration's representable bound is a numeric invariant, not an operator policy. Negative hints and positive hints on non-retry outcomes are rejected. Persist the resulting next-attempt instant in the same fenced completion transaction, so restart/replica claims cannot send early; no new queue column is needed. Missing/zero/invalid hints preserve old scheduling exactly. Other recipient formats do not gain new Retry-After semantics in this child.

Expose Slack incoming webhook as a recipient choice in the existing original Alert dialog, preserving the rule/list/history/pause/test flows and destination snapshots. Explain that the channel's webhook URL is required and contains a secret. Keep safe DOM text and owner/admin permissions; members see neither destination nor controls. Add no new application service, dependency, runtime feature flag or installation step.

<a id="close-alertas-021"></a>

Pure owning checks cover configured Slack acceptance/deduplication/URL rejection, actual serialized frequency/uptime/test messages, safe mention-like input and Unicode bounds, generated prefix-aware links, unchanged generic JSON, valid/invalid retry-header parsing and backoff floors. The production webhook body encoder and retry-delay function must have real transport/store consumers; add no injection seam solely to avoid network tests. Existing real HTTP transport owners cover actual Slack POST bytes/headers,200/ok and malformed/oversized/slow acknowledgment bodies,429/5xx/redirect/TLS/private policy and cancellation. PostgreSQL owns034 preservation/reopen, real frequency/uptime/test intents and durable hinted retry/reclaim; HTTP owns session/CSRF/member secrecy and roundtrip/test workflows. Browser and new disposable-image/restart acceptance, full integration/race/vet, exact source/publication and an authorized actual Slack channel receipt remain required before provider closure. No real Slack destination is configured or authorized by this specification alone. Preserve all accepted databases and record unavailable gates without blocking independent project units.

Omit channel, username and avatar overrides. Any2xx response is delivered.

<a id="close-alertas-026"></a>

Truncate only the summary so the URL remains visible within the section limit. Keep `markdown:false` so telemetry stays plain text. The body URL is required because Teams Workflows accept MessageCards but do not render their buttons; the action remains available to destinations that render it. Keep the card below Teams'28 KB request limit. Omit theme-specific user data and interactive controls. Any2xx response is delivered. ### Compatible discovery surface

<a id="close-identidad-065"></a>

Organization JSON uses the public case-sensitive `require2FA` boolean from the persisted organization setting. Go's case-insensitive decoder is insufficient acceptance; verify the raw wire key and actual official CLI parsing. Public authority: https://docs.sentry.io/api/users/list-your-organizations/.

<a id="close-identidad-066"></a>

Expose the read-only discovery routes used by Sentry-compatible clients: authenticated `GET /api/0/`, `/api/0/users/`, `/api/0/users/{id}/`, `/api/0/organizations/` and `/api/0/projects/`, plus public `GET /api/settings/` and `/api/settings/{organization}/` and authenticated instance-license/support-link reads. Existing session authentication and authoritative Bearer grants remain the only credential sources. Organization discovery requires `org:read`; project discovery accepts `org:read` or `project:read`; root/user/license reads accept any implemented read scope. Every result remains limited to current memberships and token project grants. Unknown or foreign user IDs return404. Sessions never gain a broader organization or project set through a query parameter.

<a id="close-identidad-067"></a>

Organization responses use the public OpenAPI `OrganizationSchema` fields with real id, name, slug, creation instant and role-neutral status/avatar defaults. Project discovery uses `ProjectOrganizationSchema` with real project id/name/slug/platform/creation time and its authorized organization object. User responses use `UserSchema`: real id, email as both email/username, display name, creation instant, `hasPasswordAuth:true`, empty identities/options and no fabricated superuser state. The API root reports version`0`, that actual user and null token metadata; token labels/scopes are not invented from the one-way secret. No fabricated first-event time, feature, billing, social, two-factor or activity data is emitted.

<a id="close-identidad-068"></a>

List routes accept strict `limit` or `per_page` from1..100 (not both with different values), a canonical offset `cursor`, and organization `query`/`owner` filters. Invalid or repeated parameter encoding returns400. Return a prefixed public `Link` header for previous/next pages and retain the canonical cursor. User/root/license objects are emitted at their Sentry-compatible trailing-slash paths. Settings reports only GoodSentry's actual instance name, version/environment, disabled billing/social/registration/organization-creation state and currently enabled product features; the per-organization settings response remains an empty social-app list so unknown slugs cannot be enumerated. The support link points to the project's public issue tracker.

<a id="close-identidad-069"></a>

These routes are read-only and add no schema, feature worker or external integration claim. Real PostgreSQL owns membership/grant isolation, search/owner filtering, ordering and25+1 pagination. Real HTTP owns trailing-slash routes, session/Bearer scope admission, strict query encoding, unknown IDs, response DTOs, public settings and prefixed links. No compatible endpoint is advertised as complete merely because a serializer or empty settings object exists.

### Compatible organization detail and project keys

<a id="close-identidad-070"></a>

Additive039_compat_management.sql supplies the remaining read/write management metadata without rewriting existing values. Existing teams receive `created_at` from their organization's creation instant as the earliest retained fact; new teams default to the database clock. Existing project keys retain their public key, project, enabled state and creation instant while receiving a deterministic API UUID derived from the public key, an empty label, an update instant and nullable rate-limit columns. New keys receive a random UUID. Preserve001..038 checksums and every prior row; reopening039 must not alter old key/team values.

<a id="close-identidad-071"></a>

`GET /api/0/organizations/{org}/` returns the live OrganizationDetailSchema base fields, actual projects and teams, role-derived access and `openMembership:false`. Projects use ProjectTeamSchema and include their actual team `{id,slug}` relation when assigned; unassigned projects return an empty team list. Teams expose real creation instant, current-user membership and member count. Owner/manager/admin access includes implemented member/team/project/event writes; member access remains read plus event write. Bearer results are limited to the token organization. No organization update/delete is claimed by this read slice.

<a id="close-identidad-072"></a>

Project-key routes are `GET`/`POST /api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/keys/` and `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE .../keys/{uuid}/`. Reads require `project:read`; mutations require `project:write` for Bearer or owner/manager/admin membership for sessions. Unknown, malformed and cross-project key IDs return404. Status filters accept active/enabled and inactive/disabled. Lists use strict limit/per_page/cursor admission,25+1 ordering by creation then UUID, and prefixed Link headers. Key DTOs expose UUID id, public key, project integer ID, label/name, `isActive`, creation instant, nullable `{window,count}` rateLimit and a DSN object with the public key, empty secret and public CSP/security URLs. No private ingestion secret is invented.

<a id="close-identidad-073"></a>

Key creation preserves the generated public key only in the DTO; no additional token rotation endpoint is implied. PUT replaces the label and nullable rate limit and accepts optional `isActive` to activate or deactivate the key; omission preserves the current state. DELETE removes exactly that key and may leave a project without an ingestion key, matching the management contract. Labels are at most200 valid Unicode characters without controls; unknown/repeated fields return400. A non-null rateLimit requires `window`1..604800 seconds and `count`1..1000000000. Additive041 stores per-key fixed-window usage. Sentry envelope admission charges event/transaction/feedback/log items once and span items by `item_count`; only a valid integer1..1000 contributes that count, while invalid or oversized values contribute one before normal payload rejection. The1000-item framing bound keeps the accumulated charge bounded and prevents untrusted counts from overflowing quota admission. OTLP admission charges each prepared log record. Both atomically insert or increment the current DB-clock epoch-aligned window; exceeding the configured count returns429 with retry seconds in Retry-After and X-Sentry-Rate-Limits. OTLP uses google.rpc.Status code8 and keeps empty authenticated exports successful. Unlimited keys create no usage row. A request may be denied before validation writes, so rejected attempts can be charged; no exactly-once claim applies. An in-process retention worker removes at most250 windows older than14 days per pass, retaining more than one maximum-size prior window.

<a id="close-identidad-074"></a>

The native Projects view offers a project-scoped SDK-key manager. Every organization member who can see a project can view its key labels, status, rate limit, creation time and copyable DSNs; only owners, managers and admins can create, relabel, rate-limit, activate, deactivate or delete keys. Deactivation is reversible and stops new ingestion through that key; deletion requires a separate confirmation and removes it permanently. Key-list pagination remains available within the dialog. Every operation uses the compatible key routes and current session authorization.

<a id="close-identidad-075"></a>

Real PostgreSQL owns039/041 preservation/reopen, UUID/team-clock backfill, organization relation/access, key CRUD/status/pages/rollback, cross-project isolation, atomic quota admission, window reset and bounded historical cleanup. Real HTTP owns trailing-slash routes, session owner/manager/admin versus member controls, Bearer read/write scopes, strict bodies/queries, DSN shape, unknown IDs, label replacement, rate-limit key input, envelope429 and OTLP protobuf429/header behavior. Chromium owns responsive native Projects key management, DSN copying, create/edit/rate-limit, activation/deactivation, confirmed deletion and forward/backward pagination. Project-key rotation uses overlapping keys: create a second active key, update the application's DSN, verify ingestion, deactivate the previous key, keep it disabled while rollback remains useful, then delete it. Reactivation remains available during rollback. GoodSentry does not add an in-place rotation route; this workflow uses the documented Sentry client-key create, `isActive` update and delete operations ([create](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/create-a-new-client-key/), [update](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/update-a-client-key/), [delete](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/delete-a-client-key/)). GoodSentry's DSN uses the public key and leaves `dsn.secret` empty. Exact-source image publication remains a separate release gate.

### Compatible project update and deletion

<a id="close-identidad-076"></a>

`PUT /api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/` accepts project name and platform plus optional nullable `eventThrottleRate` from0..100. Omission or null preserves the saved rate; zero disables throttling. The project ID, slug and creation instant remain immutable. Session owners/managers/admins or `project:write` Bearer tokens restricted to that project may update it. `DELETE` uses the same authority and permanently cascades the project keys, events, issues, telemetry, releases, alerts, attachments and retained files through existing foreign keys; missing or grant-excluded projects return404.

<a id="close-identidad-077"></a>

Additive061 stores each project's `event_throttle_rate` with a default of0 and a0..100 constraint. Create accepts the same optional nullable percentage, defaulting to0, and project list/detail/update responses expose it. At authenticated Sentry store/envelope, minidump, security/CSP, and OTLP log intake, a uniformly sampled project-level percentage rejects the entire request once with429, `Retry-After: 600`, and `X-Sentry-Rate-Limits: 600:event:project:project_throttled`;100 rejects every request directly and0 never rejects. Sampling occurs once after key/project resolution and before per-key quota admission or durable writes, so one envelope is never sampled once per item. Envelope event and minidump candidates may be normalized to check permanent-discard signatures before applying that one sample; an event-only discarded parent is acknowledged without its own throttle or quota charge. A mixed envelope still applies the request sample to independently billable siblings, and sampled requests write or charge none of them. Malformed candidate events retain the sampled throttle response. The independent per-key fixed-window quota behavior is unchanged. GoodSentry implements this project rule only; organization-level event throttling remains unsupported. The public `eventThrottleRate` schema describes a percentage probability and defaults it to0; GlitchTip documents request sampling and the429 retry response in its [API schema](https://app.glitchtip.com/api/openapi.json) and [backend changelog](https://gitlab.com/glitchtip/glitchtip-backend/-/blob/master/CHANGELOG).

<a id="close-identidad-078"></a>

The native project UI exposes Edit and permanent-delete confirmation to owners/admins, updates the current project list after saving, and states the complete cascade before deletion. Real PostgreSQL owns migration defaults, rate persistence, slug/creation preservation and cascade deletion. Real HTTP owns strict bodies, role/scope boundaries, project-rate settings and sampled429 behavior on each intake family, compatible trailing-slash routes and subsequent404. Sampling tests supply fixed percentile inputs to the pure decision function; production uses an independent uniform draw per request.

<a id="close-identidad-079"></a>

`GET/POST /api/0/organizations/{org}/repos/` exposes the existing organization-scoped repository store. POST accepts `{name,url?,provider?}`, trims name and URL, requires1..200 valid Unicode characters without controls and URL at most2048 bytes, and creates a `manual` active repository. POST requires owner/admin session or `org:write`; duplicate names409, invalid bodies400, missing authority403. Provider metadata is accepted as opaque compatibility input but no automatic GitLab/GitHub synchronization is claimed.

<a id="close-identidad-080"></a>

`POST /api/0/teams/{org}/{team}/projects/` accepts ProjectIn `{name,slug?,platform?,eventThrottleRate?}` and creates one project in the addressed existing team under the caller's current organization. Platform trims to at most80 bytes, defaults to `other`, and rejects controls. `eventThrottleRate` accepts a nullable0..100 percentage and defaults to0. Missing/foreign teams return404, duplicate slugs409 and invalid inputs400. Session owners/admins or `org:write` Bearer tokens are required; storage rechecks current authority and locks the organization, team and actor membership before creating the project, its first enabled key, project-team association and the creator's team membership atomically. DELETE returns200 with the remaining associations and is idempotent when the relation is already absent. Missing/foreign projects or teams return404, malformed team slugs400 and queries are rejected. ## SDK feedback

<a id="close-errores-163"></a>

Feedback parity requires both legacy user reports and modern captureFeedback events. Implement and accept these as separate increments. A working legacy report does not close the parent requirement. Public [legacy submission](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/submit-user-feedback/) and [project listing](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/list-a-projects-user-feedback/) distinguish the formats: the latter excludes modern feedback, which is read through issues with issue.category:feedback. Modern feedback must eventually support standalone submissions, optional associated error, attachments, safe triage and that category filter. Its detailed design follows the legacy increment; do not route it into the legacy report count or claim modern ingestion from opaque-byte storage.

### Legacy report identity and storage

<a id="close-errores-164"></a>

Receive SDK envelope items of type user_report and POST /api/0/projects/{org}/{project}/user-feedback/. Each input contains event_id, name, email and comments. Normalize a canonical32-hex or hyphenated UUID event ID to lowercase32hex. Name/email may be empty; comments must contain nonwhitespace text. Require valid UTF-8 without NUL, at most128 Unicode characters for name,254 for email and10000 for comments. Preserve text exactly and never interpret it as HTML or an address to contact. SDK input may carry additional fields; the management JSON body rejects unknown fields. Keep the existing request/decompression/item limits and SDK key containment. Accept at most one legacy report item per envelope. Its explicit payload event_id must match a supplied envelope event_id and any parent after normalization. A standalone report uses its payload identity when the envelope header omits one. Reject an attachment without a parent before persisting a standalone report. These admission choices are GoodSentry decisions, grounded in actual Browser7.120.4 captureUserFeedback wire; Node11.7.0 captureFeedback produces the distinct modern feedback item. A report does not create an event, increment an error, consume a mute, bind a release, emit error alerts or create an issue activity/comment.

<a id="close-errores-165"></a>

Migration029 adds only user_reports: numeric ID, project FK with cascade, normalized event_id, nullable numeric event receipt FK with cascade, name/email/comments, first receipt created_at and updated_at from clock_timestamp(). Unique(project_id,event_id) permits one current legacy report per SDK error identity. A composite FK from (event_receipt_id,project_id,event_id) to the matching unique event receipt tuple prevents cross-project or different-generation association. Add the required tuple uniqueness to events without rewriting rows, a partial pending created_at,id index and a receipt lookup index. Keep001..028 checksums and all historical rows exact.

<a id="close-errores-166"></a>

Store.SubmitUserReport(ctx,projectID,input) writes under the existing project:event_id receipt advisory lock, then the shared issue-project gate, then the matching event FOR KEY SHARE, then its report row. The provider requires a canonical numeric project ID and rechecks project existence; the HTTP caller owns key/grant admission. Only error/message event receipts may be linked. An existing transaction or modern-feedback identity returns invalid-report400. If the error exists, reject a submission more than30 elapsed minutes after its server created_at. A current report can be overwritten only through five elapsed minutes after its first report created_at; a later attempt returns conflict409, including identical retries. Never extend first receipt time on update. Use actual wall time after acquiring the receipt gate, independently of producer timestamps, transaction start or time spent waiting. All time boundaries are inclusive for acceptance, and a minute is60 seconds. These overwrite semantics follow the public legacy API; applying them to envelope inputs and allowing an absent parent are explicit GoodSentry decisions.

<a id="close-errores-167"></a>

An absent error yields a durable pending report, visible only to an authorized project report reader. It can bind to the first matching error receipt committed within30 minutes of the report's first receipt. Ingest performs that binding in the error transaction, under its existing receipt/project gates, before acknowledgment. Binding updates only the numeric association, preserving all report fields and clocks. A pending report older than30 minutes never binds, even if maintenance has not removed it yet. New error capture deletes that expired pending identity before accepting any same-envelope report. Once bound, parent deletion or error retention cascades the report. A later fresh error with the same SDK ID cannot inherit the deleted report. Merge, historical unmerge and future hash reset use the retained event's current issue_id and immutable origin; no copied issue ownership is stored on a report.

<a id="close-errores-168"></a>

Store.CleanupPendingUserReports(ctx) removes at most250 unbound reports with created_at strictly before one transaction_timestamp()-30min cutoff, oldest first, FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, in one transaction. A database/schema-scoped try-advisory lock prevents duplicate maintainers; busy returns0,nil and any error/cancellation/failed commit returns0 with full rollback. It takes no receipt/project/issue/event locks. Run through the existing in-process retention lifecycle at the common maintenance interval, draining startup/backlog and stopping with the application regardless of ingestion feature switches. Bound reports inherit their parent lifetime; there is no new retention variable or separate service. Exact pending duplicates preserve their first deadline. A new report submitted after physical removal starts a new report receipt and must still satisfy the parent-age check.

### Embed error page

<a id="close-errores-175"></a>

### Modern feedback contract

The Node11.7.0 probe shows one feedback item, its own event_id/type:feedback/level:info and contexts.feedback with message, optional name/contact_email/url/associated_event_id, plus ordinary attachment items from a hint. It also sends an empty message and preserves uppercase/UUID association IDs, so server validation cannot be inferred from SDK acceptance. The probe performs no network request and is not server-acceptance evidence.
