## Integrations and compatibility limits

<a id="close-lectores-001"></a>

The official sentry-wizard flow is supported through `GET /api/0/wizard/`, authenticated `POST /api/0/wizard-set-token/`, and hash-only `GET`/`DELETE /api/0/wizard/{hash}/`. The initial 64-character hash is stateless, signed and valid for ten minutes, so unauthenticated polling creates no database rows. The browser handoff selects an organization managed by the current owner/admin and atomically issues an all-project one-day token with `org:read`, `project:read` and `project:releases`. The raw credential is encrypted in migration 051, exposed only through the short-lived transfer and removed with it. The payload returns current projects, full DSNs and enabled keys. Members cannot issue the credential, bearer callers remain confined to their source organization, and the official sentry-wizard client is the external compatibility owner.

<a id="close-lectores-002"></a>

Management API routes use /api/0 where compatible with published Sentry contracts. Supported routes return stable structured JSON, pagination and meaningful status codes. The independent UI may use /api/v1 for application-specific resources. Publish supported endpoint and SDK matrices. Verify drop-in behavior with an application using an official Sentry SDK and a project DSN. Grafana datasource compatibility has its own verified workflow.

<a id="close-lectores-003"></a>

`ENABLE_OPENAPI=true` exposes `GET /api/openapi.json` as an OpenAPI 3.1 document and `GET /api/docs/` as a human-readable endpoint matrix. The document is built at startup from the method-aware patterns actually registered with the application router, so optional Uptime, MCP, administration and organization-creation routes appear only when their feature is enabled. It describes path parameters, tags, the shared error envelope, the configured public server URL, and the authentication family for each route; it does not publish credentials or claim a private route is available. `ENABLE_ADMIN=true` exposes `GET /api/v1/admin/overview` and an Administration view in the embedded UI. The endpoint rechecks current membership on every request, returns only organizations where the caller is an owner or admin, and reports non-secret instance metadata, feature state, effective retention and per-organization project/member/issue/event/log counts. It is a read-only GoodSentry operations surface, not Django administration. Both flags default false; when disabled their routes return404. Enabling either flag is accepted by startup configuration.

### Instance user administration

<a id="close-identidad-097"></a>

Extend the opt-in `ENABLE_ADMIN` surface with account operations inspired by GlitchTip's optional Django Admin, which is documented for managing users beyond the normal frontend: https://glitchtip.com/documentation/install/. Keep the existing overview and its organization-owner/admin visibility unchanged. Instance user administration is a separate global authority: an organization owner, manager or admin is not an instance administrator by virtue of that role.

<a id="close-identidad-098"></a>

Add `users.is_instance_admin boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` and `users.is_active boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true` in additive migration064. Preserve every existing account as active and do not promote existing users during migration. A successful first-owner setup on an empty database and the first implicit default owner created on an empty database receive instance-admin status. Existing installations grant the first instance administrator with `goodsentry admin grant --email <address>` inside the application container; the command must promote only an existing active account and never create or reactivate one. It is a no-op for an existing instance administrator. Before the first instance administrator exists, allow the legacy zero-admin state: only the CLI grant can leave it, and the user administration API is unavailable to every account. Administrators can grant or revoke instance-admin status in the UI.

<a id="close-identidad-099"></a>

When `ENABLE_ADMIN=true`, expose `GET /api/v1/admin/users` and `PATCH /api/v1/admin/users/{userID}` and add an Account users section to the embedded Administration view. The list is bounded and paginated, accepts a bounded case-insensitive email/name substring filter, and returns only user ID, email, name, active state, instance-admin state, and organization-membership count. It never returns password hashes, auth generations, MFA data, API credentials, OAuth state, or private invitation data. PATCH accepts only optional boolean `isActive` and `isInstanceAdmin` fields and rejects an empty patch, unknown fields, malformed IDs, and unknown users using the shared API error behavior.

<a id="close-identidad-100"></a>

Every list and update rechecks that the current account is active and an instance administrator; organization roles and Bearer token scopes do not grant these endpoints. Each update uses an explicit Read Committed transaction, acquires one shared instance-administration advisory lock, then checks the actor's active/admin state from a fresh statement snapshot before locking the target user FOR UPDATE. Session-authenticated requests use the existing same-origin and CSRF controls. In `GOODSENTRY_AUTH=disabled`, the implicit owner remains the actor and these mutations continue to use same-origin plus CSRF; the configured implicit owner cannot be disabled or demoted while it is the active identity. If that account was disabled while authentication was required, switching back to disabled authentication must fail startup rather than silently use or reactivate the account; recover by starting in required mode and reactivating it through another active instance administrator. These routes remain absent with `ENABLE_ADMIN=false`.

<a id="close-identidad-101"></a>

Once an instance administrator exists, serialized updates and the existing self-service account deletion must retain at least one active instance administrator. Account deletion acquires the same instance-administration advisory lock before locking the account and returns the last-admin conflict if deleting that account would leave none. Disabling an account increments its auth generation, marks it inactive, clears its instance-admin status, deletes its sessions and login challenges, revokes its organization API tokens and OAuth refresh tokens, and deletes its compatible account API tokens. OAuth authorization codes and other credentials tied to the previous auth generation remain unusable; neither credentials nor administrator authority return on reactivation. Every credential issuer must check active state while holding the user's row SHARE lock and keep that lock through commit. Disable uses the same Read Committed, user-row UPDATE-lock protocol as password change; fresh credential-revocation statements run after acquiring the lock, so they see any issuer that committed first, while issuers waiting behind disable observe inactive state and fail. Admin authority is checked only after acquiring the shared admin lock, preventing an actor whose authority was revoked while waiting from continuing. A rejected last-admin change leaves the account unchanged. Updating an ordinary profile is outside this administration endpoint because users already own their profile settings; account deletion remains a separate self-service/organization lifecycle operation.

<a id="close-identidad-102"></a>

The first-owner/admin bootstrap must be serialized with user creation. The first owner is granted instance-admin status only when the locked user table is truly empty; a later owner/setup path never promotes an existing account. `EnsureDefaultOwner` must fail if its configured account exists but is inactive. Migration064 itself never changes account activation or administrator status. Stop all pre-064 app processes before migrating or changing account state: older binaries do not enforce `is_active` and cannot issue credentials safely alongside this feature. After any account is disabled, rolling back to a pre-064 binary is unsupported; recover by restoring a compatible backup or deploying a forward fix.

<a id="close-identidad-103"></a>

Acceptance uses real PostgreSQL migration from063 and reopen, store concurrency checks for last-admin changes, actor revocation while waiting, credential issuance racing disable, current-session HTTP authorization, disabled-account rejection across password/session/API-token/OIDC/MCP credential paths, CSRF and feature-off route checks, and responsive Chromium for filtering, pagination, activation and administrator changes. Verify bootstrap grants only on an empty database, existing rows are not promoted, the CLI grant works in the single application image and rejects inactive/missing accounts, and open-auth mutations preserve the implicit owner. Run the focused plus full PostgreSQL integration/race suite, vet, build, documented Compose upgrade/restart and exact-source `gh` CI/image publication. Keep the feature opt-in and document that `GOODSENTRY_AUTH=required` is required before exposure beyond a trusted local network.

<a id="close-operacion-049"></a>

Optional MCP uses Streamable HTTP with authenticated scoped access to issues, logs, transactions, alerts and monitors. Optional cold archival is a spans-only tier in this increment. It writes complete span rows to Apache Parquet in local storage or S3-compatible object storage, keeps them queryable alongside hot PostgreSQL rows, and expires them at the same total age as `GOODSENTRY_TRANSACTION_RETENTION_DAYS`. It does not extend retention. The cold tier starts at `GOODSENTRY_ARCHIVE_AFTER_DAYS`; its default is the smaller of 30 days and half the configured total transaction/span lifetime, rounded down with a one-day minimum. The feature is disabled by default.

<a id="close-operacion-050"></a>

`GOODSENTRY_ENABLE_ARCHIVE` is the canonical switch. Preserve `GOODSENTRY_ENABLE_DUCKDB` and `GLITCHTIP_ENABLE_DUCKDB` as lower-priority compatibility aliases for the same capability, in that order; the implementation is pure Go and does not bundle or claim embedded DuckDB. Pin `github.com/parquet-go/parquet-go` v0.32.0 for Parquet and use AWS SDK for Go v2 for S3. PostgreSQL remains the query engine. Add an indexed cold-row catalog with one row per retained archived span: object ID, original row/project IDs, trace/span IDs, start/end/receipt times, segment flag, environment and release. It contains searchable metadata, not a replacement for Parquet payloads. Exact trace, row-ID, environment/release and time/project selectors use this catalog to choose objects; any read that needs fields outside the index loads and decodes complete matching Parquet objects.

<a id="close-operacion-051"></a>

Every span read that can include cold data runs on one Repeatable Read, Read Write transaction pinned to the PostgreSQL primary. Before creating a temporary relation, resolve the physical `spans` relation using that connection's configured `search_path`; safely quote its actual schema and name in the view definition rather than assuming `public`. Create `pg_temp.archived_spans` with the full `spans` column shape, then a transaction-local `pg_temp.spans` view over the resolved hot relation `UNION ALL pg_temp.archived_spans`. Existing SQL query owners run against that relation with their current authorization, filters, ordering, pagination and aggregation. Keep raw span JSON and attributes lossless. Candidate objects are selected from catalog entries satisfying each reader's original project, trace/row ID, release/environment and start/end-time predicates. Where roots or children lead to unbounded same-trace joins, first select trace IDs from the combined hot+cold seed relation, then load every retained cold row for those project/trace pairs before the final query. Filter each cold row by its original `created_at` against the effective `GOODSENTRY_TRANSACTION_RETENTION_DAYS` cutoff, even when its mixed-age object must remain for newer rows. Project/organization environment discovery and visibility-existence checks may use the indexed catalog projection in the same transaction because they consume only indexed fields. Decode and insert one bounded object at a time, releasing its bytes and decoded rows before loading the next object; do not retain all selected objects or their decoded rows in application memory. Drop the temporary view and table before returning the connection; rollback removes temporary state on error. With no archive manifests and archival disabled, preserve the existing direct hot-table path. A deployment with archive manifests cannot start in disabled mode or with a backend that cannot read those manifests; startup must fail clearly instead of silently omitting cold spans. Do not change Sentry or OTLP ingestion payloads.

<a id="close-operacion-052"></a>

Route all current span readers through this transaction-owned relation: direct project span lists; native transaction groups and stats v2; trace graph selection, metadata and raw payload; transaction occurrence detail; transaction span groups; scoped transaction groups, detail, trend, N+1 and N+1 pattern reads; compatible transaction groups, identity/detail/trend, span groups and N+1 reads; environment discovery/visibility existence; and release environment filtering. For each owner retain its existing access checks, time-column choice and child/root join behavior. `assignCompatibleTransactionGroupIDs` remains a separate durable write after the read snapshot and must preserve its current ID assignment semantics.

<a id="close-operacion-053"></a>

The additive catalog migration marks pre-index manifests incomplete. When archival is enabled, startup backfills those manifests from Parquet in bounded transactions before the service accepts reads or span ingestion; a missing, corrupt or inaccessible object fails startup closed rather than making historical data appear absent. New archive transactions publish manifest plus complete index rows before deleting hot spans in the same database transaction. Span ingestion and archival acquire the same transaction advisory lock for each `(project_id,trace_id,span_id)` identity in a stable order; ingestion checks only unexpired cold catalog identities while holding that lock, and archival reselects/locks eligible hot rows after acquiring it. This preserves the hot table's duplicate behavior across concurrent archive/ingest while allowing identity reuse after total retention expires.

<a id="close-operacion-054"></a>

Readers lock selected ready manifests `FOR SHARE` before downloading their objects. Expiry first changes eligible manifests to a durable `deleting` state and commits; queries ignore that state. A retryable cleanup pass then deletes each object idempotently and removes its manifest and catalog rows. A failed external delete or database commit leaves a recoverable deleting manifest, never a ready manifest that points to bytes already deleted. Because a concurrent transition can make `FOR SHARE` fail with SQLSTATE `40001` under Repeatable Read, retry the entire span-read transaction from a fresh snapshot up to three times with cancellation-aware backoff; rerun the scope/authorization and candidate selection with it, and do not skip a selected manifest or return partial results. Keep later writes such as compatible group-ID assignment outside that retry unit and execute them once after a successful read. Project deletion continues to detach the manifest so cleanup can remove its object without retaining project data. Remove expired rows from the searchable catalog at the same total receipt-age cutoff; objects with newer rows remain until their newest row expires.

<a id="close-operacion-055"></a>

`GOODSENTRY_ARCHIVE_BACKEND` is `local` by default and may be `s3`. `GOODSENTRY_ARCHIVE_AFTER_DAYS`, when set, must be from 1 through the effective transaction/span retention lifetime. Local objects live under `GOODSENTRY_ARCHIVE_DIR`, defaulting to `${UPLOAD_DIR}/archive`; Compose persists them in its existing application volume. S3 requires a bucket and region, accepts an optional endpoint and prefix for S3-compatible services, and uses the AWS SDK's standard credential chain. Never log credentials or object bodies. Bound each object to 64 MiB, split archive batches by both row count and encoded size, and make object keys deterministic from the immutable archived receipt cohort. A PostgreSQL manifest owns project, time bounds, row count and object key; deleting a project sets its manifest reference to null so the cleanup worker can remove the object without keeping the project data. Upload first; publish the manifest and delete the matching hot rows in one PostgreSQL transaction. A failed upload or database commit leaves all source rows queryable in PostgreSQL; retries use the same object key. Expiry removes hot rows and cold manifests/objects by the original server `created_at` receipt time. Keep a mixed-age Parquet object until its newest row expires and filter expired rows on cold reads. Do not archive transaction events, errors, logs, attachments, releases, or issue data under this spans-only contract.

<a id="close-operacion-056"></a>

`GOODSENTRY_ENABLE_DUCKDB` remains off by default in Compose and retains its documented historical spelling. When enabled, configuration activates this complete spans archive path; it must no longer fail startup as an unavailable feature. Local and S3 backends, invalid configuration, upload/manifest failure, rollback, retries, overlapping maintainers, cancellation, total-retention expiry, all existing direct/compatible/scoped/trace/environment/release span query surfaces, and restart recovery require real storage/PostgreSQL owners. Acceptance uses fresh logical copies only, preserves every accepted database/runtime, exercises a clean local-volume application image through archive, query, restart and expiry, tests S3-compatible requests without external credentials, runs all integration/race/vet/build checks, and verifies matching exact-source CI and published amd64/arm64 image digests with `gh`. Implementation references: [parquet-go v0.32.0](https://github.com/parquet-go/parquet-go/tree/v0.32.0), [AWS SDK for Go v2 S3 utilities](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/sdk-for-go/v2/developer-guide/sdk-utilities-s3.html), and the [AWS SDK for Go v2 S3 request examples](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/sdk-for-go/v2/developer-guide/go_s3_code_examples.html).

<a id="close-operacion-057"></a>

Generic OpenID Connect sign-in and organization-managed OIDC applications are implemented and have synthetic-identity-provider and browser acceptance. Vendor-specific deployments and external identity-provider receipts remain open; global user administration is implemented and accepted, with evidence tracked in tasks/todo.md. Session tracking is explicitly unsupported by GlitchTip; supporting or adding it in GoodSentry must be described separately from the baseline. Session Replay and profiling are Sentry expansion work, not substitutes for unfinished GlitchTip parity.

<a id="close-mcp-001"></a>

GlitchTip's public MCP guide at https://glitchtip.com/documentation/mcp/ defines `/mcp`, Streamable HTTP, API-token authentication and OAuth2 with dynamic client registration. Its 17 named tools are `list_organizations`, `list_projects`, `list_issues`, `get_issue`, `get_latest_event`, `get_event`, `update_issue`, `list_transaction_groups`, `get_transaction_group`, `list_transaction_spans`, `list_span_groups`, `detect_n_plus_one`, `get_transaction_trend`, `list_alerts`, `list_monitors`, `list_logs` and `get_log`. The guide marks four span/trend tools as requiring DuckDB. GoodSentry implements the optional MCP capability with all 17 tools, per-tool credential grants, OAuth lifecycle and official-client acceptance. The public guide remains the source for the tool names and documented behavior; current CI, image and upgrade evidence is recorded in tasks/todo.md. The feature remains disabled by default.



### MCP transport and workspace discovery

<a id="close-mcp-002"></a>

Use the official `github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk` v1.8.0, whose published module requires Go 1.25 and whose verified module sum is `h1:KIvahhYqwtbeniWVPs3TcXEA7b8jEtwfBpOTAI+Urx4=`. GoodSentry remains Go 1.27.2, one application process and one image. Do not implement a parallel JSON-RPC transport or copy Sentry/GlitchTip implementation code. Sources are the SDK's tagged public API, https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http and the GlitchTip MCP guide above.

<a id="close-mcp-003"></a>

MCP is optional and disabled by default. Disabled configurations return 404 for `/mcp` and its OAuth endpoints. When selected, construct the stateless handler and enforce the per-request authorization and tool contracts below. The accepted enabled-image and official-client behavior is recorded in tasks/todo.md.

<a id="close-mcp-004"></a>

Register the exact prefix-aware `/mcp` endpoint when constructing an MCP-selected handler. Use the SDK's stateless Streamable HTTP handler, JSON responses, request cancellation propagation and a body limit equal to the smaller of MAX_BODY_BYTES and the existing 1 MiB native JSON budget. Do not persist sessions, cache an actor between HTTP requests, supply a session ID or enable an independent SSE subscription stream. Support the SDK's current 2026-07-28 transport and its stateless 2025-11-25 legacy negotiation. POST carries protocol traffic; authenticated GET/DELETE/other methods return 405 with Allow: POST. Disabled MCP returns 404 without constructing protocol state.

<a id="close-mcp-005"></a>

Validate Origin on every request before reading its body. Absence supports native clients; a present Origin must be a single value matching the configured public scheme/authority, and native cross-site fetches remain forbidden. Preserve the SDK's localhost protection. Send no-store on protocol/error responses and do not add credentialed or wildcard CORS access. Reject URL query parameters, including bearer credentials in the URL. Expose OAuth discovery only through the routes specified below, and only when MCP is enabled.

<a id="close-mcp-006"></a>

Only one syntactically valid native Bearer credential authorizes an MCP request. Cookies and SDK keys never authorize MCP or rescue a malformed/revoked Bearer. Authenticate the token from PostgreSQL on every HTTP request, preserving database-clock expiry, revocation, current membership and its admitted organization/project grants. Storage failure remains generic 503, invalid credentials 401 with a Bearer challenge, invalid Origin 403. After admission create request-local tool handlers closing over that immutable grant and the originating HTTP context. A tool's missing scope produces an explicit MCP error result and no data query. SDK input-schema errors remain MCP tool errors; unsupported protocol operations retain SDK protocol errors. Never return raw storage diagnostics, credentials, password metadata or project DSNs.

The first two real tools are:

<a id="closure-source-table-2"></a>

| Tool | Inputs | Result | Required scope |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `list_organizations` | Optional query, page and limit | data of current authorized organizations, page, pageSize, hasMore | org:read |
| `list_projects` | Required organization ID or slug; optional query, page and limit | data of permitted project metadata, page, pageSize, hasMore | project:read |

<a id="close-mcp-009"></a>

Schemas reject unknown fields, null/wrong types and noninteger pagination. Page defaults to 1 and is 1..1000000; limit defaults to 25 and is 1..100. Query is at most 200 valid Unicode characters without controls. Organization references are 1..200 characters without controls. Defaults are applied at the SDK boundary. Use one lookahead row for hasMore and omit that row from data; return empty arrays rather than null. Both tools are read-only and closed-world in their annotations.

<a id="close-mcp-010"></a>

Reuse existing compatible organization and management-project providers and their real DTOs. Organization selection must first be limited to the credential's organization, then match its ID/slug, so a foreign numeric slug cannot shadow an authorized ID. Project reads must intersect the token's selected projects and current membership in SQL; never load a broader set and filter raw results afterward. Cross-organization or missing references produce the same generic not-found tool error. The tool scope matrix and OAuth lifecycle are specified in the following MCP sections. Preserve existing credential defaults and enforce the declared grant at each tool boundary.

<a id="close-mcp-011"></a>

Acceptance owns observable protocol behavior through a real HTTP listener and disposable PostgreSQL. Use the official SDK client to discover schemas and invoke both tools, read real metadata, page through results, verify project/organization isolation including numeric shadowing, refuse unknown/wrong inputs, and prove a revoked credential fails on the next call of an already connected client. Raw HTTP covers disabled 404, cookie/DSN separation, duplicate credentials, Origin/method/query/media/body limits, current and legacy protocol traffic and cancellation. Existing native token/discovery/SDK consumers, full integration/race/vet, the default non-root image and published matching source remain gates for the increment. Enabled-image availability and full-feature behavior are accepted; MCP remains disabled by default.

### MCP issue investigation

<a id="close-mcp-012"></a>

Expose the five issue tools listed in the public GlitchTip MCP guide through the same internal transport. These issue tools are part of the optional MCP contract; preserve the scope and organization/project boundaries below. Use the existing compatible issue/event projections and status-change transaction; do not create a second issue model, search grammar or state machine.

<a id="close-mcp-013"></a>

Read tools accept either `event:read` or `event:write`, matching the existing issue API. `get_event` additionally requires `project:read` to expose an event without a parent issue, preserving the existing project-event API boundary. Without that additional scope such receipts are excluded in SQL before identity, ambiguity or payload reads. `update_issue` requires `event:write`. A project-only or organization-only credential does not acquire issue access. Every query retains the request's fixed organization, selected project IDs and current membership; an optional project narrows that grant. No credential scope/default change or schema migration belongs to this increment.

<a id="closure-source-table-3"></a>

| Tool | Inputs | Output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `list_issues` | Required organization ID/slug; optional project ID/slug, query, environment, page and limit | Page of the existing compatible issue summaries |
| `get_issue` | Required issue_id | Existing compatible issue detail |
| `get_latest_event` | Required issue_id | Existing full compatible event projection for the latest retained issue occurrence |
| `get_event` | Required event_id; optional project ID/slug | Full compatible event and nullable compatible issue detail from one authorized snapshot |
| `update_issue` | Required issue_id and status (`resolved`, `unresolved`, `ignored`) | Compatible detail for the issue whose state was committed |

<a id="close-mcp-019"></a>

Schemas reject unknown fields, null and wrong types. Pagination retains workspace defaults and bounds. `list_issues` defaults query to `is:unresolved`, sorts by descending last_seen with the existing stable ID tie break, and uses the existing 24-hour chart definition. An explicitly empty query searches all retained states. Query permits at most 2000 valid Unicode characters without controls and then uses the native search grammar; unsupported syntax remains an explicit tool error. Optional environment is one exact value of 1..200 characters without controls. Optional project and required organization references are 1..200 characters without controls. Selecting an excluded/nonexistent project fails generically rather than expanding the search. Use SQL lookahead for hasMore; do not truncate a broad result before applying credential grants.

<a id="close-mcp-020"></a>

Issue IDs use canonical positive decimal int64 strings. Event IDs accept the existing 32 hexadecimal or correctly hyphenated UUID form and normalize case through the existing event-reference parser. `get_latest_event` follows canonical merged issue ownership and the existing timestamp/receipt ordering. All reads preserve native release, ignore, filtered-metrics and event projection semantics. Return no project credentials or raw storage diagnostics. A missing, cross-organization or excluded issue/event has the same not-found result.

<a id="close-mcp-021"></a>

Event ID is unique per project, not per organization. Add one Store operation, `CompatibleEventLookup`, returning the existing event record plus an optional existing issue record. It opens the existing repeatable-read organization boundary, resolves an optional project inside its grants, and looks up at most two authorized receipt IDs in SQL before loading payloads. An explicit lookup filter controls access to unassociated receipts; callers without project-event read authority cannot include them. The filter defaults to excluding unassociated receipts. No authorized match is not found; multiple matches require the caller to supply a project. Excluded projects must not create an ambiguity or disclose their existence. In the same transaction, load the selected event through the existing event row projection and its current parent through the existing issue reader; omit sibling navigation because this operation does not query siblings. A transaction event without an issue returns issue:null only when the additional project:read scope admits it; event:read/event:write alone continue to read issue events only. This snapshot owns the association even during a concurrent merge, ingestion or deletion; never combine independently authorized reads into a supposedly coherent event/parent pair.

<a id="close-mcp-022"></a>

`update_issue` applies the native individual update with its scoped access and audit/activity behavior. Resolve is immediate and clears a prior release-conditioned resolution; ignore is indefinite and clears a timed/count mute policy; unresolve follows the native policy cleanup. Do not accept assignments, deletion or advanced policies through this tool. Preserve unrelated assignment and grouping. Return the committed canonical issue projection and use a write annotation; the other four tools are read-only and closed-world. Repeating the same immediate status must not duplicate activity or change unrelated counters.

<a id="close-mcp-023"></a>

Acceptance uses the official MCP client against HTTP and disposable PostgreSQL. Discover and invoke all five tools; compare real retained issue/event content, filtered pages and query states, UUID normalization, latest ordering, merged IDs and nullable transaction parents with the additional project:read grant. Prove that event-only credentials cannot read, identify or gain ambiguity from unassociated receipts. Prove that identical event IDs across two permitted projects are ambiguous until narrowed, while an excluded project cannot affect results. Verify read-only and write-only scope semantics, denied writes leave state/activity intact, simple resolve/ignore clear existing conditional policies, repeated writes preserve activity counts, and every read/write respects fixed organization and selected projects. An owning PostgreSQL transaction test must demonstrate coherent event/parent association while native merge is blocked or concurrent. Run existing event/issue/merge/update consumers, complete integration/race/vet and default-image/public gates after implementation. Current full-feature, startup and published-image acceptance is recorded in tasks/todo.md.

Disabling logs must prevent tool discovery and data reads, including for previously issued credentials. Migration 043 extends the existing api_tokens_scopes_check with log:read while preserving its nonempty/known-value validation, all existing rows and every applied migration. No second log model or new ingestion/retention behavior belongs to this increment. Verify an actual version-42 backup upgrade and unchanged credential rows; unknown/empty scopes must still fail in PostgreSQL.

<a id="close-mcp-025"></a>

Both tools require a new, explicit `log:read` scope. Existing org/project/event scopes must not acquire log access; a log-only credential must not acquire issue or workspace access. Native token issuance accepts log:read only in an MCP-and-logs-selected handler, with the existing owner/admin issuance rules, generation checks, defaults and project selection intact. Default configurations reject `log:read` when MCP or logs are disabled. When both are enabled, expose it through the existing native token and MCP consent UI. The accepted optional MCP feature and its default-off behavior are recorded in tasks/todo.md.

| Tool | Inputs | Output |

| `get_log` | Required canonical positive int64 log_id | Existing Store LogDetail, including the canonical selected OTLP record when applicable |

<a id="close-mcp-028"></a>

Reject unknown properties, null and wrong types. Retain workspace pagination defaults/bounds. Organization/project references have 1..200 characters without controls. Query, environment and service accept at most 2000 valid Unicode characters without NUL; level accepts at most 32. Empty optional text filters retain the native unfiltered meaning; query preserves native ILIKE pattern semantics rather than adding a second search grammar. A provided trace_id must be 32 hexadecimal digits, normalized with the existing canonical trace parser. Reuse queryTimeWindow for optional start/end: both explicit RFC3339 instants, normalized to UTC microseconds, start inclusive/end exclusive and at most 366 days. Without a window, search all retained logs, as the native log endpoint does.

<a id="close-mcp-029"></a>

Add `ScopedLogs` and `ScopedLog` using the existing ManagementAccess shape with a required fixed organization and user. Keep current membership, organization and the token's project intersection in the SQL that loads telemetry. Resolve an optional project reference through the existing grant-contained provider before querying logs; missing/excluded/foreign projects and direct log IDs fail generically. Never load a broader log payload and filter it in Go. ScopedLogs returns at most limit+1 rows ordered by timestamp DESC,id DESC; the tool trims only the lookahead. Empty data remains an array.

<a id="close-mcp-030"></a>

Share the existing native list/detail query and projection kernels. Preserve the native Logs wrapper's 25-row pages, literal project-ID filter, search semantics and Sentry/OTLP summary shape. Preserve native Log's organization-slug boundary and introduce the fixed-ID/grant branch only through ScopedLog. The detail query must keep its existing SQL byte-capacity CASE before returning a retained OTLP batch, plus current configured record/node admission and canonical selected-record projection. A deployment limit reduction must still reject an oversized selected detail without returning the batch or an unselected record. Do not expose raw storage diagnostics, credentials, other projects' records or sibling OTLP occurrences. Create the opt-in scope through the native token API on the selected handler, and prove default/logs-disabled handlers reject issuance and omit both tools. Organization-level monitors with no project remain visible to current members because they are not project data. ### MCP performance investigation

<a id="close-mcp-039"></a>

Expose the six public GlitchTip performance tools through the selected MCP handler. Implement their observable behavior from GoodSentry's existing PostgreSQL event and indexed span data. This is a clean-room equivalent of the public capability; it does not claim a DuckDB backend or copy GlitchTip query code. Read tools require either `event:read` or `event:write`, matching the issue-event read boundary. These tools use existing event scopes and the optional MCP feature flag; they require no additional credential scope, schema migration or ingestion contract.

<a id="closure-source-table-6"></a>

| Tool | Inputs | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `list_transaction_groups` | Required organization; optional project, start/end, page and limit | Page of native transaction groups |
| `get_transaction_group` | Required organization and transaction; optional project, operation and start/end | One native transaction group and latest sample reference |
| `list_transaction_spans` | Same group inputs plus page | Native grouped child spans for the latest authorized sample of that group |
| `list_span_groups` | Required organization; optional project, operation, description query, start/end, page and limit | Authorized span groups with count, error count, latency and project IDs |
| `detect_n_plus_one` | Required organization; optional project, start/end, threshold, page and limit | Repeated child operation findings grouped by project, trace, operation and description |
| `get_transaction_trend` | Required organization and transaction; optional project, operation and start/end | Daily UTC count/error/latency buckets |

<a id="close-mcp-046"></a>

Reject unknown/null/wrong inputs. Text references are1..200 valid Unicode characters without controls; transaction/operation/description text follows the existing stored limits and is checked for valid UTF-8 without NUL. Pagination retains the workspace defaults/bounds. Optional start/end follow the existing native RFC3339 microsecond, half-open, maximum366-day contract. Lists/groups/spans default to the native24-hour window; trends default to14 days. Empty transaction/operation values retain native empty meanings. Threshold defaults5 and accepts2..100. A missing, excluded, foreign, unprojected or multiple-matching group uses a generic not-found/ambiguous tool error; do not return partial samples.

<a id="close-mcp-047"></a>

Add grant-contained Store providers over the existing event and span projections: `ScopedTransactions`, `ScopedTransactionGroup`, `ScopedSpanGroups`, `DetectNPlusOne` and `ScopedTransactionTrend`. Apply fixed organization, current membership, `all_projects` and selected project IDs in the SQL that reads events/spans. A requested project first resolves through the existing project grant and then adds exact equality; never load extra traces and trim them in Go. Group output must use one lookahead row where paged. Trend input and output days are UTC; output window is ordered ascending and includes only retained samples. N+1 is repeated non-segment child operations within one authorized project/trace at or above the threshold; findings identify the trace's canonical segment transaction where available and order deterministically by repeated count and total duration. It is an observation, not a claim that every repeated operation is harmful.

<a id="close-mcp-048"></a>

Reuse native event/segment span unions, group sorting and latency definitions. `get_transaction_group` selects exactly one transaction+operation group in the authorized scope; when operation is omitted and multiple operations exist, return an ambiguity error. `list_transaction_spans` obtains the latest sample from that same selected group, passes its authorized project into the existing indexed span-group reader, and returns the existing projection. Group origin remains verifiable through `sample.source`, `sample.id` and `projectId`. No broader native reader may authorize the MCP request by itself.

<a id="close-mcp-049"></a>

Acceptance creates real transaction events with indexed child spans across selected, excluded and foreign projects, multiple operations and multiple UTC days. The official MCP client discovers schemas/annotations, verifies group filtering/paging/sample identity, span breakdown, cross-project span groups, threshold/order for actual N+1 traces, ascending trend buckets and empty results. Prove selected and all-project token intersections, foreign organization isolation, invalid windows/IDs/operations, missing scope, membership removal and absence of extra raw data. Existing transaction/span/trend/metrics consumers plus full integration/race/vet, non-root image, upgrade/recovery, matching public source/CI/image and exact binary comparison remain gates. All 17 tools, OAuth and token/consent UI are accepted for this optional capability; the overall product goal has separate open parity tasks.

### MCP OAuth authorization

<a id="close-mcp-050"></a>

When MCP is selected, provide a same-origin OAuth2 authorization server for MCP clients, based on the public MCP2026-07-28 authorization specification, RFC9728 protected-resource metadata, RFC8414 authorization-server metadata, RFC7591 dynamic client registration, RFC7636 PKCE and RFC8707 resource indicators. Do not copy Sentry/GlitchTip implementation code. This OAuth authorization server implements MCP client authorization; it does not claim OpenID Connect or issue ID tokens. It is available only when MCP is enabled. The OAuth flow, forced-2FA/current-account checks, all 17 tools and enabled-feature UI acceptance have passed, with evidence in tasks/todo.md.

<a id="close-mcp-051"></a>

Public RFC9728/RFC8414 metadata endpoints are prefix-aware and no-store: `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`, `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp` and `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`. They advertise the canonical resource `DOMAIN/PREFIX/mcp`, local issuer `DOMAIN/PREFIX`, authorization/token/registration/revocation endpoints, authorization-code+refresh grants, S256 PKCE, token response method POST and only enabled scopes: `org:read`, `project:read`, `event:read`, `event:write`, and, when logs are enabled, `log:read`. No implicit/password grant, client credentials, wildcard redirect URI, bearer query token or credentials in logs.

<a id="close-mcp-052"></a>

`POST /oauth/register` accepts a bounded JSON object: `client_name`1..100 valid characters, `redirect_uris`1..5 distinct absolute HTTPS URIs (HTTP allowed only for loopback `127.0.0.1`/`[::1]` with no fragment/userinfo), `token_endpoint_auth_method` exactly `none`, and `grant_types`/`response_types` omitted or exactly authorization_code/refresh_token and code. Generate an opaque public `client_id`; reject duplicate/unknown/null fields and excessive bodies. Registration never grants a user or token. Consent occurs separately.

<a id="close-mcp-053"></a>

`GET /oauth/authorize` validates response_type=code, registered client and exact redirect URI, state at most512 characters without controls, optional resource exactly equal to the canonical MCP URI, nonempty requested scopes that are enabled and supported, and PKCE S256 challenge of43..128 base64url characters. Invalid client/redirect fails locally without redirect; other validated request errors redirect to the exact client URI with code-less OAuth error/state and `iss`. An unauthenticated request renders a branded login form; POST `/oauth/login` authenticates against current users, enforces the current auth generation and creates the same secure session cookies, then redirects only to a relative validated authorization URL. An authenticated GET renders organization and project selection plus requested scopes without issuing a code.

<a id="close-mcp-054"></a>

`POST /oauth/authorize` requires the current session, same-origin form, session CSRF, and the same validated request. The user chooses exactly one current organization, either all of its projects or a nonempty subset, and explicitly approves the requested scopes. Denial redirects with `access_denied`. Approval atomically rechecks current membership, generation and project ownership and stores only a hash of a random single-use authorization code valid10 minutes. Response includes code, state, and `iss`. A code is bound to client/redirect URI/user/organization/project grant/scopes/PKCE challenge.

<a id="close-mcp-055"></a>

`POST /oauth/token` accepts form encoding. Authorization-code exchange requires exact client/redirect and a43..128 base64url verifier whose SHA-256 base64url value equals the stored challenge; expired/consumed/mismatched/replayed codes fail generically. It atomically consumes the code and issues a random one-hour native `gs_` Bearer token plus a thirty-day opaque refresh token, both stored as keyed hashes with the same organization/project/scopes. Response is JSON no-store with access_token, token_type Bearer, expires_in3600, refresh_token and scope. Refresh-token exchange requires client_id and, for public clients, rotates exactly once: revoke/link the predecessor and its prior access token, atomically issue a fresh access/refresh pair with the unchanged grant. Reuse, expiry, client mismatch and revoked grants fail generically. `POST /oauth/revoke` accepts form client_id/token and revokes matching access or refresh identity idempotently without revealing whether it existed.

<a id="close-mcp-056"></a>

Add additive044 storage for OAuth clients, hashed authorization codes and hashed refresh tokens. Access tokens reuse native api_tokens and their exact AuthenticateAPIToken path. All migrations preserve prior rows and constraints; existing token scopes/defaults remain unchanged. Store operations transactions own code consumption, grant rechecks, token insertion, refresh rotation and revocation. Expired/consumed rows are bounded to configured retention by an existing lifecycle worker; no unbounded secret history. Never return code/token hashes, client metadata not intended by DCR, raw DB errors, passwords or project DSNs.

<a id="close-mcp-057"></a>

MCP Bearer denial changes from bare `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` to include canonical `resource_metadata` and a minimal enabled scope challenge; malformed/missing/revoked token still401 and origin403. OAuth discovery/registration/token/revocation endpoints return404 when MCP is disabled, and credentials do not authorize them. OAuth authorized tokens work through the official MCP client exactly like native tokens and are rechecked per request. Tokens remain revocable through the native organization API with immediate effect.

<a id="close-mcp-058"></a>

The existing native credential form exposes `log:read` only when MCP and logs are enabled, with accurate explanatory text. The embedded UI shows OAuth client grants in the organization token list as read-only/manageable metadata without exposing refresh secrets, and revocation revokes linked refresh access. MCP feature state is visible in authenticated UI only after activation. No default scope widens.

<a id="close-mcp-059"></a>

Acceptance runs real HTTP/PostgreSQL and the official Go MCP client/OAuth handler. It discovers both metadata documents and canonical resource, registers a public client, rejects unsafe redirects/scopes/PKCE/resource, performs login+consent for a selected owner/member organization and project subset, exchanges code, calls representative tools, verifies no-store/CSP/no secrets, rotates refresh once, rejects code/refresh replay/client/redirect/PKCE misuse, revokes from native UI/API and observes next-call failure. It covers logs-disabled scope discovery, foreign organization/project tampering, current-generation/membership revocation, forced-2FA if configured, prefix routing and disabled-MCP404. Additive044 must pass actual old-image backup upgrade/reopen and retain all previous rows. Full integration/race/vet, non-root image, official Sentry clients, restart, matching public source/CI/image and exact binary comparison remain gates. The optional MCP feature has passed these acceptance gates; keep it disabled by default unless explicitly enabled.

## Commands and source layout

Commands to make executable as increments land:

```sh
go run ./cmd/goodsentry
go build -trimpath -o bin/goodsentry ./cmd/goodsentry
go test ./...
go test -race ./...
go vet ./...
DATABASE_URL=postgres://goodsentry:goodsentry@localhost:5432/goodsentry go test ./... -tags=integration
docker compose up -d
GOODSENTRY_IMAGE=goodsentry:local docker compose up --build -d
docker compose down
```

<a id="close-operacion-058"></a>

The external JavaScript artifact acceptance uses official sentry-cli3.8.0 and Node SDK11.7.0. Prepare two disposable projects on the same target and a mode0600 JSON fixture containing `baseURL`, `cookieHeader`, `csrfToken`, `orgSlug`, `project: {id, slug, dsn}`, `otherProject: {id, slug, dsn}` and a project:releases `token` restricted to the first project; optional `version` selects the release variant. Keep credentials outside the repository. The harness creates original/minified fixtures, performs actual CLI injection and upload, captures six real SDK exceptions and retrieves their stored frames through the authenticated API:

```sh
GOODSENTRY_ARTIFACT_FIXTURE=/private/artifact-fixture.json \
GOODSENTRY_CLI_PATH=/tools/sentry-cli \
GOODSENTRY_SDK_MODULE=/tools/node_modules/@sentry/node \
GOODSENTRY_OUTPUT_DIR=test-results/artifact-cli-sdk \
node tests/compat/artifact_cli_sdk.mjs
```

<a id="close-operacion-059"></a>

The issue-query SDK acceptance consumes a separate mode0600 fixture with the same target/session/organization/two-project fields, without a CLI token. It sends33 real SDK events and writes independent seed/order metadata for browser acceptance:

```sh
GOODSENTRY_ISSUE_FIXTURE=/private/issue-fixture.json \
GOODSENTRY_SDK_MODULE=/tools/node_modules/@sentry/node \
GOODSENTRY_OUTPUT_DIR=test-results/issues-sdk \
node tests/compat/issues_sdk.mjs
```

<a id="close-operacion-060"></a>

The browser fixture additionally supplies disposable owner login credentials, the resulting cohort's `issueIdsByIndex`/`orders` and independent object/pair tag cases with exact expected totals; the primary owns seeding and explicitly classifies supplemental wire fixtures separately from SDK traffic. Run `tests/compat/issues_browser.mjs` with `GOODSENTRY_ISSUES_FIXTURE` and `GOODSENTRY_TEST_OUTPUT` against that isolated target. Its test-only browser dependency stays outside the application image.

<a id="close-operacion-061"></a>

cmd/goodsentry owns composition and lifecycle. internal/config owns environment parsing. internal/protocol owns wire decoding. internal/event owns event normalization. internal/store owns PostgreSQL and migrations. internal/server owns HTTP/auth/application endpoints and embedded UI. internal/jobs owns scheduled checks and deliveries. Tests live beside their production owners; external SDK and browser exercises live under tests/compat.

<a id="close-operacion-062"></a>

Use gofmt, ordinary Go errors with context, parameterized SQL and explicit context propagation. Example style:

```go
func (s *Store) Project(ctx context.Context, id string) (Project, error) {
    var p Project
    err := s.pool.QueryRow(ctx, projectSQL, id).Scan(&p.ID, &p.Name)
    if err != nil {
        return Project{}, fmt.Errorf("read project: %w", err)
    }
    return p, nil
}
```

## Verification and delivery

<a id="close-operacion-063"></a>

Protect protocol parsing with table cases and fuzzing for binary framing, decompression bombs and malformed lengths. Test default/custom grouping and timestamp semantics at their owning boundary. Use actual PostgreSQL for migrations, duplicate retries, concurrency, isolation, restart persistence and job recovery. Do not mock SQL to establish persistence claims.

<a id="close-operacion-064"></a>

Exercise pinned official Go, Python, Node and browser Sentry SDKs against the running server. Verify the event through the authenticated API/UI after capture. Include transactions, logs and attachments where each SDK supports them. Exercise source-map CLI uploads, mapped frames, alert delivery, uptime down/recovery and retention. Use a real browser for onboarding, project setup, DSN, issue triage and responsive UI. Container verification builds the image, starts the documented compose stack, sends SDK traffic, restarts and checks persistence. Record exact checks and outcomes in tasks/todo.md.

<a id="close-operacion-065"></a>

Each new test must identify the consumer contract, a credible regression, the gap in existing coverage and whether it would introduce a test-only production seam. Prefer observable outcomes and real boundary exercises. Run focused checks during implementation, then full tests, race detection, vet, relevant integration and browser checks. Failed checks remain visible; do not weaken them to finish. Commit each coherent verified increment atomically with a why-focused commit message.

<a id="close-operacion-066"></a>

Always preserve clean-room provenance, user scope, existing controls and data. Ask before publishing, deploying to an external environment, destructive migration or changing an approved design materially. Never commit secrets, remove failing proof to obtain green checks, report untested compatibility, or call partial GlitchTip parity complete.

## Current evidence and unresolved proofs

The initial empty workspace now contains a Go implementation and local Git history. WSL Go 1.27.2, PostgreSQL 18 and Docker work. Real Go 0.50.0, Python 2.72.0, Node 11.7.0 and browser 11.7.0 SDK errors, transactions and typed logs have been retrieved through authenticated APIs. The Node SDK exposed a span v2 gap; its unchanged acceptance now passes after the capture-to-query regression was repaired. Chromium verifies actual SDK requests from a separate origin with CORS. The account/project/error browser workflow passes at 320, 768 and 1440 pixels with the compiled Tailwind stylesheet. The compose image starts as a non-root user and acknowledged events survive an application restart. Full GlitchTip parity remains incomplete. The local sandbox launcher fails before process creation; individually approved WSL commands run successfully.

Native issue search passes historical same-event/tag/release selection, coherent snapshot totals/pages, exact selector controls and safe tag navigation through real PostgreSQL/HTTP, official Node SDK, responsive browser and final-image upgrade/restart acceptance. The completed issue workflow also covers assignment/activity, merge/unmerge, bulk changes and deletion; its database, API, browser, CLI/SDK and publication evidence is recorded in tasks/todo.md. Supported management API routes, generated OpenAPI and the optional read-only administration surface have local PostgreSQL/HTTP/browser evidence and [exact-source CI](https://github.com/jerolan/good-sentry/actions/runs/40023245722). Those checks exercise GoodSentry's own API contracts. The final requirement-by-requirement audit remains open.

Modern JavaScript artifact-bundle endpoints now pass actual CLI injection/upload/retry and official Node SDK mapped-exception acceptance, with PostgreSQL staging, scoped atomic assembly and restart recovery. Final image restart, browser regressions and matching remote CI/publication pass with exact evidence in tasks/todo.md. Native debug-file support includes project-scoped immutable uploads, chunked DIF assembly, and bounded Minidump parsing and symbolication for Breakpad, ELF/DWARF, Mach-O and classic PDB artifacts. The native unwinder covers bounded DWARF CFI and ARM EHABI, and symbol archives are inspected without extraction. Exact format support and remaining provider-receipt gaps are recorded in tasks/todo.md; this is not universal native-symbol support. Native authenticated source-map upload and mapped ingestion pass HTTP, PostgreSQL, browser and Compose acceptance. The June 2026 GlitchTip release adds /v1/logs and optional email despite older guides saying otherwise. Its OTLP product authentication and full routing remain undocumented in the sources examined; implement the standard deliberately and verify an actual exporter before claiming support. Opt-in Parquet span archival now supports local and S3-compatible storage and is queryable through the verified span readers; `GOODSENTRY_ENABLE_DUCKDB` and `GLITCHTIP_ENABLE_DUCKDB` remain compatibility aliases for this archive feature. This does not claim broader DuckDB analytics.

## Public provenance

- [Official CLI source-map guide](https://docs.sentry.io/platforms/javascript/sourcemaps/uploading/cli/) defines injection, file DebugIDs and optional release/dist; upload with --release does not itself create a release. [CLI configuration](https://docs.sentry.io/cli/configuration/) defines SENTRY_URL and SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN. GlitchTip's public OpenAPI SHA2567b60511c4823a0dfe2b82fe1cdb16e5c5d092c250bb5b9ac87a8e842df948360 documents chunk/assembly routes, multipart file_gzip and checksum/chunks/projects with nullable version, but does not define response states, scopes, expiry or idempotence. Our own pinned CLI3.8.0 recorder confirms ordered/full checksums, raw/gzip fields, subset/empty missing chunks, repeated assembly and version/dist; quota, synchronous commit, scope, cleanup and matching decisions above are independent GoodSentry contracts. Only the actual application upload-to-SDK proof establishes implementation.
- [GlitchTip documentation](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/), [installation](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/install/), [SDK integration](https://glitchtip.com/sdkdocs/all-sdks/).
- [GlitchTip tag-search syntax](https://glitchtip.com/blog/2020-12-08-glitchtip-1-2/) and [clickable issue tags/order control](https://glitchtip.com/blog/2021-02-19-glitchtip-1-4/) establish the public baseline; the public OpenAPI declares query/environment/project/sort/limit/cursor but does not define same-event historical matching, filtered summary counts or grammar errors. Native grammar bounds, quoted literal stars, snapshot semantics and lifetime summaries above are explicit GoodSentry choices. Compatible API0 listing remains separate work.
- [Organization project list](https://docs.sentry.io/api/organizations/list-an-organizations-projects/), [project detail](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/retrieve-a-project/) and [pagination](https://docs.sentry.io/api/pagination/) define the public read paths, scopes and Link behavior. The credential lifecycle, restricted-project intersection, cursor encoding and native UI above are independent GoodSentry decisions. Actual sentry-cli3.8.0 recording confirms its project list requests `?cursor=` and consumes string project id/slug/name; recorder responses alone do not establish our implementation.
- [Public release contracts](https://docs.sentry.io/api/releases/), [release creation](https://docs.sentry.io/api/releases/create-a-new-release-for-an-organization/), [release deletion](https://docs.sentry.io/api/releases/delete-an-organizations-release/), [CLI releases](https://docs.sentry.io/cli/releases/) and GlitchTip's published OpenAPI define the release/project/commit/deploy surface. Official CLI3.8.0 black-box traffic confirms creation's dateStarted, finalize PUT, local-commit snake_case/patch_set, repository/previous-head reads and concrete deploy finish requirements. Synthetic recorder replies establish client behavior only. Unspecified sharing, deletion, dates, cursor, retry and observation semantics above are independent GoodSentry decisions; repository-backed automatic refs remain pending.
- [Sentry client-key list](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/list-a-projects-client-keys/), [create](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/create-a-new-client-key/), [update](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/update-a-client-key/) and [delete](https://docs.sentry.io/api/projects/delete-a-client-key/) define the public key status, rate-limit and mutation fields. GoodSentry applies the supported label, `isActive`, rate-limit and DSN fields through its own implementation.
- [Public monitor OpenAPI](https://app.glitchtip.com/api/openapi.json), schemas MonitorIn/MonitorSchema/MonitorCheckSchema, [second-resolution intervals](https://glitchtip.com/blog/2023-05-15-glitchtip-3-2/) and [TCP monitors](https://glitchtip.com/blog/2023-07-15-glitchtip-3-3/). The schema describes consecutive-failure confirmation; unspecified scheduling, body matching and concurrency semantics above are independent GoodSentry decisions.
- The same public OpenAPI defines ProjectAlertIn, EmailAlertRecipientIn, WebhookAlertRecipientIn and TestAlertResultSchema. It describes frequency, environments and destinations, but not cooldown, rolling-window boundaries, webhook payloads or delivery/retry guarantees. Those are explicit independent choices above. [Go SMTP](https://pkg.go.dev/net/smtp) and [RFC 8314](https://www.rfc-editor.org/info/rfc8314/) guide mail transport; no upstream server source is used.
- [Error tracking](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/error-tracking/), [performance](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/performance/), [uptime](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/uptime-monitoring/), [logs](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/logs/), [CLI contracts](https://glitchtip.com/documentation/cli/).
- [GlitchTip 6.2 release](https://glitchtip.com/blog/2026-06-22-glitchtip-6-2-released/), [6.1 release](https://glitchtip.com/blog/2026-03-23-glitchtip-6-1-released/), [merge behavior](https://glitchtip.com/blog/2025-05-19-glitchtip-5-0-released/).
- [Sentry authentication specification](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getsentry/sentry-docs/master/develop-docs/sdk/foundations/transport/authentication.mdx), [envelope grammar](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getsentry/sentry-docs/master/develop-docs/sdk/foundations/envelopes/index.mdx), [item types](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getsentry/sentry-docs/master/develop-docs/sdk/foundations/envelopes/envelope-items.mdx).
- [Transport](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getsentry/sentry-docs/master/develop-docs/sdk/foundations/transport/index.mdx), [compression](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getsentry/sentry-docs/master/develop-docs/sdk/foundations/transport/compression.mdx), [rate limiting](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getsentry/sentry-docs/master/develop-docs/sdk/foundations/transport/rate-limiting.mdx), [logs](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/getsentry/sentry-docs/master/develop-docs/sdk/telemetry/logs.mdx).
- [Standalone span v2 wire format](https://develop.sentry.dev/sdk/telemetry/spans/#wire-format), independently checked against the public output and transport of `@sentry/node` 11.7.0. [Official sentry-cli 3.8.0](https://github.com/getsentry/sentry-cli/releases/tag/3.8.0) was exercised against our own recording server to observe artifact-bundle requests; synthetic recorder responses do not establish real Sentry server behavior.
- GlitchTip's public performance guide describes group-level child-operation breakdown, and its OpenAPI publishes `transaction-groups/{id}/spans` with aggregate op/description/count/average/p95/total time. Its logs guide describes trace correlation without specifying incomplete-trace reconstruction. Those remain separate from the occurrence API above. [Legacy transaction/span payloads](https://develop.sentry.dev/sdk/foundations/envelopes/event-payloads/transaction/) and the standalone span specification define identities and timestamps; the ingestion limit of 1000 spans applies per envelope, with multiple envelopes permitted for one trace. [Sentry Trace View](https://docs.sentry.io/concepts/key-terms/tracing/trace-view/) describes a broader waterfall, including incomplete roots. Native GoodSentry occurrence scope/pagination/tie-breaking are explicit independent design choices.
- The standalone span specification's [common attributes](https://develop.sentry.dev/sdk/telemetry/spans/#common-attribute-keys) require the string segment ID on every emitted span; its example uses the same segment reference on root and child. PostgreSQL documents [continuous-percentile interpolation and null inputs](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/functions-aggregate.html) and [SKIP LOCKED for competing queue consumers](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-select.html#SQL-FOR-UPDATE-SHARE). The derived legacy projection reuses Go normalization because tested PostgreSQL casts round fractional timestamps differently; original event JSON remains authoritative.
- [ECMA-426 source-map format](https://tc39.es/ecma426/), including VLQ decoding, mapping indices, unmapped segments and indexed sections.
- [OTLP HTTP1.11 specification](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/otlp/#otlphttp), [logs data model](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/otel/logs/data-model/), [logs protobuf1.11](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-proto/v1.11.0/opentelemetry/proto/logs/v1/logs.proto), [common AnyValue schema](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-proto/v1.11.0/opentelemetry/proto/common/v1/common.proto), [Profiles dictionary zero sentinel](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-proto/v1.11.0/opentelemetry/proto/profiles/v1development/profiles.proto), [export response schema](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-proto/v1.11.0/opentelemetry/proto/collector/logs/v1/logs_service.proto), [ProtoJSON](https://protobuf.dev/programming-guides/json/) and [exporter endpoint/header configuration](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/otel/protocol/exporter/) define the native log wire/value contracts. The original Go codec proof verifies official signal-module1.11.0/protobuf1.36.11 behavior; it does not establish product support. [Python protobuf exporter1.45.1](https://pypi.org/project/opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-proto-http/1.45.1/) and [JSON exporter0.66b1](https://pypi.org/project/opentelemetry-exporter-otlp-json-http/0.66b1/) supply the pinned external clients. [PostgreSQL18 text limitations](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/datatype-character.html) explain bytea authority and NUL-safe search projections. Header routing, at-least-once occurrence semantics, native detail DTO, timestamp/severity/search fallbacks and admission budgets above are independent GoodSentry decisions; GlitchTip's undocumented auth is not asserted as verified behavior.
- [Traditional release file upload API](https://docs.sentry.io/api/releases/upload-a-new-organization-release-file/). Legacy store/session/check-in documentation is in the archived getsentry/develop documentation repository and must not override current contracts.


## Current authorized delivery and compatible attachments (2026-10-18)

The current user request authorizes continuous work and publication directly on `main`, without new pull requests. This supersedes historical per-PR publication gates in the planning log. Existing data, acknowledged compatibility, migrations and clean-room development remain preserved.

<a id="close-errores-207"></a>

Implement public Sentry attachment list/retrieve contracts independently from https://docs.sentry.io/api/events/list-an-events-attachments/ and https://docs.sentry.io/api/events/retrieve-an-event-attachment/. Exact project event routes accept sessions or scoped `project:read`/`project:write` credentials, check API token scopes and revocation at request admission, then enforce current organization membership, the admitted project grants, retained-parent and file selection in one repeatable-read snapshot, and never grant access through DSN keys. Existing native 64-character content identities stay intact. Add stable generated numeric API identities without replacing bytes, receipt clocks or native identifiers. Persist attachment type and SHA-1 on new captures; historical selected checksums use bounded per-row fallback without requiring a database extension. Duplicate capture preserves original metadata and both identities.

<a id="close-errores-208"></a>

Lists cap projected filename to 2048 Unicode characters, content type to 256 and attachment type to 128 before database transfer, disclose any truncation with `metadataTruncated:true`, preserve stored originals, and return public metadata without bytes, with bounded pages, opaque offset cursors and preserved name filters in prefix-aware Link headers. `query` is a case-insensitive filename substring; `is:screenshot` selects `event.attachment` named `screenshot.png`. This screenshot classification is an explicit GoodSentry decision because the public contract does not define its storage rule. `download` presence, including empty/false values, selects an octet-stream download. HEAD returns size/disposition without bytes. Filenames remain metadata and safe basename-only MIME parameters. Invalid/repeated or unsupported parameters return 400; inaccessible parents/files return 404; foreign-project and revoked credentials cannot read metadata or data. Native minidump symbolication remains separate.

<a id="close-operacion-067"></a>

Simplify the README and move all detailed verified behavior to linked documentation. Forward optional .env application settings through Compose while fixing the internal port to 8000. Publish a single non-root image for amd64/arm64 through GitHub Actions only after matching CI passes. Registry publication and anonymous pull require independent remote verification before documentation claims availability. Once verified, Compose defaults to `ghcr.io/jerolan/good-sentry:main`, with `GOODSENTRY_IMAGE` allowing a revision tag or immutable image digest. Retain `build: .` for explicit source builds; the normal quickstart uses `docker compose up -d` to pull the published image. The installation guide explains `--build` for contributors and `pull` before updates.


### Browser CSP reporting

<a id="close-errores-209"></a>

Public browser security reports are part of error tracking. Implement both exact `/api/{project}/security/` and `/api/{project}/csp-report/` routes, including configured domain prefixes. Accept legacy `application/csp-report` JSON objects and Reporting API `application/reports+json` arrays, plus `application/json`. Authenticate the current enabled project key using `sentry_key`; reject conflicting header credentials and wrong project keys. Optional `sentry_version` must be 7. Allow bounded release, environment and client query metadata, with SDK CORS and no credentialed browser reads. Enforce the configured ingestion byte limit and at most 100 reports in one batch.

<a id="close-errores-210"></a>

Validate every report before starting writes. Support `csp-violation` only; reject unsupported report kinds explicitly. Normalize legacy kebab-case and modern camel-case fields to a `csp` event, preserve original report extensions, retain numeric line/column/status fields and report age, accept the standard modern nullable referrer/blockedURL/sourceFile/sample/lineNumber/columnNumber values while preserving their original nulls, and group equivalent violations by directive and blocked URL without query/fragment. Reporting API age is a JSON number of elapsed milliseconds, not a browser-local date-time. Accept finite nonnegative numeric age, including fractional/exponent forms, within Go duration range. Preserve whole milliseconds exactly; multiply the remaining fraction by one million using ordinary binary64 arithmetic, then truncate that fractional nanosecond result. Subtract the resulting duration from the server receipt instant and emit UTC; retain the original JSON number unchanged for inspection. Reject duration overflow and pre-year-1 results. These precision/bound choices are explicit GoodSentry decisions; the Reporting API serialization algorithm does not impose an integer-only wire grammar. This grouping is a GoodSentry design decision. Every browser delivery receives an independent event identity because browser reports supply no retry identity. Database errors may leave previously committed valid batch occurrences; there is no claimed all-or-nothing batch transaction. Existing normal error capture, issue reads and compatible CSP entry serialization own persistence and inspection.

<a id="close-errores-211"></a>

Acceptance requires pure format/grouping/invalid-batch cases, real prefixed HTTP+PostgreSQL capture and reads, current-key failures, media/body bounds, no writes for malformed input, CORS and the existing SDK/feedback/alert regressions. Public contracts are W3C CSP3 reporting, Reporting API report-upload HTTP requests and Sentry client-key security-report URLs.


<a id="closure-contract-v1"></a>

## Contrato de cierre aprobado, versión 1

El contrato aprobado el 25 de octubre de 2026 se integra por instrucción expresa
del usuario en esta sesión. La procedencia y aprobación permanecen en
[WF-0007](tasks/wayfinder/0007-goal-reanudable.md#resolution-comments-final-resolution).
Esta sección es la autoridad del cierre; el plan ordena el trabajo y las tareas
registran implementación, evidencia y siguiente acción. La integración documental
no declara el producto terminado, no ejecuta aceptación y no inicia ni modifica
ningún Goal. Su ejecución futura requiere la instrucción expresa acordada.

### Autoridades, referencia y reconciliación

#### Decisión del 27 de octubre de 2026: bases históricas indisponibles

Por instrucción expresa del usuario se retira, exclusivamente para
CLOSE-errores-033, CLOSE-errores-047, CLOSE-errores-084 y CLOSE-logs-028,
la obligación de recuperar o preservar las bases históricas identificadas
respectivamente por 312, 270, 246 y 524 eventos, sus filas y recibos asociados,
y los runtimes o uploads históricos cuya comparación dependía de ellas.
Estas frases en los bloques originales quedan sustituidas por esta decisión.
No se afirma que esos datos se hayan preservado ni que las pruebas hayan pasado.

Para cada criterio se usará un entorno aislado y una base de prueba trazable,
con su estado inicial y datos de aceptación documentados; comprobar los cambios,
la persistencia y el reinicio exigidos contra esa base. Los controles de
migración siguen siendo exigibles mediante un estado anterior apropiado creado
para la prueba, sin atribuirle identidad histórica. Proteger los datos y
runtimes existentes y conservar las demás pruebas, versiones, condiciones de
imagen, publicación y CI de cada bloque. Los comprobantes antiguos siguen
siendo históricos; la decisión no acredita por sí sola ningún criterio.

#### Decisión del 27 de octubre de 2026: runtimes de retención ausentes

El usuario autorizó aceptar copias verificadas desde los respaldos conservados
y retirar únicamente la obligación de conservar los runtimes históricos de
retención que ya no están presentes. Para los checkpoints de retención de
errores se restauraron y compararon exactamente los respaldos de 4.258, 4.290
y 4.305 filas, con sus snapshots históricos. Estas copias sustituyen la
exigencia de mantener los procesos, imágenes/configuración/montajes y estados
de los originales ausentes al ejecutar sus criterios de retención y protección.
No se afirma que esos runtimes sigan preservados. Cada copia usada se identifica
y contrasta con su respaldo; no se infiere identidad de datos de otro checkpoint.

Se conservan los respaldos y comprobantes históricos, las filas y bytes
recuperables que exige cada criterio, y todas las pruebas funcionales,
migración, expiración limitada a fixtures propios, permisos, navegador,
reinicio, recuperación, imagen y CI. Esta decisión no acredita por sí sola
ningún resultado ni autoriza a alterar un runtime existente. No se atribuye
preservación a bytes de volúmenes que no estén respaldados y comprobados.

El cierre abarca todos los requisitos aprobados de esta especificación y los
resultados de [WF-0004](tasks/wayfinder/0004-contrato-compatibilidad.md), con las
resoluciones finales de [WF-0005](tasks/wayfinder/0005-sacrificios.md) y
[WF-0006](tasks/wayfinder/0006-evidencia-externa.md). Prevalecen sobre estados
históricos de discusión. Una discrepancia sin resolución expresa requiere una
decisión; nunca permite reducir silenciosamente el alcance.

La referencia queda congelada en GlitchTip v6.2.6, tag
`d3039088e913a591a8e6544a2d2fffc865a9711a`. La compatibilidad se acredita por
resultados observables con los clientes fijados, sin prometer universalidad.
Las novedades futuras no amplían este cierre. Conservar clean-room, datos,
controles y capacidades existentes, además de sus verificaciones particulares.

El límite histórico del incremento de archivo de spans no excluye el archivo
de errores, logs y transacciones del cierre. Sus criterios diferenciados abajo
son exigibles en almacenamiento local y compatible S3. El cierre no impone DuckDB.
La exigencia histórica de recibos reales por cada arquitectura nativa queda
sustituida por el crash Linux/amd64 representativo aprobado, conservando las
regresiones de formatos/arquitecturas y los demás contratos nativos.
Los pendientes históricos de repositorios automáticos, WebAuthn, muestreo global,
fuentes Java y recibos externos se reconcilian únicamente mediante las excepciones
expresas de esta sección. No se eliminan funciones ni controles existentes.

### Clientes fijados

| Cliente | Versión de aceptación |
|---|---|
| sentry-go | 0.50.0 |
| sentry-sdk Python | 2.72.0 |
| Sentry Node y Browser | 11.7.0, cada uno |
| sentry-cli | 3.8.0 |
| sentry-native con Crashpad | 0.17.4, crash real Linux/amd64 |
| Exportador Python OTLP protobuf/HTTP | 1.45.1 |
| Exportador Python OTLP JSON/HTTP | 0.66b1 |
| Cliente oficial MCP Go modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk | v1.8.0 |
| Grafana / Sentry datasource | 13.0.2 / 2.2.8 |

Las pruebas no aplicables a un SDK se justifican por su contrato; no se omiten
silenciosamente. No fijar SDK/JDK Java, GitLab ni accesos para pruebas aplazadas.
La evidencia histórica de cualquier revisión requiere contraste antes de reutilizarla.

### Inventario estable y granularidad

Cada ancla `close-<familia>-<número>` anterior identifica como criterio
`CLOSE-<familia>-<número>` el bloque normativo completo que la sigue. Cada resultado,
límite, error, permiso y condición de aceptación del bloque es obligatorio.
Un comprobante parcial no vuelve vigente todo el criterio: debe enumerar los
subresultados comprobados y los aún pendientes. Los bloques de implementación
con varios controles no se reducen a comprobar su ruta feliz. Las filas de tablas
normativas y los flujos transversales tienen IDs individuales en la matriz siguiente.
El registro exhaustivo de IDs, enlaces y estados está en
[tasks/todo.md#closure-evidence-v1](tasks/todo.md#closure-evidence-v1).

El inventario se realizó sobre `c5964e5d511972f879ee7ca3f9481dbdd1b94bd4`.
Se conserva íntegro el texto previo. Encabezados, mapa de propiedad, comentarios
de autorización/estado, procedencia y comprobantes históricos son contexto;
los comandos y ejemplos pertenecen al contrato y checks que los introducen.
Las reglas de arquitectura, clean-room y verificación también tienen criterios
propios y se aplican a todas las unidades que dependan de ellas. La evidencia
histórica conservada no acredita por sí misma ninguno de los estados nuevos.

### Excepciones expresas

Solo se aplazan los resultados de esta tabla. Sus límites conservan autoridad
sobre pendientes históricos; sus funciones y controles existentes siguen exigibles.
No anuncian compatibilidad o proveedores verificados.

| ID inmutable | Resultado aplazado y límite aprobado |
|---|---|
